# 8ball — DOCTRINE.md

> One page. Read every release. Edit only deliberately.

This document is the constitution of `8ball`. The codebase obeys it. PRs that contradict it require an explicit doctrine amendment in the same change.

## §1. What this is

A fixed designed deck that knows you. Enter your name and date of birth once. Shake.

**Current free/paid composition (authoritative) — read this before the lineage prose below.** The free surface is **five** coordinates from the date of birth (tarot birth card · sun · public animal (year) · life path · catalog numeral), rendered on a constant compartment **specimen sheet** where higher-tier coordinates appear as sealed cells (§1.D v0.37); **three** paid rungs (t1/t2/t3; t3 completes the single sheet and carries the §1.J comparative — a second person's complete sheet plus the relation layer — per §1.D v0.68; both `t4` and `t5` are retired into t3, see §1.D v0.60/v0.68). The current per-surface authority is the §1.x clause family — §1.A rising · §1.B numerology · §1.C birth card · §1.D tier ladder · §1.E provenance · §1.F atlas+density · §1.G coordinate meanings · §1.H t3 written-entry rotation · §1.I registry + concordance · §1.J dyad relation layer · §1.K moon sign · §1.L the four system lines · §1.M the numerology hexagon. (§1.D's v0.63 kua block is retired by its v0.67 amendment.) The two paragraphs immediately below describe the original v0.2.3 surface and are retained as lineage per L17; where they disagree with §1.A–§1.M, the clauses win.

The product calculates ten baseline calibrated coordinates from (name, DOB): sun sign, Chinese five-element, public animal (year-pillar), private animal (month-pillar), life path, expression/name number, personality, maturity, soul urge, and birthday. The two animals pair on a single line via an equilibrium arrow (`⇌`); three of the numerology coordinates (life path, expression/name number, soul urge) render as a text triplet on line 4 of the result card. The (sun sign, public animal) pair drives a 144-card catalog index — computed positionally in `core/engine.js` (sun-row × 12 + animal-col + 1, rendered as roman numeral). Life path drives bracket resolution (low/mid/high) within a card cell via `resolveBracket`, not the catalog index. The catalog index is the only card-derived field surfaced.

As of v0.2.3 the result card surfaces seven of the ten baseline coordinates (chinese five-element, sun, public animal, private animal, life path, expression/name number, soul urge), optional rising sign, and the catalog index. The remaining three baseline coordinates (personality, birthday, maturity) are computed on buildProfile but reserved for v0.3.0+ paid surfacing — see §1.B. The locked render path ships symbols only — no interpretation, no per-symbol explanation — and is the canonical free surface. The unlocked render path (gated by paid credits per §1 v0.22 amendment / §4.B / §5.C) surfaces the card-content layer (name, type, habit, note per cell) from `content/cards.v1.full.js`; private authoring source is preserved at `~/dev/8ball-private/cards.v1.full.js`.

The voice is declarative-observational, framed in strengths and weaknesses. Cards openly reference the symbol systems they draw from — sun sign, Chinese five-element, Chinese zodiac animals (year and month pillars), numerology life path, name number, soul urge — and name them as such.

**v0.22 amendment (v0.3.0).** As of v0.3.0, the public repo ships the full card-content layer at `content/cards.v1.full.js` (144 entries, name · type · habit · note × low/mid/high). Visibility is JS-gated behind the `eight_ball_credits_v1` localStorage flag — unset/zero renders the locked surface (symbols only), positive value renders the unlocked surface (symbols + card-content). The deck bytes are inspectable via View Source — the lock is a UI convention, not a server-gated fortress (see §5.C). **The free-surface invariant "symbols only — no interpretation, no per-symbol explanation" still applies to the locked render path; the unlocked render path is the named exception, gated by paid credits.**

**v0.36 amendment (v0.6.0).** As of v0.6.0 the free surface is **four coordinates**: tarot birth card (§1.C), sun sign (bare — rising joins at t1), public animal (year-pillar, alone — the private month-pillar animal joins at t1), and the catalog numeral. The v0.2.3 seven-coordinate free surface described above is superseded and preserved as lineage per L17; those coordinates relocate to the paid rungs per the §1.D tier ladder. The locked render path still ships symbols only — no interpretation, no per-symbol explanation — and remains the canonical free surface. The v0.22 unlocked card-content layer relocates to tier t3 (§1.D / §4.B v0.36): credits buy reads, the stored tier sets density at every render — and credits held with no stored tier are the pre-v0.6.0 purchase shape, grandfathered to t3 per §1.D.

**§1.A. Rising sign — surface coordinate, not a driver.**

As of v0.2.1 the result card surfaces an eighth coordinate when the operator provides birth time, country, latitude, and longitude: the rising sign (astronomical ascendant). Rising sign is computed from `(year, month, day, hour, minute, utcOffsetMinutes, lat, lng)` via standard ascendant math (Meeus); see `core/rising.js` for the exact algorithm. It is rendered on line 2 of the result card paired with the sun sign as `${sunSign} ↑ ${risingSign}`.

Rising sign is **surface-only**: it does not enter `getCard`, `resolveBracket`, or any catalog computation. The catalog driver remains `(sunSign, animal)` per §1. Rising adds a coordinate to the visual surface; it does not branch the card layer.

When required birth-time or location fields are missing, rising sign is `undefined` and line 2 falls back to the v0.2.0 bare-sun-sign render. Existing v0.2.0 profiles in localStorage continue to work without modification.

**v0.21 amendment (v0.2.7.2).** Birthplace input is city-level via `core/cities.js` — a 53,308-entry pop≥7500 subset of GeoNames cities5000, lazy-loaded from `assets/cities.json` per §5 (v0.21 same-origin lazy-load permission). Selecting a city atomically sets `tz` (IANA timezone), `lat`, `lng`, and country code on the form state. The country-code payload shape from v0.2.1 is retained for backward compatibility with stored v0.2.1–v0.2.7.1 profiles, but the UI no longer surfaces country selection.

Rising-sign math is now DST-aware and historical-tz-aware. The `computeRising({...tz...})` API in `core/rising.js` resolves the UTC offset for the (year, month, day, hour, minute) wall-time input via `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `{ timeZone: tz, timeZoneName: 'longOffset' }` — gives correct offset including DST transitions and historical-rule changes (e.g. Indiana pre-2006 no-DST, Russia post-2014 permanent UTC+3, US summer DST). As of v0.5.2, stored legacy country-code profiles first resolve the country/zone code to a representative IANA timezone, then use the same `computeRising` path as fresh city profiles. Legacy `getRisingSign(..., utcOffsetMinutes, ...)` is retained only as a low-level fixed-offset helper; both APIs are polar-safe.

Polar latitudes (|lat| > 66.5°, strictly past the polar circles) return `null` from both rising APIs. The horizon-plane geometry degenerates at the poles and a rising sign is not astrologically meaningful inside the polar circle. The UI surfaces "rising unavailable at this latitude" at city-selection time when |lat| > 66.5°. Boundary cases (|lat| == 66.5°) return a valid sign — the rule is strict greater-than.

Pre-1970 dates: `Intl.DateTimeFormat`'s historical-tz coverage falls back to LMT (Local Mean Time) approximation for some timezones in pre-tzdata-coverage periods. This is acknowledged as a precision tradeoff; the about-modal disclosure copy lands at v0.2.8 (calculator-framing rewrite cycle).

**v0.43 coherence note (B1).** The about-modal disclosure copy named above in the future tense ("lands at v0.2.8") shipped long ago — the calculator-framing about-modal copy is pinned on every push by `tests/payments_markup.test.js` (its about-modal "disclosure copy" suite). Read it as past-tense (shipped); the LMT-precision tradeoff it discloses is unchanged. Per L17 the original wording is preserved verbatim above.

The v0.14 wording (fixed UTC offsets per country entry; DST and historical-tz changes "out of scope") is retired by this amendment. The catalog-driver rule (rising surface-only, never entering `getCard` or `resolveBracket`) is unchanged.

**§1.B. Numerology surface — text triplet, calc reserved.**

As of v0.2.3 the result-card numerology surface renders as a three-number text line: life path, expression/name number, soul urge. Values are always space-separated for readability (e.g. `3 8 3`, `3 11 3`). **v0.23 amendment (CiC Fix A): the v0.22 hasMaster-branched concatenation (`383` for all-single-digit, spaced when any master present) is retired — the concatenated form read cognitively as a single three-digit number rather than three independent coordinates.** Codified by `tests/numerology_display.test.js`.

The additional calc fields personality (consonants only), birthday (day-of-month reduced with master preservation), and maturity (life-path sum plus expression/name-number sum, reduced with master preservation) remain on `buildProfile` as data-only — computed but not surfaced. They are reserved for v0.3.0+ paid surfacing per §1.

Catalog driver (§1) and rising semantics (§1.A) are unchanged. v0.2.3 reverts the v0.2.2 hexagon surface; this is breaking-on-render and additive-on-data. Calc-version remains v1.

**v0.43 coherence amendment (B2 + B3).** Two clauses above describe superseded states; per L17 the original wording is preserved verbatim and superseded here:
- **Numerology surface — now a name-derived pair, not a triplet (B2).** The "three-number text line (life path, expression/name number, soul urge)" describes the pre-v0.38 surface. §1.D v0.38 split `lifePath` (DOB-derived) onto the free surface, leaving the t1 numerology a name-derived **pair** (expression/name number + soul urge). #48 noted this under the §1.D *table*; §1.B itself is updated here. The always-space-separated invariant (CI-pinned by `tests/numerology_display.test.js`) is unchanged.
- **Personality / birthday / maturity — surfaced at t2, no longer "reserved" (B3).** Those three fields, described above as "reserved for v0.3.0+ paid surfacing," were surfaced at tier **t2** by §1.D v0.6.0 — the reservation's release condition fired and the clause is updated here to record it. They remain surface-only (no catalog/bracket effect) per §1.D.

**v0.54 nine-number amendment (calc v3, controller correction, STAGED 2026-07-20).** Active Pythagorean numerology has exactly nine terminal values: `1` through `9`. Life path, expression/name number, soul urge, personality, birthday, and maturity all use repeated digit-sum until a single digit remains; historical master stops are retired, so `11 → 2`, `22 → 4`, and `33 → 6`. Maturity continues to combine the already-reduced life-path and name-number components before its final reduction. The unreduced `*Sum` fields remain available as calculation trails, but they are not coordinate values. When a name supplies no contributing letters to a subset (no standard vowel for soul urge or no consonant for personality), that coordinate is unresolved (`null` in core, `—` on the entitled sheet), never the tenth value `0` and never a fabricated 1–9 meaning; maturity is likewise unresolved if its required name number is absent. Every resolved numerology coordinate is interpreted through the same nine-entry meaning registry in its own role beside two other numerology coordinates; the context panel labels that integrated layer `with the other numbers`. This amendment supersedes earlier active-surface references to preserved masters while leaving the historical v1 content files immutable.

**v0.62 master-preserving amendment (calc v4, controller decision, STAGED 2026-07-31).** The v0.54 nine-number amendment above is **superseded**; its text stands as lineage per L17. Active Pythagorean numerology has exactly **twelve** terminal values: `1` through `9` plus the master stops `11`, `22` and `33`. Life path, expression/name number, soul urge, personality, birthday and maturity all reduce by repeated digit sum **while the value is above 9, stopping immediately at a master stop** — so `29 → 11` and `38 → 11`, while `44 → 8` (44 is not a stop and its digit sum is already single). Maturity is unchanged in method: it reduces each required component first, adds the canonical components, and reduces the result under the same rule — which means it can itself land on a master, as `22 + 11 = 33` does. The unreduced `*Sum` fields remain calculation trails, not coordinate values, and a master must never live only in a trail: it is the coordinate. The v0.54 unresolved guard **survives verbatim** — an absent contributing letter class is `null` in core and `—` on the entitled sheet, never the tenth value `0` — and it is the only part of the nine-number cut that outlives it. Every one of the twelve resolves a registered meaning through `content/meanings.v3.js` without fallback; the master entries are the immutable `content/meanings.v1.js` records, reused byte-for-byte and never re-authored. Persisted current and saved profiles need no migration: they store reconstruction inputs, not derived outputs, so reopening recomputes under calc v4.

**Why, on the controller's word.** This is a product and commercial decision, not a calculation correction: `11`, `22` and `33` are part of what the product sells, and the operator ruled *"master numbers sell so keep them."* It was forced by a truth defect rather than a preference — the locked X-offer specimen shows `11` and `22` while the shipped calculator reduced them, and the 2026-07-31 Codex preflight (`audits/codex_x_offer_preflight_2026-07-31_response.md`) returned STOP on exactly that mismatch. The repair the controller refused is the cheap one: deleting the masters from the creative. The product is made truthful instead.

**What v0.54 got right, and is not being un-learned.** Two of its rules survive this reversal because they were never about the domain's size: the unresolved-not-zero guard above, and the versioning discipline that kept the historical content files immutable through the narrowing — which is precisely why widening again costs a new active file and no edit to a shipped one.

**§1.C. Tarot birth card — surface coordinate, not a driver (v0.5.0).**

As of v0.5.0 the result card surfaces a tarot birth card: a Major Arcana coordinate computed from the DOB alone, rendered as roman numeral + lowercase name (e.g. `XXI · the world`). It is the **lead row** of the free card, above the five-element coordinate. Computed in `core/birthcard.js` (`getBirthCard`), carried as the additive `birthCard` field on `buildProfile`.

This is a **free-surface coordinate**, inside the §1 symbols-only invariant — a labeled coordinate (numeral + arcana name), never interpretation. No per-card meaning text, no per-arcana explanation; that register stays the paid card-content layer (§1 v0.22). **Superseded in part by §1.G (v0.44):** this coordinate (and sun / public animal / life path, the other three free-tier VALUES) may now surface a static, tradition-cited meaning entry on tap — citation of what a named tradition says, not the interpretation this clause originally reserved for the paid layer. The distinction §1.G draws is citation-vs-interpretation, not free-vs-paid; the paid card-content layer (name/type/habit/note for the compound catalog reading) remains untouched and is not given away by §1.G. The clause adds a coordinate to the visual surface in the same way §1.A's rising sign does — it does **not** enter `getCard`, `resolveBracket`, or any catalog computation; the catalog driver remains `(sunSign, animal)` per §1.

Reduction tradition is **digit-sum**: sum every digit of the DOB, reduce by repeated digit-sum until ≤ 22. This is distinct from the life-path reduction in `core/profile.js` (which preserves the master stops `11 / 22 / 33` per §1.B v0.62 / §3 calc v4 — the intervening wording "floors at a single digit `1..9`" was the v0.54 nine-number contract and is preserved here as lineage per L17, superseded when calc v4 restored the masters; calc v3 rewrote this same parenthetical in lockstep with its own calculation change, which is why calc v4 does too); the birth-card reduction floors at ≤ 22 because the target range is the 22 Major Arcana, and **that contract is untouched by calc v4** — `22 → 0 · the fool` still holds. A reduced value of 22 maps to `0 · the fool`, guaranteeing every DOB resolves to a real arcana (the out-of-range `XXII` never renders). Both choices are operator-locked (see journal); codified by `tests/birthcard.test.js`.

Because it is a free coordinate, the birth card is included in the §5.D share surface — the shared PNG and the on-screen free card carry the same five coordinates. It carries no PII (numeral + arcana name only, no name/DOB) and no paid-content layer, so the §5.D invariants (a)/(b) hold unchanged.

**v0.36 amendment (v0.6.0).** The "same five coordinates" count in the paragraph above describes the v0.5.0 free surface and is preserved verbatim as lineage per L17. As of §1 v0.36 / §1.D the free card carries four coordinates and the shared PNG matches the card at the user's current render tier (§5.D v0.36). The birth card itself is unchanged by the tier ladder: it remains the lead coordinate of every tier's card, including free, and stays inside the §5.D share surface at every tier.

**§1.D. Tier ladder — coordinate density, surface-only (v0.6.0).**

As of v0.6.0 the paid surface is a three-rung tier ladder that reveals progressively more of the coordinate sheet. The ladder is operator-locked (2026-06-11):

| Tier | Param | Price | Card contents (cumulative) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **free** | — | $0 | birth card · sun (no rising) · public animal (year) · catalog numeral — 4 coordinates |
| **t1** | `?paid=t1` | $3 | + rising (when computable) · five-element · private animal (month) · numerology triplet (life path / expression / soul urge) |
| **t2** | `?paid=t2` | $6 | + personality · birthday · maturity · day pillar (animal + stem-element) |
| **t3** | `?paid=t3` | $9 | + hour pillar (animal + stem-element; four pillars complete) · the written 144-card entry (name/type/habit/bracket note) as ceiling bonus |

**Table currency (per L17 — the table above is operator-locked 2026-06-11 and preserved verbatim).** Two cells are superseded by later amendments: the **free** row is now **five** coordinates — `life path` joined free at v0.38 (the DOB-derived numerology split, amendment below) — and the **t1** `numerology triplet` is now the name-derived **pair** (expression/name number + soul urge), the life path having moved to free. Since v0.37 the card renders the full sheet at every tier as a compartment specimen sheet; sealed (higher-tier) cells carry no value. The **Price** column is superseded by the v0.55 ownership amendment (below): the rungs repriced 2026-07-26 to **t1 · $1 / t2 · $2 / t3 · $3** (Gumroad live; product IDs and `?paid=` params unchanged). The coordinate composition of every row is NOT superseded — the density ladder is unchanged.

Clauses, mirroring the §1.A / §1.B / §1.C surface-clause pattern:

- **Surface-only.** Tier coordinates add to the visual surface the way §1.A's rising sign does — none of them enters `getCard`, `resolveBracket`, or any catalog computation. The catalog driver stays `(sunSign, animal)` per §1; day and hour pillars never enter the catalog.
- **Stored tier.** `eight_ball_tier_v1` (§5 allow-list) records the highest rung purchased; absent = free. Monotonic: `applyPaidReturn` sets tier = max(current, purchased) by ladder order — never downgrades. An upgrade purchase raises the tier and adds credits like any paid return.
- **Density rule (single, total — v0.6.0 remediation R1).** Render density = f(stored tier, credit state), resolved at render time by one helper (`getRenderTier` in `ui/payments.js`, delegating to the pure `resolveRenderTier` in `core/payments.js`) — never a function of boot circumstance or shake action. Cold-boot rehydration, same-card re-shake, same-pair re-submit, free-try renders, paid reads, and paid-return boots all render the device's entitled tier. Free devices (no tier, no credits) render the free card.
- **Legacy grandfather (v0.6.0 remediation R2).** Credits present with no stored tier are the pre-v0.6.0 purchase shape — that product sold the written-entry unlock, which now lives at t3 — so the device resolves to t3 and the tier is persisted on first detection. Deterministic, total, never downgrades.
- **Credits unchanged.** Every paid return (any rung) grants +3 credits via the existing `applyPaidReturn` path; tries/credits semantics, pending-profile mechanic, and β-idempotent re-shake are unchanged from v0.22. Credits and the §4.B tries cap govern how many readings; the stored tier alone governs how much sheet each one shows.
- **t3 carries the card-content unlock.** The §1 v0.22 unlocked card-content layer relocates to t3 — the written entry is the ceiling bonus, not a per-credit unlock.
- **Rollback flag.** Free-tier composition is defined by the exported `TIER_COORDS` render constant in `ui/tiers.js`; reverting free to the pre-v0.6.0 surface is a one-line change plus a test update, not surgery.
- **Element systems not reconciled.** The free/t1 five-element stays the simplified year element (§3 calc v2); the day/hour stem-elements are NEW pillars labeled as such ("day pillar", "hour pillar") in the §2 clinical register. No contradiction surface, no §3 calc-version bump.

**v0.37 amendment (v0.7.0) — compartment render.** The card renders the full coordinate sheet at every tier. Coordinates above the device's render tier appear as sealed compartments: row, label, and cell structure visible; the coordinate value absent from the DOM — absent, not hidden. The free-surface invariant is unchanged in substance: the free render carries exactly four coordinate VALUES per the §1.D table; v0.7.0 adds the visible sealed structure of the remaining sheet. Sealed ≠ unresolvable: a paywalled field renders the seal treatment; a field that cannot resolve from given inputs (rising without birth time/place; hour pillar without birth time) renders the `—` empty field at any entitled tier. The written 144-card entry remains a t3-only unlock; its sealed block carries no entry text at any lower tier.

**v0.38 amendment (v0.7.x) — free life-path split (override #3, controller-authorized 2026-06-12).** The §1.D numerology coordinate splits by derivation. `lifePath` (DOB-derived) joins the free surface — preserving the free = DOB-only invariant (every free coordinate derives from date of birth alone, no name input) — so the free render carries **five** coordinate VALUES: birth card · sun (bare) · public animal (year) · life path · catalog numeral. The name-derived numerology narrows to the inner **pair** (expression/name number and soul urge) and stays at t1; the t1 "numerology triplet" in the table above is superseded and preserved as lineage per L17 — it is now a pair, the life path having moved to free. Surface-only and catalog-isolated like every §1.D coordinate: life path continues to drive low/mid/high bracket resolution within a catalog cell per §1, never the catalog index `(sunSign, animal)`; `getCard` is unchanged. §5.D: life path being free, the free share-PNG and the on-screen free card carry the same five coordinates — the PNG now includes the life-path numeral. It is a DOB-derived number carrying no name/DOB string, so the §5.D PII invariants (labeled numeral/symbol only; never name or DOB) hold unchanged, the same basis as the birth card and the catalog numeral. Free composition stays the one-line `TIER_COORDS` rollback flag (`ui/tiers.js`): `FREE_COORDS` gains `lifePath`; `CELL_COORD` maps `lifePath → lifePath` (free) while `nameNumber` / `soulUrge → numerology` (t1).

**v0.55 amendment (ownership + open free surface, 2026-07-26).** A rung purchase is **permanent and unlimited**. This supersedes three clauses above: (1) the **Stored tier** bullet's "adds credits like any paid return" — `applyPaidReturn` now performs exactly one write, the monotonic `tier = max(current, purchased)`, plus the pending-profile render; no credits exist to add. (2) The **Density rule** bullet's "f(stored tier, credit state)" — density is now f(stored tier, legacy-credit grandfather signal): a stored tier governs every render; the R2 legacy read is the only surviving credit consultation. (3) The **Credits unchanged** bullet in full — retired. There is no counter, no cap, and no per-read debit anywhere: quantity is unlimited at every tier, free included (§4.B v0.55), and **depth is the only thing money buys**. The **Legacy grandfather (R2)** clause SURVIVES verbatim: legacy credits with no stored tier still resolve to t3, the tier is persisted on first detection, and the credits are ignored forever after — no existing buyer is ever downgraded. The rungs repriced 2026-07-26 to **$1 / $2 / $3** (Gumroad live: rzqezp → `?paid=t1`, neysyv → `?paid=t2`, xjpvp → `?paid=t3` — IDs and delivery links unchanged). The tier table's coordinate composition and the `TIER_COORDS` constant are UNCHANGED: free = 5 of 15 · t1 = 10 · t2 = 14 · t3 = 15 + the written card entry.

**v0.58 amendment (public rung — a fourth rung, t4 · $9, 2026-07-29).** The ladder gains a **fourth** rung, `t4`, param `?paid=t4`, price **$9**. It is an APPEND, and the append is the whole of its mechanical footprint: `TIER_ORDER` becomes `['t1','t2','t3','t4']`, every existing rung keeps its rank and its meaning, every stored tier keeps its entitlement, monotonicity is unchanged, and **no t1/t2/t3 owner is affected in any way**. The **R2 legacy grandfather does NOT follow the top of the ladder** — a device with legacy credits and no stored tier still resolves to `t3`, because that product sold the written entry, not a rung that did not exist when it was bought; `tests/public_surface.test.js` pins this so a later rung cannot silently widen it.

What t4 adds is **not a coordinate**. The coordinate sheet is complete at t3 (15 of 15, plus the written entry); t4 adds a **reading of it** — three ranked domain families, one anti-fit family, and one shape-of-role line, computed offline and deterministically by `core/public.js` from the birth **date alone** (no name, no birth time). Like `cardEntry` it is a BLOCK, not a compartment: it takes no cell in the 14-cell grid and is excluded from the §1.F density census, so the t4 census is byte-identical to t3's. The §1.D v0.37 sealed-DOM rule governs it unchanged — below t4 the block renders its seal with the value nodes empty, absent rather than hidden.

**Three limits this clause states rather than leaves to be found.** (1) The tier's mode-of-work driver is the **life path** — a coordinate the FREE sheet already shows — so nothing in the t4 read is new information; it is a re-reading of open coordinates against fixed tables. Whether that is the right basis for a paid rung is an open design question, recorded at `PUBLIC_TIER_SPEC.md` §6.1. (2) Day-master **strength is the seasonal-state model, month branch only** (旺相休囚死), a documented simplification a practitioner may disagree with. (3) **No cross-model audit has read any of it** — neither the engine (#144, L48 sighting #14) nor this rung. §10 asks for one on a doctrine change; the lane is unavailable and the clearance is an explicit controller override, which is a decision, not a substitute.

**Commercially the rung is not live, and fails closed until it is.** The Gumroad product does not exist; creating it is the controller's action, never an agent's. `T4_PRODUCT_URL` in `ui/payments.js` ships **empty**, and while it is empty the t4 CTA carries no `href` and stays hidden, so no visitor can reach a dead checkout. Filling that constant in is the whole of what makes the rung buyable. The **§4.B v0.56 single-$3-offer sprint is materially untouched** while it runs (to 2026-08-08): the result-rail offer control, the paywall's $3 headline and the t3 product are byte-unchanged, and the only new element is a hidden anchor.

**Price-column reconciliation, stated plainly.** The rung table's Price column was superseded to **$1 / $2 / $3** by the v0.55 ownership amendment. This clause adds a rung at **$9**, which does not fit that run and is not claimed to: the $9 comes from the exposure-radius ladder the commissioning brief describes (private · comparative · public), a framing recorded in **no other tracked file** and one this project's constitution has never adopted. This amendment defines the rung's mechanics and price and takes **no position** on the radius ladder, on re-pricing t1–t3, or on retiring any existing rung. Reconciling the two ladders remains open doctrine work. The §2 arcade-toy carve-out survives intact and needs no re-amendment: t4 is a one-time purchase of permanent, unlimited density at that rung — no subscription, no recurring billing, no counter, no clawback.

**v0.59 amendment (public rung — the mode driver moves off the free surface, 2026-07-29).** §1.D v0.58 shipped the t4 read with its mode of work keyed by the **life path** — a coordinate the FREE sheet has carried since §1.D v0.38. That clause named the problem in its own text ("nothing in the t4 read is new information; it is a re-reading of open coordinates") and shipped anyway on controller word. This amendment closes it: the mode is now keyed by the **birthday** number (day of the month reduced by the §1.B v0.54 nine-number rule), a **t2** coordinate.

Three properties make it a fix rather than a substitution. It is **date-only**, so the tier's input contract is unchanged and no birth time is introduced. Its **domain is exactly 1..9**, so the authored nine-mode table carries over unedited — no copy is re-authored under cover of a mechanical change. And it is **not free**, which is the whole point: a paid rung's driver is now itself paid information.

On the merits rather than the mechanics: §1.G's own meaning registry names the birthday **"the recurring skill"** and the life path **"the long route"**. A mode of work is a recurring skill. The first keying was defensible; this one is apt.

**Content versioning (§4).** `content/public.v1.js` is IMMUTABLE and untouched. `content/public.v2.js` imports every table byte-for-byte and changes exactly two things: the mode provenance string (which named the wrong coordinate), and the modes' self-naming `lifePath` field, re-derived as `birthday` — a table whose own field lies about what keys it is the drift §4's versioning rule exists to prevent.

**What this does NOT change.** The ladder, the price, the entitlement, the census, the sealed-DOM rule, the fail-closed offer, and every other rung: all unchanged. `T4_PRODUCT_URL` remains empty and the rung remains unbuyable, so **no purchaser has ever received the superseded keying** — this correction reaches nobody's paid reading, which is precisely why it was worth making now rather than after a sale. The two limits §1.D v0.58 recorded and this amendment does not close: strength is still the month-branch seasonal-state model, and no cross-model audit has read the engine, the rung, or this clause.

**v0.60 amendment (the public rung is retired; the read folds into t3, 2026-07-29).** §1.D v0.58 added a fourth rung `t4` at **$9**; §1.D v0.59 corrected its driver. This clause **retires the rung**. `TIER_ORDER` returns to `['t1','t2','t3']`, the `?paid=t4` param is no longer a tier, and the public read — three ranked domain families, one anti-fit, one shape-of-role line — becomes a **t3 ceiling block** alongside the written card entry. It is still a BLOCK, not a coordinate, so the §1.F census is unmoved: t3 remains 15 of 15.

**Why, on the controller's ruling.** Three reasons, recorded because the rung was defended in this document two clauses ago. **(1) The tier was named for an exposure radius it never delivered.** Its commissioning brief called it *"you + the world; work is its legible instance"* — but the block renders privately, is absent from the §5.D share surface, and cannot be shown to anyone. What shipped was a depth increment priced as a radius increment. **(2) The price ordering could not be defended.** t3 sells fifteen coordinates plus a written 144-card entry for $3; t4 sold five derived strings for $9, computing **no new coordinate** — it is a lookup over values the t3 buyer already sees. **(3) There are no sales to build on.** The revenue-reset diagnosis on record is that the payment path *"works and has zero outside orders"*; adding a rung above a price that has never converted optimizes the end that is not binding.

**Nobody loses anything, and that took explicit work.** The rung was never buyable, but its unsigned `?paid=t4` return was live (§5.C), so devices can hold a stored `'t4'`. A retired rung therefore resolves to the rung that absorbed it — `RETIRED_TIERS = { t4: 't3' }` in `core/payments.js` — and the UI persists that rewrite on first detection, the same shape as the §1.D R2 legacy grandfather. **The case this protects is not hypothetical:** the stored tier is the only record of a purchase, so a genuine t3 buyer who opened the t4 URL once would otherwise be dropped to **free** by the retirement. `tests/public_surface.test.js` pins the migration and that case by name.

**The price column stops contradicting itself.** §1.D v0.58 put a $9 rung in this clause while the Price column above read **$1 / $2 / $3** — a contradiction v0.58 named and did not resolve. With the rung retired, the column is correct again and the exposure-radius ladder the brief describes remains, as before, adopted by no clause of this constitution.

**What survives from v0.58/v0.59.** The engine (`core/public.js`), its tables (`content/public.v1.js` + `v2.js`), the render module (`ui/public.js`), the sealed block and its §1.D v0.37 treatment, and the birthday keying. Only the rung, its price and its unopened offer are withdrawn. §4.B v0.56's single-$3 sprint is byte-untouched and is again the only purchase surface.

**v0.62 amendment (calc v4 — the public read's mode driver keeps its master value, and reaches its table through a declared bridge).** §1.D v0.59 moved the t3 public read's mode of work onto the **birthday** number and rested on one property: *"its domain is exactly 1..9, so the authored nine-mode table carries over unedited."* Calc v4 (§1.B v0.62) restores the master stops, so that property is gone — a birthday of `11` or `22` is a real coordinate value and the mode table has nine entries. (`33` is not reachable from a day of the month; the bridge covers it anyway so the mapping is total over the domain rather than over what happens to be reachable.)

**The two wrong fixes, named so they are not re-proposed.** Reducing the birthday before keying the mode would put `11` on the sheet while the reading silently treated it as `2` — the truth defect this whole cycle repairs, reintroduced one clause later. Authoring three master work modes would be new paid copy shipped under cover of a calculation change, which is not what a calculation change is for.

**What ships instead.** The birthday coordinate keeps its master value everywhere it is shown and everywhere it is reported. Only the TABLE LOOKUP is bridged, through the declared `MASTER_MODE_BRIDGE` in `content/public.v3.js` — `11→2`, `22→4`, `33→6`, the same three links the immutable v1 Concordance registry files, and no other mapping. **The bridge is disclosed in the returned reading, not applied silently**: `mode.birthday` carries the master, `mode.modeKey` carries the table key actually read, `mode.bridged` is a boolean, and `mode.bridgeNote` states the substitution in prose. `PUBLIC_SOURCES.mode` names the bridge rather than a reduction rule that no longer runs on this coordinate. `tests/public.test.js` pins all of it, and pins that `WORK_MODES` still has exactly nine entries — so a tenth-through-twelfth appearing there is a CI failure, not an unreviewed piece of paid copy.

**The disclosure REACHES THE READER, and saying so cost a refused audit.** The paragraph above was true and insufficient, and this clause records that rather than quietly widening. The first implementation disclosed the bridge in the object `core/public.js` returns and stopped there: the sole production formatter (`formatPublicRead` in `ui/public.js`) emitted three strings and discarded `mode.bridgeNote`, so a reader whose sheet showed birthday `11` received mode-`2` copy with nothing connecting the two — **the silent substitution this very clause names as the wrong fix, recreated one layer down at the render surface.** A Codex pre-merge audit refused the branch on it (P1, 2026-07-31, `audits/codex_calc_v4_premerge_audit_2026-07-31_response.md`), and it survived a green suite because every existing surface test used a non-master birthday. The block now carries a **fourth line**: the engine's own `bridgeNote`, empty for an unbridged reading. It is rendered on the **host sheet and on BOTH dyad sheets** — a second person with a master birthday is exactly as entitled to the disclosure as the first — and it is cleared on the sealed branch and on a subsequent unbridged render, so no entitled string and no stale note survives (§1.D v0.37). `index.html` is untouched: `ui/public.js` injects the node and its scoped CSS at init in the §6 v0.23 DI shape `ui/meanings.js` and `ui/dyad.js` already use, and the host's four-id boot wiring is byte-unchanged. The node collapses on `:empty` rather than the `hidden` attribute, deliberately — this repo has a logged case of an author `display:` rule beating the UA `[hidden]` rule and shipping a "hidden" control visible in production. **`content/public.v1.js` and `content/public.v2.js` are IMMUTABLE and untouched**; `content/public.v3.js` imports every table byte-for-byte and adds only the bridge, its note template, and the corrected provenance string. Nothing else about the block moves: it is still a t3 ceiling BLOCK, still absent from the §1.F census, still governed by the §1.D v0.37 sealed-DOM rule, and `ui/public.js` renders the same three strings it rendered before.

**v0.61 amendment (the dyad rung — a fourth rung, `t5` · $6, 2026-07-30).** The ladder gains a fourth rung, **`t5`**, param `?paid=t5`, price **$6**. `TIER_ORDER` becomes `['t1','t2','t3','t5']`. It is an APPEND and the append is the whole of its mechanical footprint on the existing rungs: every one keeps its rank and its meaning, every stored tier keeps its entitlement, monotonicity is unchanged, and **no t1/t2/t3 owner is affected in any way**. The **R2 legacy grandfather does NOT follow the top of the ladder** — a device with legacy credits and no stored tier still resolves to `t3`, because that product sold the written entry and never a second person's sheet; `tests/dyad_surface.test.js` pins this beside the pin §1.D v0.58 left for the same reason.

**The gap at `t4` is load-bearing, not cosmetic.** The obvious token for a fourth rung is `t4`, and taking it would have been a silent, unrecoverable defect. `t4` was the public rung for part of 2026-07-29 (§1.D v0.58) and was retired the same day (v0.60), which left `RETIRED_TIERS = { t4: 't3' }` and devices that can hold a stored `'t4'` forever from the unsigned `?paid=` return. A dyad shipped as `t4` would put the same key in `TIER_ORDER` **and** in `RETIRED_TIERS`, so `normalizeTier` would rewrite every paying dyad device to `t3` before `isTier` ever saw it — **paying customers silently collapsed to the rung beneath the one they bought.** `t5` was never live as an unsigned return, so it is clean. The invariant is now derived rather than remembered: `RETIREMENT_COLLISIONS` in `core/payments.js` is computed from both tables at module load and pinned empty, so a future rung cannot reuse a retired token without failing CI. **Rank is ladder POSITION, never the digit in the token** — `t5` ranks 4 — and that too is pinned.

**What t5 adds is a relation, not a coordinate.** The coordinate sheet is complete at t3 (15 of 15, plus the written entry and the public read). t5 buys a **second complete sheet** plus a **relation layer** between the two, computed offline and deterministically by `core/dyad.js` from the two calculated profiles alone. Three axes, each a lookup or a join over frozen tables in `content/dyad.v1.js`: the directed five-element relation between the two **day masters**, one authored passage per direction (A→B and B→A are separate entries, never one sentence read twice); the **combined life path**, the digit-reduced sum of the two, whose number meaning is read from the existing nine-entry registry rather than re-authored; and the **card pair**, read across the two registers the four-slot grammar exposes to a pair — the year branches through the same five named branch families §1.I already uses, and the life-path brackets as an ordered pair against the deck's own arrival → construction → command arc. Like `cardEntry` and `publicRead` it is a **BLOCK**, not a compartment: it takes no cell in the 14-cell grid and is excluded from the §1.F density census, so the t5 census is byte-identical to t3's. The §1.D v0.37 sealed-DOM rule governs it unchanged — below t5 the block renders its seal with the value nodes empty, absent rather than hidden.

**The relation layer computes no new coordinate for either person.** The day master, the life path, the catalog index and the bracket are all values the single sheet already carries; what is new is the relation between two of them. That is the same limit §1.D v0.58 recorded against itself and it is recorded here rather than left to be found — with the difference that the second sheet IS new information, because it belongs to a second person the device has never read.

**Commercially the rung is not live, and fails closed until it is.** The Gumroad product does not exist; creating it is the controller's action, never an agent's (§10). `T5_PRODUCT_URL` in `ui/dyad.js` ships **empty**, and while it is empty the CTA carries no `href` and stays hidden, so no visitor can reach a dead checkout. **Filling that constant in alone does not make the rung buyable** (corrected post-`t5` audit, PR #187 R6 — a prior draft made the entry control's visibility react to this constant with no click path behind it, a dead button waiting to be exposed the moment the URL was set). `dyadEntryVisible` is entitlement-only and does not consult this constant; a real below-t5 offer needs its own coherent click path designed and shipped together with whatever control triggers it. The **§4.B v0.56 single-$3 sprint is byte-untouched** while it runs (to 2026-08-08): the paywall, its $3 headline, the result-rail offer control and the t3 product are unchanged, and `index.html` still contains exactly one Gumroad URL. **Price-column reconciliation, stated plainly:** the rung table's Price column reads $1 / $2 / $3 per v0.55; this clause adds a rung at **$6**, which does not fit that run and is not claimed to. The $6 comes from the entity-count ladder the commissioning brief describes (one person · two people), a framing this constitution has not adopted and takes no position on here. Reconciling the two ladders remains open doctrine work. The §2 arcade-toy carve-out survives intact and needs no re-amendment: t5 is a one-time purchase of permanent, unlimited density at that rung — no subscription, no recurring billing, no counter, no clawback.

**Corrected pre-merge on an independent audit (PR #187, 2026-07-30).** The first implementation of this clause was refused by a Codex pre-merge audit with six P1 findings, and three of them were contradictions between this document and the code shipped under it rather than defects in the code alone. They are named here because a clause that was wrong once should say so: the entry control and person B's sheet were reachable **below t5** while this clause said t5 buys them; the relation engine **recomputed** the day master while §1.J said it recomputes nothing; and the combined-life-path meaning was **re-authored** in `content/dyad.v1.js` while §1.J said it comes from the existing registry. All three are closed in code, and the sentences below now describe what ships. The audit's own record is `audits/codex_pr187_premerge_audit_2026-07-30_response.md`.

**Entitlement is all-or-nothing.** There is no partial dyad. Below t5 the entry control is absent, the screen refuses to open, a submission is refused, and the render produces nothing — four independent gates on one predicate (`dyadEntitled`), because the previous version's single gate on the relation passages left the whole second sheet open. A t3 device — which owns the complete SINGLE sheet, the written entry and the public read — receives no second sheet at all. That is the boundary the rung is priced on.

**Two limits this clause states rather than leaves to be found.** (1) The two dyad sheets are built by `ui/sheet.js`, a second DOM writer over the SAME pure value mapping (`cellRenderState`) and the same tier constants the host sheet renders through, rather than a second live instance of `ui/tiers.js`. That module is a singleton imported by 14 of the 51 test files, two of which assert on its source layout; converting it would have meant rewriting protected tests to fit a refactor, and the audit packet requires that existing single-reading behaviour remain unchanged — which a new module cannot regress and a rewrite of the shipped path can. The equivalence is pinned by a bounded differential test — one profile per life-path facet-anchor group, across every tier, every coordinate cell against a REAL `renderTierSections` host render (not a second call to the shared `cellRenderState` function standing in for it), plus the written-entry and public-read blocks each side varies independently of the cell grid (corrected post-`t5` audit, PR #187 R3 — the first version of this claim named "every profile" over a profile space that name-derived numerology makes unenumerable, and its own suite never actually drove `renderTierSections`, so it could not have caught R2's written-entry regression). The cost of the bound is named: sheets built there carry no element `id`, so they are deliberately **not tappable** (the §1.D v0.52 interaction clause covers the host sheet only) — emitting the host's ids would make `ui/meanings.js`'s `getElementById` lookups ambiguous and break the host's own panel. (2) **A fresh independent Codex re-audit has cleared the corrected branch.** The audit cited above read the previous implementation and returned DO NOT MERGE; a second, independent Codex re-audit read `e34458a` and returned SAFE TO MERGE, recorded at `audits/codex_pr187_premerge_reaudit_2026-07-30_response.md`. Nothing in that record makes the t5 checkout live — the Gumroad product still does not exist and `T5_PRODUCT_URL` still ships empty.

**v0.63 amendment (the kua block — the first gender-keyed surface, controller override, 2026-08-03).** The t3 ceiling gains a third BLOCK, `kuaRead`: the Eight Mansions personal trigram (Ba Zhai Ming Jing), computed by `core/kua.js` from the SOLAR birth year and the profile's optional `gender`. The solar-year boundary is Li Chun from `core/calendar.js`'s authority-pinned term table, at-or-after — deliberately not a fixed Feb-4 (Li Chun 2021 is Feb 3, and the test suite carries that discriminating case). Like `cardEntry` and `publicRead` the block takes no cell in the 14-cell grid and is excluded from the §1.F census, so t3 remains 15 of 15; the §1.D v0.37 sealed-DOM rule governs it unchanged; and it joins t3's coordinate list, so the t5 append carries it onto both dyad sheets via a handed-in read.

**Gender is the first non-DOB demographic input, and its rules are stated here rather than left to be found.** (1) It is OPTIONAL, forever: the control defaults to empty, an off-vocabulary value is dropped at every write seam (strict `'male' | 'female'`), and no surface may require it. (2) It never touches the free surface: the free = DOB-only invariant (§1.D v0.38) is untouched because, like the name input, the field exists on the form but feeds only a sealed paid block. (3) A profile WITHOUT gender renders BOTH classical values, labeled, with a register note naming the method's gender dependence — never a silent single-gender default. (4) The formula's raw 5 has no trigram and files male→2 / female→8; the remap is RETURNED by the engine (`{number, remapped}`) and rendered as a note — the §1.B v0.62 anti-silent-substitution rule applied to this coordinate. (5) A competing modern school changes the constants for post-2000 births (male 9−S / female 6+S); the product ships the single continuous rule (male 11−S / female 4+S over the solar-year digit sum, arithmetically identical to the classical 19xx statements and pinned so by a century sweep) and names the fork in `content/kua.v1.js`'s provenance. **The two wrong fixes, named so they are not re-proposed:** carrying both schools as a switchable table would put two values behind one coordinate and re-open the §1.B v0.62 truth defect; writing the tradition's directional guidance would ship oracle register.

**Register law: registry facts only.** Eight Mansions' traditional use of the trigram filing is directional guidance — auspicious and inauspicious orientations — which is oracle register and permanently out per §12. `content/kua.v1.js` carries citation entries in the §1.G shape (what the named tradition FILES: trigram, glyph, element, seat direction, east/west group), and `tests/kua_content.test.js` pins the no-guidance register mechanically alongside the voice scans. The block is not tappable, matching `publicRead`'s recorded limit.

**Process note, recorded because §13 requires the trigger to be named.** Product-surface work is frozen on the reach-bottleneck finding; this feature ships as a **controller override** — the operator's explicit 2026-08-03 decision chose the vehicle (kua), the tier (t3 block), and the process (override + cross-model audit before merge) — per the §1.G v0.44 precedent; override authority not re-litigated. §8 gate 6 / §10 cross-model audit precedes merge; the Codex lane is parked, so the audit runs as an operator relay per the PR #198 precedent.

**v0.65 amendment (no-gender kua renders NOTHING, operator word 2026-08-06).** Rule (3) above is superseded per L17 (its text stands as lineage): a profile WITHOUT gender no longer renders both classical values — on the operator's direct word, the dual-value fallback and its register note are **retired**, and the kua block is **ABSENT** for a no-gender profile: hidden entirely, value nodes empty, no seal. The block now has three states — open (entitled, gendered, resolvable) · sealed (below t3, the ordinary block seal) · absent (entitled but no read: no gender on file, or unresolvable) — and absent is deliberately NOT a seal, because a seal at full entitlement would violate the F4 sealed ≠ unresolvable rule; it is also NOT a silent single-gender default, which remains the named wrong fix. What rule (3) protected survives intact: the method's gender dependence is never hidden behind a defaulted value — absence discloses by showing nothing rather than by explaining. `ui/kua.js` drops `formatKuaBoth` and the `getKuaBoth` import (a source pin in `tests/kua_surface.test.js` keeps the fallback from quietly returning); `core/kua.js` is unchanged in behavior and API — `getKuaBoth` remains pure engine surface under its own tests, its doc comment reframed engine-only in the same change (the journal records the edit). The dyad sheets follow the same three-state rule (`ui/sheet.js`): a no-gender person B carries no kua block beside a gendered person A. Rules (1)/(2)/(4)/(5), the register law, the census exclusion and the not-tappable limit are all unchanged; the optional gender field itself is untouched — supplying one still renders that gender's kua exactly as v0.63 shipped it.

**§1.J. The dyad relation layer — named relations between two readings (v0.61, t5).**

§1.I licensed comparing two browser-local saved readings and bounded it with a **register law**. The dyad is the paid, authored-passage form of the same act, and it is bound by that same law rather than a looser one written for it. §1.I is the controlling precedent; this clause extends it and narrows nothing.

**Register law, extended.** Every dyad passage is a **citation of a named relation between two coordinates**, never an interpretation of two people. It MUST NOT emit a compatibility percentage, harmony score, ranking, prediction, advice, diagnosis, soulmate claim, recommendation, or invented synthesis across traditions — the §1.I list, verbatim — and it adds one rule §1.I did not need: **no person may be the grammatical subject of any sentence.** The subject of every authored line is a branch, an element, a bracket or a number. Both rules are mechanical, not aspirational: `tests/dyad_content.test.js` scans the tables AND the assembled runtime output through the canonical §2/§4 voice tables plus a compatibility-claim scan and a person-subject scan, each with a positive-fire sentinel (the `pii_scan` guard-the-guard pattern) so a scan that could never fail is itself a failure. Where a tradition's own English name would trip the law — 六合 *liuhe* is conventionally rendered "six harmony" — the passage carries the **transliteration**, which is both stricter and more faithful than the gloss.

**The unfiled state is mandatory.** As in §1.I, an unsupported or same-value pair is `unfiled` and **says so in the prose**: the branch tables name no relation for a branch with itself, so the dyad claims none. Fabricating an `adjacent` reading to avoid an empty cell is the failure mode this rule exists to prevent.

**Calculation isolation.** The dyad engine takes **two already-calculated profiles**, never two payloads — the order §1.I fixes for Concordance, and load-bearing here for the same reason. It recomputes no coordinate: the day master is **read off `profile.dayPillar`**, so a supplied coordinate that contradicts the raw date still wins and there is one calculation of record. (The first implementation re-derived it from `yyyy/mm/dd`. It agreed with the supplied value in every ordinary case, which is precisely why a green suite proved nothing and an audit had to find it; "usually identical" is not the contract.) A missing or internally incoherent pillar throws rather than resolving to a plausible wrong relation, and the surface seals the block. The engine does not enter `getCard` or `resolveBracket`'s rule — it calls both, per profile, exactly as the single render does — and returns the two profile objects it was handed, so each side is byte-identical to the standalone reading **by construction**. There is no second calculation path to keep in sync. `core/profile.js`, `tests/fixtures.json` and the catalog driver are untouched; the calc version does not move.

**One entry contract for both forms.** The second-person form takes the same fields as the primary onboarding form — name, date of birth, optional birth time, optional birthplace — and runs the **same validator** (`validateBirthInput` in `ui/profile.js`), not a second implementation of the same rules: trimmed name, a real local-today future gate, and the 1900 floor the calendar tables require. Person B therefore resolves a rising sign when a birthplace is given, which the first implementation could not. `ui/citysearch.js` was converted from a module singleton to a per-instance controller to make that safe: its handlers were module-scope closures over module-scope refs, so a second birthplace field would have silently repointed the **primary** form's listeners and dropped the rising sign from every shipped single reading.

**What renders, and what is cleared.** Each side renders a complete standalone sheet — every coordinate, its own written 144-card entry, its own public read — followed by the relation layer. Closing the screen, or an invalid re-submission, blanks **every** node the render can write, from one enumeration shared with the fill path; `hidden` is not deletion, and the first implementation left person B's first name and fourteen coordinate values in live hidden DOM. Person B's birthplace selection is dropped by the same path, so a stale city cannot hand the next second person someone else's timezone.

**Content and deck boundary.** Static tables live in `content/dyad.v1.js`, immutable and versioned per §4 — a revision ships as `dyad.v2.js`. The wuxing cycles, the branch-relation families and the `recorded, not certified.` qualifier are **imported from** `content/concordance.v1.js`, not redefined; the nine number meanings are the existing registry — the combined-path axis emits `NUMEROLOGY_MEANINGS[n].body` **byte-for-byte** beside a neutral clause that states only the arithmetic of the reduction. (The first implementation authored nine bodies here that restated the registry's own clauses — for 1, "starting a sequence rather than joining one" against the registry's "…joining one already underway" — while also looking the registry up and rendering only the copy. Two sources for one number's meaning is the drift §4's versioning rule exists to prevent.) **No deck string reaches the relation layer** — the t3 written entry is what t3 sells, and a t5 passage that paraphrased or recombined it would sell the ceiling twice. `tests/dyad.test.js` and `tests/content_shape.test.js` both sweep the assembled output against all 864 deck strings for leakage.

**v0.62 amendment (calc v4 — masters are valid dyad input and valid dyad output).** Three sentences of this clause described the retired nine-number contract and are superseded; everything else, including the register law, the unfiled state, the calculation isolation and the privacy boundary, is unchanged and narrowed in no respect. **(1) Input.** Both life paths are validated against the ACTIVE terminal domain (`1..9, 11, 22, 33`) **before they are summed**, so a master enters the relation as itself and is never reduced ahead of validation. A value outside the twelve still throws — `10` and `44` are pinned by name, because a plausible integer that no reduction terminates on is the sharp case a widened domain must still refuse. **(2) Output.** The combined path uses the same master-preserving reducer, so the sum runs `2..66` and can itself land on `11`, `22` or `33` — `9 + 2`, `11 + 11` and `11 + 22` all do. Its meaning is read from the active registry (`content/meanings.v3.js`) byte-for-byte, exactly as before; the registry simply has twelve entries now instead of nine. **(3) The reduction clause selects on `sum !== combined`, not `sum > 9`.** Those were the same test while every terminal value was a single digit and are not the same test now: a sum of `11` that STOPS at `11` would otherwise fire the reduced frame and announce that 11 reduces to 11 — a claim about a reduction that did not happen, which is the exact defect the two-frame split exists to prevent. **Content versioning.** `content/dyad.v1.js` is IMMUTABLE and untouched; the active tables are `content/dyad.v2.js`, which imports all 25 ordered element pairs, the six branch registers, the bracket arc and the nine ordered bracket registers unedited, and changes exactly two strings — the two combined-path frames and the numerology provenance line — each of which named the nine-number rule rather than describing a relation. The bracket axis needs no change: masters resolve to `high` per §1.H v0.62, so the nine ordered bracket registers stay total.

**Privacy boundary (§5 / §5.F).** No account, backend, sync, analytics event, remote database, third-party storage, endpoint, network call, runtime dependency, or calculation-engine change is introduced. **The second person is never persisted**: they live in one module-local binding for the life of the screen and are gone on close and on reload — the §5.F transient shape. `ui/dyad.js` names no `localStorage` key, reads none and writes none, so `tests/privacy_scan.test.js`'s key allow-list is **unchanged**. No dyad share artifact ships: the §5.D surface is the single sheet and is byte-untouched.


**v0.67 amendment (the kua block is DELETED; the gender input is retained, 2026-08-06 operator word).** The §1.D v0.63 kua block and its v0.65 three-state render are **withdrawn in full**: `core/kua.js`, `content/kua.v1.js`, `ui/kua.js` and the three kua suites are deleted, `kuaRead` leaves `T3_COORDS` and the unseal delta, and the instanced dyad sheets lose the block. The clauses above stand as lineage per L17. **No census change** — kua was a BLOCK, never a compartment, so §1.F is unmoved at 16.

**Where the deleted content file went, recorded because it declared itself immutable (added 2026-08-06 on a pre-merge audit finding).** `content/kua.v1.js` carried the standard §4 header — *"Once shipped it is IMMUTABLE — a future change is a kua.v2.js that carries these tables unedited, never an in-place edit."* Deleting a file that says that is worth stating plainly rather than leaving to be discovered. **It is not a §4 breach:** §4's immutability governs *editing* a shipped pool in place, which is the taste-drift failure it exists to prevent; this is a withdrawal of the whole surface on the operator's word, and the file was not edited at any point. **It is not lost, either** — the last version to ship is `a463a27:content/kua.v1.js`, reachable in full from history, which is the same "archive, don't delete" posture §1.G records for the purged `lab/` prototype. What was deliberately NOT done is retain the file unreferenced in the tree: a content module with no reader is dead code, and the same cycle that deleted this block spent a refinement pass removing exactly that. If the kua surface is ever revived it starts from `kua.v2.js` carrying those tables unedited, per the header's own instruction.

**The offer shrinks, and the copy says so.** The paywall and about-modal no longer promise "the kua line": the complete sheet is the eleven sealed coordinates, their meanings (the moon cell excepted per §1.K), the written card entry and domain fit. The price is unchanged at $3 by operator ruling — a rung's contents narrowing without a price change is a commercial decision recorded here rather than an omission.

**The optional `gender` field SURVIVES the deletion, and that is the part this clause exists to flag.** §5 v0.63 admitted it to the storage allow-list on the stated ground that it "feeds only the kua line". That consumer no longer exists, so as of this amendment the product **stores a demographic input that no surface reads**. It was retained on explicit operator word; the field keeps its strict `'male' | 'female'` vocabulary, keeps being dropped off-vocabulary at every write seam, keeps round-tripping through the archive, and still never touches the free surface. Its control was rehomed from the deleted `ui/kua.js` into `ui/profile.js` (the form controller), which also closed a defect the move exposed: `resetFormDisplay` had cleared the field through the retired hook, so a stale gender briefly survived a form reset — the F1 residue class, caught by its own test and fixed.

**The open question, stated rather than left implicit:** a stored field with no reader either needs a named purpose or needs removing from the §5 allow-list. This amendment does neither — it records that the retention is deliberate and the justification is now vacant, and leaves both to the operator. Person B's second-gender input on the dyad screen was NOT retained: it existed solely to key person B's kua line and would otherwise collect a demographic for no surface at all.


**v0.68 amendment (the comparative folds into t3 — one price, 2026-08-06 operator word).** `dyadRelation` moves from the retired t5 rung into **`T3_COORDS`**: the $3 rung now carries the §1.J comparative — a second person's complete sheet plus the named relation layer — alongside the written entry and the public read. **`t5` is RETIRED into t3** (`RETIRED_TIERS = { t4: 't3', t5: 't3' }`), `TIER_ORDER` returns to `['t1','t2','t3']`, and `RETIREMENT_COLLISIONS` stays empty because t5 leaves the ladder in the same change that adds it to the table.

**Nobody can be downgraded, and the reasoning is stronger than it was for t4.** `T5_PRODUCT_URL` shipped **empty for the rung's entire life** (§1.D v0.61 fail-closed), so no device can hold a *purchased* t5; the unsigned `?paid=t5` return was nonetheless reachable, which is why the migration exists at all. And unlike an ordinary retirement, the successor is a **superset** of what the retired rung conferred: everything t5 could render, t3 now renders. This retirement cannot reduce anyone's access even in principle.

**The census does not move.** `dyadRelation` is a BLOCK, not a compartment — it takes no cell in the 15-cell sheet and is excluded from the §1.F census, exactly as `cardEntry` and `publicRead` are. t3's open/sealed/total stays 16/0/16, and no compartment's entitlement changed.

**One inversion is deliberate and is recorded rather than deleted.** §1.D v0.61 / §1.J said "t5 buys the second sheet AND the relation" and `tests/dyad_surface.test.js` pinned the consequence as *"t3 — which buys the complete SINGLE sheet — gets no second sheet at all."* That sentence is now false by decision. The test was **rewritten, not removed**, and its replacement states the flip explicitly, so the change reads as a product decision in the suite rather than as a silently relaxed guard. Every other F2 protection survives unchanged in kind — `dyadEntitled` is still the single predicate every gate consults, the entry control is still absent below the entitled rung, `open()` and `submitSecond()` still refuse and render nothing, and the R6 rule that no product-URL constant can surface a dead control is untouched. Only the threshold moved, from t5 to t3.

**Commercially, this clause changes the product's contents, not its storefront.** The listing at `xjpvp` still says what it said; updating the Gumroad product description to name the comparative is an **operator-hand action** — no agent may mutate the store (§10 / the standing credential and action-time-approval gates). Until that happens the shipped surface and the storefront copy disagree, and that gap is stated here rather than left to be discovered. The price is unchanged at $3 by operator word; `T5_PRODUCT_URL` and the unshipped `neysyv` comparative listing (§1.D v0.62, unpublished) are now moot for entitlement purposes.

**§1.E. Provenance placards — labeled derivation note, surface-only (Coordinate Legibility Pack, v0.40).** Each coordinate may carry a **provenance placard**: a labeled, surface-only note naming the DERIVATION of that coordinate — its method/source (e.g. `digit-sum reduction`, `tropical zodiac`, `ascendant`, `solar term`, `letter-value sum`) — rendered per row in the row's cell order, mirroring the `.coord-title` grammar. The placard is **clinical derivation only, never interpretation** (§2): it names HOW a coordinate is computed, never what it "means." It is **tier-invariant** (a coordinate's derivation does not change with seal state) and **catalog-isolated** like every §1.x surface coordinate (§1 / §1.D): the note is keyed to the coordinate, never to a profile value or the catalog driver `(sunSign, animal)`; `getCard` is unchanged. It adds **no coordinate VALUE** to any surface — the free-surface VALUE count is unchanged (still five per §1.D v0.38) — and carries **no PII**: it names a derivation method/source, never a name, DOB, or derived value. A **sealed** compartment's placard still names the coordinate's derivation (structure/label only, the value absent per the v0.37 compartment contract) — so a hatched cell reads as a real, sourced coordinate withheld at the user's tier, not a rendering failure (the F1-WATCH legibility fix). Visibility reuses the existing symbol-label toggle (`.card.labels-revealed`) — off by default, **no new localStorage key**. The placard is **NOT serialized into the §5.D share artifact**: the share path reads `.coord-val` + `.coord-title` (via `ui/tiers.js shareRowRefs`, which `ui/share.js` consumes as a snapshot), never `.coord-prov`, so the PNG is unchanged. Implementation: `ui/tiers.js` `PROV_NOTE` (coordinate → derivation note) + `provText` (pure, per-row join) + an init-time `.coord-prov` write pass; `index.html` carries the `.coord-prov` CSS gate only.

**§1.F. Legibility surfaces — atlas legend + density census, labeled, surface-only (Coordinate Legibility Pack cuts 2–3, v0.41).** Two further legibility surfaces extend the §1.E pattern; this clause grants them the literal coverage flagged at the cut-2 Codex audit (Hook 7). Both are **surface-only** and **catalog-isolated** (keyed to coordinates or the tier constants, never a profile value or the catalog driver `(sunSign, animal)`; `getCard` unchanged), add **no coordinate VALUE** and **no PII**, and stay in the §2 clinical register (a label/census, never interpretation, never a CTA). **(a) Atlas** — a per-coordinate system-name legend (e.g. `life-path number`, `soul-urge number`) that disambiguates the abbreviated multi-cell row titles; gated by the existing `.card.labels-revealed` toggle (off by default, **no new localStorage key**), rendered in `.coord-atlas` outside `.coord-title` so it is **not serialized into the §5.D PNG**. **(b) Density census** — an **always-on** aggregate count in the result rail, OUTSIDE `#card-face` (so off the §5.D PNG): `N of 15 coordinates open · M sealed at paid tiers` (the 14 sheet cells plus the always-open catalog numeral — base 15, and the free count 5, match the §1.D free-coordinate framing), computed purely from `TIER_COORDS` + `CELL_COORD` via the pure `tierDensitySummary` — never from a profile. It is the on-screen counted form of the §5.D v0.39 `sealed remainder` marker, with **no FOMO/urgency/price** (§2 v0.35 / §5.C). Both retire the F1-WATCH "hatch reads as broken" risk — atlas at the per-cell level, density at the whole-sheet level. §1.E remains the provenance clause; §1.F covers the atlas + density siblings.

**Census currency (per L17 — the clause above is preserved verbatim, 2026-08-06).** One figure in (b) is superseded and the rest of the clause is not. The census reads **`N of 16`**, not `N of 15`: §1.K added the moon compartment, so the sheet carries **15 cells plus the always-open catalog numeral — base 16**. The v0.64 footer entry recorded the move (`§1.F census base 15 → 16`) and `tierDensitySummary` in `ui/tiers.js` has computed it from `CELL_KEYS.length + 1` since; this clause body still said 14/15, which is the half-edited shape §1.D's own ladder row was corrected for earlier in this cycle. **The free count is unchanged at five** — moon joined t1, not free, so the §1.D free-coordinate framing the parenthetical appeals to still holds. Everything else in (a) and (b) — surface-only, catalog-isolated, no coordinate VALUE, no PII, no new localStorage key, off the §5.D PNG, no FOMO/urgency/price — is unchanged and narrowed in no respect. Blocks stay excluded from the count: `cardEntry`, `publicRead` and `dyadRelation` are not compartments, so folding the comparative into t3 (§1.D v0.68) left the census untouched at 16.

**§1.G. Coordinate meanings — static, tradition-cited descriptions (v0.44, controller override).** Four of the five free-tier coordinate VALUES — arcana, sun, public animal, life path — are tappable. Tapping opens a labeled panel carrying a static, hand-authored entry from `content/meanings.v1.js`: a short register tag plus one to two sentences stating what the *named tradition* (tarot / western tropical zodiac / Chinese zodiac / Pythagorean numerology) associates with that specific symbol. This is **citation, not interpretation**: entries name what a tradition says, never what the reading "means" for the person who drew it, never advice, never a prediction — same §2 clinical/anti-oracle boundary as everywhere else in this document, enforced here by `tests/meanings_content.test.js` (BANNED_VOICE_REGISTER + BANNED_PATTERNS scan against every entry, imported from the same canonical lists `tests/profile.test.js` uses against the deck — no duplicated policy table) plus a second-person-address scan (`you`/`your`) with zero tolerance. **The catalog index is deliberately excluded** — the compound `(sunSign, animal)` reading is the paid card entry (name/type/habit/note, §1/§4); a free meaning for the catalog value would give away what t3 sells. **Static, not generated:** no network call, no LLM, no DOB or name leaves the device — a stricter privacy posture than the alternative considered and rejected this cycle (an LLM-narrated derivation-trace prototype, `lab/`, built 2026-06-26 through 07-03, which required a serverless proxy and transmitted DOB-derived strings to an external API for two of its five coordinates; purged 2026-07-04 rather than shipped, tag `archive/interrogation-layer-purged-2026-07-04` preserves it as historical record per L17-adjacent "archive, don't delete" discipline). Implementation: `content/meanings.v1.js` (58 entries: 22 arcana + 12 sun + 12 animal + 12 life path incl. masters 11/22/33), `ui/meanings.js` (`initMeaningsUI(refs)`, DI shape per §6 — injects its own panel markup + scoped CSS at init time rather than touching `index.html`'s static markup/style block, keeping the §6 1500-line single-file budget to a 2-line net footprint there: one import, one init call). **Controller override, not a measured reach fix:** shipped while product features are FROZEN per the reach-bottleneck finding (`~/Desktop/8ball/reach/REACH_CONTROL.md`, 2026-06-24 — real reach remains ~2 orders of magnitude below the ~2–3k tripwire); same override class as the v0.38 free-life-path-split (§13 named-trigger discipline does not bind a controller override, per precedent). **Dissent on record:** the orchestrator's standing recommendation is that product-surface work stay frozen until reach moves, on the reasoning that a confusing paywall/no-visible-counter problem (separately fixed this same week, PR #69) compounds faster at low reach than a new explainer surface helps it; the operator elected to ship this anyway, override authority not re-litigated.

**v0.52 amendment — every coordinate compartment is interactive (controller override, STAGED 2026-07-20).** The v0.44 sentence limiting tap interaction to four free-tier values is superseded at the **interaction layer only**. All fourteen specimen-sheet coordinate compartments are tappable and keyboard-reachable at every tier, including sealed and unresolved compartments; the catalog numeral remains excluded. The four coordinates backed by `content/meanings.v1.js` continue to show their exact versioned tradition-cited entries. Every other resolved coordinate opens a clinical detail containing only its existing system name (`ATLAS_NOTE`) and derivation method (`PROV_NOTE`) plus an explicit statement that no added interpretation is assigned. An unresolved compartment states the additional birth input required to calculate it. A sealed compartment may name its coordinate system, derivation method, and entitlement state, but MUST NOT place the withheld coordinate value anywhere in the DOM or panel. These status/provenance details are not new meanings, do not expand the catalog driver, do not alter tier entitlement, and add no content generation, persistence, network call, PII transmission, share serialization, or engine input. Interaction minimum: native-equivalent button semantics, Enter/Space activation, Escape/close dismissal with focus restored to the invoking cell, visible focus, and a 44px minimum target. Implementation remains inside `ui/meanings.js`; `content/meanings.v1.js`, `core/`, and `tests/fixtures.json` remain unchanged.

**v0.53 amendment — value meaning in the context of the full sheet (controller correction, STAGED 2026-07-20).** The v0.52 provenance-only treatment for the ten newly interactive coordinates is superseded. Every **resolved** sheet coordinate now opens (a) a value-specific meaning and (b) a separately labeled `in this sheet` synthesis that states the role that meaning plays beside two resolved neighboring coordinates. The synthesis is deterministic and finite: it reads only the fourteen already-rendered coordinate value nodes, maps them through versioned static tables, and combines three registered themes through fixed coordinate roles (`frame`, `outward agenda`, `public instinct`, `long route`, and their sibling roles). It MUST NOT introduce advice, prediction, diagnosis, compatibility scoring, certainty, second-person address, a cross-user comparison, or any claim that one tradition scientifically validates another. `content/meanings.v2.js` re-exports the immutable v1 arcana/sun/animal/number tables, adds five static element meanings, grammatical number-theme aliases, and the fourteen fixed context roles; rising/private-animal/numerology coordinates reuse the matching v1 value tables through their own labeled coordinate role, while a resolved day/hour pillar combines its registered animal and element meanings. Unresolved cells state that no value is available to place in context and name the missing input; sealed cells state only that the absent value's meaning cannot yet be read, preserving sealed-value DOM purity. Catalog exclusion, tier entitlement, engine/calculation inputs, share output, persistence, network behavior, and PII boundaries remain unchanged. This correction intentionally changes the panel's job from “how it is derived” to “what this value means here, in harmony with the others.”

**v0.54 meaning-layer amendment.** `content/meanings.v2.js` exposes `NUMEROLOGY_MEANINGS` as the exact active `1..9` subset of immutable `content/meanings.v1.js`; master-number entries remain only in v1 as historical shipped content and are unreachable from the current calculation and UI. For each of the six numerology coordinates, both preferred partners and fallback partners are other numerology coordinates. Other traditions retain the `in this sheet` cross-coordinate context behavior defined at v0.53.

**v0.62 meaning-layer amendment (calc v4).** The first sentence above is superseded; the rest of the clause stands unchanged. The active registry is `content/meanings.v3.js`, which exposes `NUMEROLOGY_MEANINGS` over the **full twelve-value** terminal domain — no longer a subset of v1 but the whole of its life-path table, because narrowing was the only thing v2 did to it. `content/meanings.v1.js` and `content/meanings.v2.js` are IMMUTABLE and untouched; v3 imports both and adds exactly one field to the three master entries: `theme`, the process noun the `with the other numbers` synthesis needs (`11 → intuition`, `22 → construction`, `33 → guidance`), constrained to be consistent with v1's own bodies. **The master registers and bodies are reused byte-for-byte and pinned as such** — `tests/meanings_content.test.js` asserts identity with v1, not similarity, so a paraphrase in v3 fails CI rather than becoming a second authority for a number's meaning. Every numerology coordinate can now show a two-character value; the §1.B always-space-separated invariant is what makes that safe, and `tests/numerology_display.test.js` drives all six cells at `11 / 22 / 33` to pin it.

**§1.H. T3 written-entry rotation — explicit paid flip (v0.49, controller override, SHIPPED #93).** On a t3 result, an explicit `flip again` advances the written entry through the shipped v1 note positions in strict round-robin order (`low → mid → high → low`) and decrements exactly one paid credit.
