The card's catalog position, name, type, habit, and symbols remain fixed; only the selected `note` position changes. The first visible position is anchored by the existing life-path grouping: life paths 1–3 start at `low`, 4–6 at `mid`, and 7–9 at `high` (pre-calc-v3 lineage: masters 11/22/33 also anchored `high` — retired by the v0.54 anchor amendment below). Free, t1, and t2 flips remain cosmetic and never debit credits; same-profile form submission remains idempotent. The currently visible zero-based position is persisted in `eight_ball_facet_index_v2` (§5; keyed `_v1` until the v0.54 calc-v3 amendment versioned it): a new profile or a consumed pending-profile paid return resets it to the life-path anchor, while reload and same-profile submit preserve it; `try another` preserves it until another profile is submitted, and `forget` clears it. A zero-credit t3 flip clears any stale pending-profile intent and opens the paywall without advancing the position or spending an unseen read; the paid return grants credits without auto-consuming a facet, and only the next explicit flip advances and debits. **Content truth:** this is the c.1 mechanical-reuse path over immutable `content/cards.v1.full.js` `note.low/mid/high` strings. It does not claim those strings were laterally re-authored as outward/inward/returning facets, does not create a v2 deck, and makes no new taxonomy claim. On 2026-07-19 the controller explicitly overrode both the Codex read-only lane restriction and the standing traction/c.2-content prerequisite to authorize this c.1 implementation. Shipped 2026-07-19: Grok + Claude round-1 review returned MERGE WITH FIXES, all absorbs landed, their delta re-audit returned SAFE TO MERGE, and the change was squash-merged to `main` as `0e49773` (#93) after the ordinary release gates.

**v0.54 anchor amendment.** Calc v3 narrows the only valid life-path inputs to `1..9`; therefore the anchor groups are exactly `1–3 → low`, `4–6 → mid`, and `7–9 → high`. `resolveBracket` and `anchorFacetIndex` reject `11/22/33` rather than treating them as active coordinates. A profile whose old raw path passed through a former master now anchors from its reduced single digit. The stored position key is versioned `eight_ball_facet_index_v1` → `eight_ball_facet_index_v2`: the retired v1 value (possibly a former-master `high`) is never read and is cleared once on the first facet read after calc-v3 load. Funded flip history is not representable in the single stored index, so the first v3 load re-anchors every device — accepted cost, named in the journal 2026-07-20 §3 row 8 supersession entry.

**v0.62 anchor amendment (calc v4).** The v0.54 anchor amendment above is superseded and stands as lineage per L17. Calc v4 widens the valid life-path inputs to `1..9, 11, 22, 33`, so the anchor groups are `1–3 → low`, `4–6 → mid`, and `7–9` **plus `11 / 22 / 33` → high** — the pre-calc-v3 contract, restored. `resolveBracket` and `anchorFacetIndex` accept the master values and reject everything else; the two are the SAME partition of the terminal domain and `tests/facet_rotation.test.js` pins their agreement across the whole of it, so widening one and not the other fails CI rather than diverging. **There is no fourth bracket**: a catalog cell has exactly three note positions, so a master must land in one of the three, and `high` is where it landed before v0.54 retired the values.

The stored position key is versioned again, `eight_ball_facet_index_v2` → **`eight_ball_facet_index_v3`** (§5). The reason is the v0.54 reason, running the other way: a position stored under calc v3 was computed against a life path that has since MOVED — a device that read `1970-01-04` stored `mid` for a reduced `4`, and that profile now anchors `high` as a restored `22` — so a v2 value read under calc v4 would render a note slot never computed for the life path on screen. Both retired generations are cleared once on the first v3 facet read, and both stay named in `ui/payments.js` (and on the §5 list) solely for that clear. As at v0.54, flip history is not representable in the single stored index, so the first v4 load re-anchors every device — the same accepted cost, named again rather than assumed.

**v0.55 amendment (ownership — the flip is owned, 2026-07-26).** Credits no longer exist (§1.D / §2 v0.55), so the funded-flip mechanics above are superseded: on a t3 device the explicit `flip again` **always** advances the note position in the same strict round-robin order — nothing is debited, and the zero-credit paywall-opening branch is retired (`nextFacetState` takes only the position; `consumeFacetShake` always advances). t3 is the highest rung; a device that owns it has nothing left to buy from a flip. Free/t1/t2 flips remain cosmetic. Every other §1.H rule holds: anchor by life path, reset on new/consumed-pending profile, preserve on reload and same-profile submit, `forget` clears, position stored in `eight_ball_facet_index_v2`, content truth unchanged (c.1 mechanical reuse of the immutable v1 note slots).

**§1.I. Registry + Concordance — finite post-calculation relations (v0.51, controller override 2026-07-19).** Previous Readings MAY let the user select exactly two valid saved entries and open a separate Concordance screen. Both stored input payloads are recomputed independently through the unchanged `profileFromPayload` → `buildProfile` path; only after both calculated profiles exist does `ui/concordance.js` look up matching axes. The calculation core, catalog driver, bracket resolution, card output, tries, and credits are not inputs to relation lookup and are not mutated; the stored device tier is read once, solely to decide whether the five-element axis is included at all (§1.D entitlement), and never alters any axis's status or output. The compared axes are sun sign × sun sign, public animal × public animal, life path × life path, birth card × birth card, and five-element × five-element only when the device tier entitles the element coordinate.

**Register law.** Every axis emits exactly one textual status from `registered | adjacent | unfiled`, plus the two coordinate values, relation text, named registry, filing citation, and the qualifier `recorded, not certified.` `registered` means a named finite registry contains that relation; `adjacent` means two records may be juxtaposed but no shared registry is claimed; `unfiled` means the checked registry contains no named relation for that pair. The MVP's matching-axis lookups do not fabricate an `adjacent` relation: unsupported or same-value pairs are `unfiled`. The v1 registered inventory — historical record since the v0.54 registry amendment below; `tests/concordance.test.js` now pins the active v2 registry, which reuses every non-numerology table and retires the master-reduction links — was: 66 distinct western sign-distance pairs, 33 named earthly-branch animal pairs, 10 distinct wuxing phase pairs with direction, 3 Pythagorean master-reduction links, and 21 adjacent project-trump sequence pairs. Static data lives in immutable `content/concordance.v1.js`; a changed registry ships as a new versioned file — the active registry is `content/concordance.v2.js`.

**v0.62 registry-currency amendment (2026-08-01).** Two version-specific claims inside the Register law clause above are superseded, and the clause itself is preserved verbatim per L17 rather than edited — including by this amendment, which is the point: the first draft of this correction rewrote that clause in place with inline qualifiers, and an independent pre-merge audit (PR #190) blocked it for violating the very rule the change was written to serve. Quoting fragments of a clause is not preserving it. **(1)** "`tests/concordance.test.js` now pins the active **v2** registry, which reuses every non-numerology table and **retires** the master-reduction links" was accurate for the v0.54-through-v0.61 window only. Calc v4 (§1.B v0.62) restored the master numbers 11/22/33, and the active registry **files** the three master-reduction links rather than retiring them. **(2)** "the active registry is `content/concordance.v2.js`" names the v0.54-through-v0.61 active file; the active registry as of v0.62 is `content/concordance.v3.js`. `content/concordance.v1.js` and `content/concordance.v2.js` both remain immutable historical record per §4 — their continued presence is deliberate, not drift.

**v0.54 registry amendment.** Current runtime imports `content/concordance.v2.js`. It reuses every non-numerology v1 table, changes the life-path source label to the nine-number system, and retires the three master-reduction links from active Concordance. Every distinct or same-value `1..9` life-path pair is `unfiled` absent a separately named finite registry; values outside `1..9` are invalid. The immutable v1 inventory remains historical record.

**v0.62 registry amendment (calc v4).** The v0.54 registry amendment is superseded and stands as lineage per L17. Current runtime imports `content/concordance.v3.js`, which reuses every v1/v2 table unedited and restores two things v2 withdrew: the life-path domain, now the full `1..9, 11, 22, 33`, and **exactly the three master-reduction links** `11↔2`, `22↔4`, `33↔6`. Those three are re-exported from the immutable v1 inventory rather than re-declared, so the active registry cannot acquire a fourth by hand. **Nothing else is filed.** All 66 distinct life-path pairs are checked; 3 are `registered`, 63 are `unfiled`, and no pair is ever `adjacent` — a same-value pair included, since a number does not reduce to itself. No compatibility claim, score, ranking or synthesis is added on any axis: the register law of the base clause is unchanged and this amendment narrows nothing in it. Values outside the twelve remain invalid — widening to admit the masters admits no other double-digit value, which `tests/concordance.test.js` pins against `10`, `12` and `44` by name. The v1 inventory's other counts (66 sign-distance pairs, 33 branch pairs, 10 wuxing pairs, 21 trump-sequence pairs) are untouched, so the v1 inventory is once again the active one in full.

**Anti-oracle boundary.** Concordance is structural citation, not interpersonal interpretation. It MUST NOT emit a compatibility percentage, harmony score, ranking, prediction, advice, diagnosis, soulmate claim, recommendation, or invented synthesis across traditions. Local saved titles may label the two on-screen columns; the screen does not show DOB. No comparison share artifact ships in this MVP. The screen discloses that it recalculates now, makes no compatibility claim, and stores no comparison. §5.F controls retention and failure behavior.

**§1.K. Moon sign — surface coordinate, not a driver (v0.64, operator word ASTRO-MOON-ADD-01).**

The specimen sheet carries a **moon sign** coordinate: the zodiac sign containing the Moon's geocentric ecliptic longitude at the birth instant, computed in `core/moon.js` (`computeMoonSign`) via the Meeus ch.47 longitude series — the same authority family `core/calendar.js` cites for solar longitude (ch.25) and new-moon timing (ch.49), and `core/rising.js` for the ascendant. The implementation carries the full 59-term Table 47.A longitude sum plus the Venus/Jupiter perturbation terms, is geometric rather than apparent (nutation ≈0.005° is three orders of magnitude below a 30° sign boundary — same disclosed-approximation class as calendar.js's JDE-as-JD-UT treatment), and reproduces Meeus's own worked Example 47.a to ~0.001 arcsec (`tests/moon.test.js` pins the book's published 133.162655° directly).

**Surface-only, like every §1.x coordinate.** Moon sign does not enter `getCard`, `resolveBracket`, or any catalog computation; the catalog driver remains `(sunSign, animal)` per §1.

**Input gate — identical to rising's, deliberately.** The Moon moves ~0.5°/hour, so the coordinate needs the birth time resolved to UT; `buildProfile` resolves `moonSign` inside the same validated time/tz block as `risingSign` (direct `opts.tz`, or the §1.A legacy country→IANA derivation), so the two coordinates are available or absent together — which the sheet's row structure assumes. Missing time/place → `undefined` (line falls back exactly as rising does); unresolvable tz → `null`. **The one behavioral divergence:** moon sign has **no polar-latitude guard** — it is the Moon's position in the sky, not a function of the observer's horizon, so a polar-born profile shows `rising — unavailable` beside a normally-resolved moon sign, and `computeMoonSign` takes no lat/lng at all. The math needing no location while the gate requires one is a product-UX choice (coordinate-pair coherence), not an astronomical necessity, and this clause records that asymmetry deliberately.

**Tier placement: t1**, alongside rising — same input requirements, same rung. The §1.D operator-locked table's t1 row is superseded-in-part per L17: t1 now also opens `moon`. The §1.F census base moves **15 → 16** (15 sheet cells + the catalog numeral); the free surface is untouched — moon is not a free coordinate, free stays five VALUES per §1.D v0.38, and the free = DOB-only invariant is unaffected (moon needs birth time + place, which free never collects).

**Deferral reversed on operator word.** Moon sign was explicitly deferred at the 2G-3+ cut (8BALL.md item 10: "held behind v0.3.0; may not return") and scoped out of the pillars build (journal 2026-05-31: "separate core/moon.js, reuses calendar.js Meeus infra"). The reuse claim proved optimistic — calendar.js's ch.25/ch.49 code gives no lunar longitude at an arbitrary instant, so this is a new ch.47 implementation, closer in weight to rising than to a table reuse. Operator word ASTRO-MOON-ADD-01 (2026-08-06, freeze override authorized through merge) reverses the deferral; the product-feature freeze (reach bottleneck) is otherwise unchanged, same override class as §1.G v0.44 / the kua block. **Known residuals, stated rather than found.** (1) The pre-rendered `/cards` JPEGs and the paywall's fixed specimen preview (`spec_no-v.jpg`, alt text "all fifteen coordinates open") show the pre-§1.K 15-coordinate sheet; regenerating 611 catalog assets is separate work this clause does not claim. (2) The moon compartment ships **without a §1.G meanings tap panel** — §1.G's "all sheet compartments are interactive" currency is superseded-in-part to "all but moon": twelve moon-sign citation entries are authored content, which per §4 costs a new versioned content file and its own §1.G amendment, not a rider on a coordinate clause. Until that cycle runs, the moon cell renders value/seal/unres like every compartment but takes no tap.

**§1.L. The specimen sheet is four system lines (v0.66, operator word 2026-08-06).**

The sheet renders **exactly four lines, one per divination system, with no interleaving**: **TAROT** (arcana) · **ASTRO** (sun · rising · moon) · **NUMEROLOGY** (life path · expression · soul urge · personality · birthday · maturity) · **ANIMALS** (five-element · year animal · month animal · day pillar · hour pillar). This supersedes the nine per-coordinate rows the v0.37 compartment sheet shipped; those rows stand as lineage per L17.

**All fifteen compartments survive — only their grouping moves.** The §1.F census is unchanged at 16 (15 cells + the catalog numeral), `CELL_COORD` is untouched, and entitlement stays strictly **per compartment**. The consequence is stated rather than discovered: a line now **mixes tiers** — ANIMALS spans four of them (`animal` free · `element`/`innerAnimal` t1 · `dayPillar` t2 · `hourPillar` t3) — which the §1.D v0.37 per-compartment seal contract already governs. What is new is only how many cells sit under one title.

**Two placements are judgement calls, recorded so they are not mistaken for derivations.** (1) The **five-element** sits on ANIMALS, not on its own line: it is the year-stem element of the same lunar-year derivation the year animal comes from, so it is Chinese-system, not a fourth tradition. (2) The operator's brief named the astro line "Sun + Saturn + Moon"; the product **has no Saturn coordinate** and computing one would be a new ephemeris feature, not a reformat, so the line reads **sun · rising · moon** — rising being the existing third astro coordinate. Both were flagged before the build, neither was silently decided.

**Line titles are STATIC.** The v0.37 paired-row title grammar — which rewrote the sun and animal titles per render, swapping a dot for a relation glyph (`SUN · RISING` → `SUN ↑ RISING`, `PUBLIC · PRIVATE` → `PUBLIC ⇌ PRIVATE`) to mark a resolved entitled pair — is **retired**, and those literals are quoted here as lineage precisely because `tests/tiers.test.js` now source-pins the modules against them. A line spans a whole system rather than a pair, so a pair-relation title has nothing to describe; and the fact the grammar encoded (entitled AND computed) is already carried per compartment as value · `—` · seal. `refs.sunTitle` / `refs.animalTitle` and the `coord-sun-title` / `coord-animal-title` ids retire with it. `ROW_TITLES` moves to `ui/tiers.js` as the single constant the host sheet and every instanced dyad sheet read; `ui/sheet.js` re-exports rather than restating it, which removes the two-file drift the §1.J differential test existed to catch.

**The legend must align 1:1 with its compartments — a correctness rule, not a completeness preference.** The §1.F atlas legend and §1.E provenance placard are read **positionally** against the cells beside them, so a partial map silently misaligns. Before this clause, `ATLAS_NOTE` deliberately omitted the personality/birthday/maturity and day/hour-pillar cells because their own row titles self-named them. The regrouping put those cells on lines beside noted ones, and the ANIMALS legend rendered **three notes against five compartments** — a reader could not tell the year five-element (`water`) from the hour pillar's stem element (`rat · water`), two genuinely different coordinates that collide in value for some profiles. Every one of the fifteen cells now carries an atlas note, keyed in DOM order so the join is cell order by construction. The invariant is pinned generally (one legend entry per cell on every line), not just for the line that exposed it. **Found by reading the rendered card, not by the suite** — the suite was green throughout.

**Layout.** `.coord-cells` wraps. With `nowrap` the six-compartment numerology line's min-content width pushed `#card-face` wider than its `.flip-stage`, and the card ran off a 375px viewport — clipped **silently** by `body { overflow-x: hidden }` rather than scrolling, so a geometry check that asked the document about scroll width reported clean while the rendered card lost its last compartment and the catalog numeral. A system line taking **two visual rows on a phone is accepted** (operator ruling, 2026-08-06); compartments keep the 52px touch target rather than shrinking to force one row.

**§1.M. The numerology line is a hexagon (v0.66, operator word 2026-08-06).**

The six numerology coordinates render at the **six vertices of a hexagon** rather than in a row:

```
   [1][2]        1 life path   2 expression
[3]      [4]     3 soul urge   4 personality
   [5][6]        5 birthday    6 maturity
```

This **restores the v0.2.2 surface** (shipped 2026-05-09 at `fa552ca` as an inline SVG hexagon, six vertices = the same six coordinates) which v0.2.3 reverted the next day. The reversion's own ground is now spent: at v0.2.2 three of the six — personality, birthday, maturity — were additive calc fields with no surface, so the shape had empty vertices; §1.D v0.6.0 surfaced all three at t2, so every vertex now carries a coordinate.

**Built as a grid, not as an SVG polygon**, and that is load-bearing rather than an implementation taste: every vertex stays a real `.coord-cell` carrying its own `.coord-seal`, so the §1.D v0.37 per-compartment seal contract, the sealed-DOM purity rule (a withheld value is absent, not hidden) and the F4 sealed ≠ unresolvable distinction all survive untouched — an SVG polygon would have had to reimplement each of them. **DOM order is 1..6 and reads top-to-bottom, left-to-right across the vertices**, which is what keeps the §1.L legend alignment true and the tab / screen-reader order coherent; the hexagon is a visual arrangement over an unchanged document order. The instanced dyad sheets carry the same geometry (`ui/sheet.js`), because a flat row beside a hexagon would be a real §1.J divergence, not a cosmetic one.

**Known divergence, stated:** the §5.D share PNG builder composes its own layout from the per-cell snapshot and renders the numerology cells in a row, not a hexagon. The artifact's invariants (a)–(d) are untouched — no sealed value, no PII, no per-result link — but card and PNG are no longer geometrically identical. Reconciling them is separate work this clause does not claim.





## §2. What this is NOT

**Currently enforced (CI gates, see §7):**

- Not a "spiritual" product. The voice is declarative-observational and materialistic, not mystical or guidance-oriented. Banned-voice-register scan (`BANNED_VOICE_REGISTER` in `tests/profile.test.js`) ran on `cards.v1.js` content in v0.1.x. As of v0.3.0 (v0.22 amendment to §4 + §7 stage 1 extension) the scan runs against the tracked deck at `content/cards.v1.full.js` in CI; the private authoring pipeline at `~/dev/8ball-private/` continues to pre-scan before each release-prep `cp` to the public path.
- Not a SIRR product. Never references SIRR. Never imports from SIRR. The two repos are siblings, not parent/child. (See §9.) Enforced by `tests/pii_scan.test.js`.
- Not an analytics product. No tracking, no telemetry, no third-party scripts. (First-party server-side request logs — the host's own aggregate traffic analytics — are not "telemetry" under this rule: the product injects no client, makes no call, emits no beacon. See §5.) Network behavior is scope-restricted per §5: only same-origin lazy loads of static assets (e.g. `assets/cities.json`) and §5.B user-initiated calls (Netlify Forms feedback POST + Gumroad Buy Link redirect) are permitted. `fetch()`, `XMLHttpRequest`, and `navigator.sendBeacon` remain forbidden in tracked source. Enforced by `tests/privacy_scan.test.js`. The page uses system fonts only — no Google Fonts, no third-party CSS.

**Review-discipline (no current code surface to scan; rule fires when a feature is proposed):**

- Not a horoscope app. Not a numerology calculator. Not a guidance product. (Voice register; partly captured by the banned-list above.)
- Not a logged-in product. No accounts, no signup, no "save your reading."

**v0.50 amendment (Saved Readings MVP, controller override 2026-07-19).** The final phrase above is superseded only for a browser-local archive of completed readings. 8ball remains not logged-in: no account, signup, identity layer, email capture, cloud sync, remote database, or third-party storage is introduced. A reading is saved only after the user presses the explicit `save reading` action, stays in that browser's `localStorage`, and can be reopened, renamed, deleted individually, or cleared in full. §5.E is the controlling scope; anything beyond it remains prohibited by the original line and §12's permanent account-system ban.

**v0.51 amendment (Registry + Concordance MVP, controller override 2026-07-19).** Comparing two browser-local saved readings does not turn 8ball into a compatibility, guidance, social-profile, or logged-in product. The §1.I register law and §5.F transient-retention boundary are controlling; the §2 anti-oracle, no-account, no-analytics, and no-remote-storage rules remain literal.
- **Honest invitation permitted; manipulation forbidden (v0.35).** 8ball may be shared, posted, linked, and discussed by the operator as a person, and may carry a soft contextual invitation (a plain link, a "here's the thing"). Forbidden is manipulative/coercive distribution: dark patterns, engagement-bait, false urgency, undisclosed funneling, spam, extractive growth-hacking. Participating as a human in a community is not a funnel. The anti-funnel value protects against manipulation, not against being seen. This clause is doctrine; prior "not a funnel / no CTA / no link in bio" language that lived only in operating/session plans (never in this file) is superseded by it.
- **Arcade-toy carve-out (v0.22, v0.3.0).** Paid surface is one-time purchase, fixed-credit. Three dollars buys three reads with depth content unlocked. Tier ladders (v0.3.1+: $6 / 6 tries at depth+hexagon; $9 / 9 tries at depth+hexagon+four) extend the model without changing its shape. Not a subscription. Device-stored credit counter preserves the no-account constraint. The arcade frame is load-bearing — any future amendment that breaks `dollars == tries` or introduces recurring billing requires explicit re-amendment.
- **Arcade-toy carve-out re-amendment (v0.36, v0.6.0).** This is the explicit re-amendment the v0.22 clause requires. The shipped tier ladder (§1.D) is $3 / $6 / $9 rungs, each a one-time purchase granting **+3 reads at that rung's coordinate density** — `dollars == tries` is superseded: every rung buys exactly three reads; dollars buy density, not try count. The v0.22 contemplated ladder ($6 / 6 tries; $9 / 9 tries) is preserved above as lineage and never shipped. Everything else holds unchanged: one-time purchase, fixed-credit, no subscription, no recurring billing, device-stored counter, no account.
- **Arcade-toy carve-out re-amendment (v0.55, ownership, 2026-07-26).** Second exercise of the v0.22 explicit-re-amendment requirement. The rungs reprice to **$1 / $2 / $3** and `fixed-credit` is superseded entirely: a rung is a one-time purchase of **permanent, unlimited** density at that rung — no read counter, no cap, no per-read debit; the free surface is likewise open (unlimited readings at free density; the three-free-tries demo is retired per §4.B v0.55). The load-bearing frame survives intact: one-time purchase, no subscription, no recurring billing, no account; the device-stored state shrinks to the monotonic tier flag (`eight_ball_tier_v1`) plus the read-only legacy-credit grandfather signal (§1.D R2). Any future amendment that introduces recurring billing or claws back a purchased rung still requires explicit re-amendment here.

## §3. The calculation contract

`core/profile.js` is the calculation core. Changes fall into two categories:

**Breaking changes** — modify existing outputs (sunSign, animal, lifePath, nameNumber, chineseElement, soulUrge for any input). The contract:

1. Add or update fixtures in `tests/fixtures.json` reflecting the intended new behavior.
2. Update the algorithm.
3. Run `npm test`. All cases must pass.
4. Bump the calc-version note at the bottom of this file.

**Additive changes** — add new outputs without modifying existing ones (e.g. new functions exported, new fields on the `buildProfile` return value). The contract:

1. Add direct unit tests for the new outputs in `tests/profile.test.js`.
2. Run `npm test`. All cases must pass, including the new tests.
3. Existing `tests/fixtures.json` cases must remain byte-identical (no silent drift).
4. Calc-version stays the same major (additive doesn't break v1 consumers).

Algorithm versions documented:

- **calc v3.1** (v0.57, 2026-07-29) — Lunar-year and solar-term cusps are resolved in the civil time of their own era: Beijing local mean time (UTC+7:45:40) before China Standard Time was adopted in 1929, UTC+8 from 1929 on. calc v2 evaluated every year at a flat UTC+8, which files a new moon or solar term landing in the first ~14 minutes after midnight one day late. Across the 1900–2100 table range that is four dates: lunar new year **1916 (Feb 4 → Feb 3)**, and three solar terms — 1911 lixia, 1912 xiaohan, 1927 bailu — each moving one day earlier. Only pre-1929 births are affected, and the visible defect was a 1916-02-03 birth filed under the previous year's animal (rabbit) instead of dragon. ~~Numerology, sun sign, rising sign, birth card, and the catalog index are untouched~~ **— erratum, 2026-07-29 P1-D: FALSE, struck per L17 rather than silently rewritten. The catalog index is `(sunSign, animal)`-driven (§1), and `animal` there is the public year-pillar animal — the exact coordinate this entry says moved. A 1916-02-03 birth's catalog index necessarily moves with it: Aquarius+rabbit was `cxxiv`, Aquarius+dragon (the corrected animal) is `cxxv`, verified against shipped `core/engine.js`. Numerology, sun sign, rising sign, and birth card genuinely are untouched — none of those inputs depend on the year-pillar animal — but the catalog index is not among them. This was a blast-radius/truth defect in the record, not a code defect: the shipped catalog computation was already correct; only this claim about it was wrong.** no stored reading needs migration, because only reconstruction inputs persist. Verified against three independent implementations — sxtwl (寿星万年历, astronomical) and the table-based lunardate and borax — which agree with each other on every year 1900–2050 and, after this change, with `core/calendar.js` on every year 1900–2100. **The Hong Kong Observatory tables named under calc v2 could not be reached from the implementing environment (egress policy blocked `hko.gov.hk`); the three-library consensus stands in for them and should be re-confirmed against HKO when an operator can reach it — done, 2026-07-29 (P1-D, journal-recorded): ~~the consensus disagreed with HKO at eight further boundaries outside this entry's four-date blast radius (1911 lixia, 1912 hanlu, 1912 xiaohan, 2014 jingzhe, 2016 xiaoshu, 2045 xiaoshu, 2047 jingzhe, 2097 lixia — see journal.md). All eight sit within the documented ~15-minute error budget of the low-accuracy solar-longitude formula, right at local midnight; `core/calendar.js` now carries a small, sourced correction table (`HKO_SOLAR_TERM_CORRECTIONS`) for exactly those eight, verified against the full 1901–2100 HKO index at zero remaining mismatches (2,400 solar + 200 lunar comparisons).~~ **— erratum, 2026-07-30 (Codex pre-merge audit, finding F): the struck sentence is wrong in three separate ways and is superseded per L17 rather than edited in place. (1) BLAST RADIUS — "outside this entry's four-date blast radius" is FALSE. Two of the eight, **1911 lixia and 1912 xiaohan**, are named in this entry's own four-date list two sentences above; they are inside it. The correction table restores exactly the HKO values this entry had moved away from for those two boundaries (1911 lixia back to May 7, 1912 xiaohan back to Jan 7), so for them calc v3.1's pre-1929 offset was a regression this table reverses, not a further discovery beyond it. The genuinely new boundaries are the other **six**. (2) ATTRIBUTION — "the consensus disagreed with HKO" is unsupported. The cited evidence file (`audits/calc_v3_1_pre1929_offset_evidence_2026-07-29.md`) uses sxtwl, lunardate and borax as **lunar-new-year** oracles only; its whole comparison table is lunar-new-year dates, and no artifact on file shows lunardate or borax evaluated at a solar-term boundary at all. Nothing establishes what those libraries say about these eight. The disagreement on record is between `core/calendar.js` and HKO — that and no more. (3) CAUSE — "all eight sit within the documented ~15-minute error budget of the low-accuracy solar-longitude formula" states a diagnosis the evidence does not reach. An error bound is not a deterministic threshold: a closer approximation can agree with the authority by luck while a farther one fails, so proximity to the bound is consistent with the claim but does not demonstrate it, and no independent high-precision ephemeris artifact exists to separate formula error from omitted time corrections. The eight are **authority-pinned residual corrections** — correct against HKO, cause not established. SCOPE, corrected in the same breath: the comparison covers **all 12 month-starting (jié) terms plus the lunar-new-year date, for every year 1901–2100** — 2,400 solar and 200 lunar comparisons at zero mismatches — NOT the "full HKO index." HKO publishes 12 major and 12 minor terms; only the 12 month-starting ones are compared here, and the same solver also drives major-term/leap-month logic that carries no direct coverage. What is unchanged by this erratum: the eight override values themselves, which are verified byte-exact against the 200 official HKO source files (`audits/fixtures/hko_calendar_authority_1901_2100.json`, all 200 SHA-256s independently re-derived 2026-07-30).**
- **calc v4** (v0.62, STAGED 2026-07-31) — Master-preserving Pythagorean numerology. All six resolved coordinate outputs reduce by repeated digit sum **while above 9, stopping at `11`, `22` or `33`**, so the terminal domain is exactly `1..9, 11, 22, 33` (`29 → 11`, `38 → 11`, `44 → 8`). Maturity is unchanged in method — reduce each component, add the canonical components, reduce again under the same rule — and can therefore land on a master itself. This is a breaking calculation change in `core/profile.js`, coordinated with `tests/fixtures.json`, direct unit tests, tier rendering, bracket/facet anchors, the v3 active meaning registry, the v3 Concordance registry, the v2 dyad tables, and the v3 public tables with their master-mode bridge. Calc v3's `null`-not-zero guard for an empty contributing letter subset is RETAINED unchanged; only the terminal domain moves. Lunar-year, solar-term, sun-sign, rising-sign, pillar, birth-card and catalog-index algorithms are untouched, and the tarot birth-card reduction (digit sum floored at ≤ 22, `22 → 0 · the fool`, §1.C) is a separate contract this does not touch. Persisted reading schemas need no migration because they store reconstruction inputs, not derived coordinates; reopen/reload recomputes under calc v4. The one persisted thing that DOES move is the t3 facet position, which is a derived anchor rather than an input — see §1.H v0.62 and §5.
- **calc v3** (v0.54 through v0.61) — All six resolved Pythagorean coordinate outputs reduce by repeated digit sum to exactly `1..9`; 11/22/33 are no longer terminal values, and an empty contributing letter subset is unresolved rather than zero. This is a breaking calculation change in `core/profile.js`, coordinated with `tests/fixtures.json`, direct unit tests, tier rendering, bracket/facet anchors, the v2 active meaning view, and the v2 Concordance registry. Lunar-year, solar-term, sun-sign, rising-sign, pillar, birth-card, and catalog-index algorithms are unchanged. Persisted reading schemas need no migration because they store reconstruction inputs, not derived coordinates; reopen/reload recomputes under calc v3.
- **calc v2** (v0.2.7.1 through v0.53) — Real lunar new year table for year-pillar cusps (year-pillar animal + 5-element pillar). Real solar-term table for month-pillar (`innerAnimal`). Both tables span 1900–2100, evaluated at date-precision in canonical Asia/Shanghai timezone (UTC+8); DOB given as `YYYY-MM-DD` is treated as the truth — no timezone conversion of user input. Lunar new year dates and the 12 month-start solar-term dates are computed in JS via Meeus astronomical algorithms (Chapter 25 sun position, Chapter 49 lunar phases with periodic + planetary corrections), mirroring the `core/rising.js` Meeus pattern; results match Hong Kong Observatory canonical tables byte-exact at the sanity-lock checkpoints in `tests/profile.test.js`. Zero runtime deps, zero devDeps, zero build step. The v1 Feb-4 fixed-cutoff approximation is retired. **`getInnerAnimal` signature changed from `(month, day)` to `(year, month, day)`** as a breaking calc change. All existing fixtures other than the v1-bug-locking fixture #6 (CNY Feb 4 cutoff) remain byte-identical; fixture #6 is intentionally re-spec'd because it was locking the bug being fixed.
- **calc v1** (initial through v0.2.7) — Pythagorean life path with master numbers 11/22/33 preserved at the final reduce. Western tropical sun signs at standard cusps. Chinese zodiac with Feb 4 cutoff approximation (retired in calc v2). Additively extended at v0.2.0 with: chinese five-element (2-year cycle, 1924 anchor), public/private animal split (year-pillar already present at `animal`; month-pillar added as `innerAnimal` via solar-term cutoff approximations), soul urge (vowel-only Pythagorean, masters preserved), and unreduced sums (lifePathSum, nameNumberSum, soulUrgeSum) exported for potential future surfacing. Additively extended at v0.2.1 with optional rising sign (surface-only ascendant) when birth time + country + lat/lng are present. All additions covered by direct unit tests in `tests/profile.test.js` and `tests/rising.test.js`; existing fixtures unchanged.

If a fixture changes silently, the test gate catches it. If a test changes silently, code review catches it. If both change in the same commit without a doctrine note, the reviewer rejects.

## §4. Content rules — copy & traits

- **No slurs.** Banned-pattern check runs in `tests/profile.test.js`.
- **No medical/diagnostic framing.** Cards do not adopt clinical or diagnostic vocabulary, including ironic adoption (e.g., "diagnosis," "syndrome," "disorder").
- **Cultural-symbol respect.** If the deck draws from any tradition (tarot, I Ching, runes, zodiac, etc.), cards respect that tradition's lineage. No syncretic flattening, no caricature of source traditions.
- **No targeting minors.** Copy assumes adult user. UI does not pander to children. **v0.48 amendment:** the v0.2.6 18+ acknowledgment gate (§4.A) is retired — see §4.A for the historical mechanics and the retirement rationale. This bullet's substance (adult-assuming copy, no pandering) stands on its own without a mechanical gate.
- **No real-person targets.** No "you're like [public figure]" lines.
- **Universal floor.** Cards should land equally on a person who picked their own DOB.
- **No card-content strings in tracked files.** As of v0.2.0 the card content (cell `name`, `type`, `habit`, `note` bodies) lives privately at `~/dev/8ball-private/cards.v1.full.js`. No public-repo tracked file (source, tests, fixtures, `journal.md`, audit notes, this doctrine) may contain a card-content string. Audit dispositions and content-batch logs reference cells by coordinate path (e.g. `aries.rabbit.note.mid`); before/after strings are stored with the private deck. Forward-looking from doctrine v0.11; pre-v0.11 trait-pool / template-pool excerpts in journal entries (retired `traits.v1.js` / `templates.v1.js` system, deleted from repo in 2F-2) are out of scope. **v0.22 amendment (v0.3.0):** the rule is scoped, not retired — exactly one tracked file, `content/cards.v1.full.js`, is permitted to contain card-content strings (the 144-card content layer: `name`, `type`, `habit`, `note` × `{low, mid, high}` brackets). All other tracked files remain prohibited, including source modules in `core/`, all `tests/`, `tests/fixtures.json`, `journal.md`, audit notes under `audits/`, `DOCTRINE.md`, `8BALL.md`, `README.md`. Audit dispositions continue to reference cells by coordinate path; the carve-out covers the deck file alone, not its discussion in tracked text. Voice-register enforcement (`BANNED_VOICE_REGISTER` scan in `tests/profile.test.js`) now runs against `content/cards.v1.full.js`. The private location at `~/dev/8ball-private/cards.v1.full.js` is retained as the authoring source; `content/cards.v1.full.js` is a verbatim copy maintained via `cp` at release-prep time.
- **Versioned, not edited.** Shipped content batches are immutable. New release = new file (e.g. `traits.v2.js`, `cards.v2.js`). Diff lives in `journal.md`.

**§4.A — 18+ acknowledgment gate. RETIRED v0.48 (2026-07-06).**

**Retirement rationale.** The gate was a v0.2.6 operator call ("kids shouldn't use it"), not an externally-mandated requirement — no app-store or payment-processor rule required it at the time. It was later cited as one of several disclosures in a pre-emptive Lemon Squeezy KYC response document, but that payment path was itself retired in favor of Gumroad by v0.3.0.3, so that citation no longer applies to anything live. Operator judgment on reflection: the modal's copy ("for adults" / "8 ball is a toy for adults") read as adult-content-warning theatre — exactly the framing §4.A's own mechanics clause said to avoid — rather than the intended clinical, self-attestation tone. Retired rather than reworded: removes the modal, the `isAgeAcknowledged` read, the `eight_ball_age_ack_v1` localStorage flag, and the about-modal's disclosure line. The underlying content posture (copy assumes an adult user, no pandering to minors) is unchanged — see the §4 "no targeting minors" bullet above, which no longer depends on a mechanical gate to hold.

Mechanics below preserved verbatim as history per L17 (supersede, don't edit) — this is what shipped from v0.2.6 through v0.47:

- On first load, a modal blocks the onboarding form. Single button: "confirm." Tapping it is the acknowledgment.
- Acknowledgment persists via `eight_ball_age_ack_v1` localStorage flag (string `'true'` on confirm). On subsequent visits the gate is skipped.
- Acknowledgment is a click-through, not verification. Copy must be explicit about that — no implication of legal age-gating, no health-product warning theatre, no false claim of identity verification.
- Disclosure: the about-modal mentions the gate's existence and shape so the user knows what the storage flag is for.

If a future feature needs age verification (paid tier, adult-content layer, regulatory requirement), that requires a new §4.A amendment and likely a §5 amendment for whatever data-collection surface the verification introduces — this retirement does not preclude reintroducing a gate, mechanically or in spirit, if a real need surfaces.

**§4.B — Three-free-tries cap (v0.22, v0.3.0).**

Three free tries total per device. First three unique `(name, dob)` pairs render the locked card (symbols visible, depth bars dimmed, lock icon top-left — Pattern C surface per `~/Desktop/8ball/sessions/v03_pattern_c_locked_vs_unlocked.html`). The fourth new pair triggers the paywall modal. The cap is monotonic and per-device — wiping localStorage resets it. Paying does not reset the cap; paying adds paid credits separately (see §5 `eight_ball_credits_v1`). The about-modal discloses the cap exists ("first three readings are free. after that, three dollars buys three more reads…"). The localStorage-wipe reset is intentionally not advertised in user-facing copy — disclosing the cap shape is the load-bearing disclosure; advertising the bypass is not.

The cap exists to admit the offer plainly: this is a paid toy, not an ad-supported product, not a free-trial-with-aggressive-upsell. Three free tries is the demo.

**v0.36 amendment (v0.6.0):** the paywall is a three-rung ladder (t1 · $3 / t2 · $6 / t3 · $9 per §1.D) — three plain `<a href>` CTAs to the three Gumroad products (§5.B Call 2 v0.36). The three-free-tries cap is unchanged; every render — free tries included — shows the device's entitled density per the §1.D density rule (a device with no tier and no credits renders the free card). t3 carries the card-content unlock — the §1 v0.22 paid card-content layer relocates to t3. Paying still does not reset the cap; every rung adds +3 paid credits (`eight_ball_credits_v1`) and raises the stored tier monotonically (`eight_ball_tier_v1`, §5).

**v0.55 amendment (open free surface, 2026-07-26):** the three-free-tries cap is **retired** — the base clause above and the cap language in the v0.36 amendment are superseded. Every render on a free device returns the free card: unlimited readings, unlimited profiles, no counter, no fourth-pair paywall trigger (Path A retired). The demo IS the product's free surface now; what the paywall sells is depth, priced t1 · $1 / t2 · $2 / t3 · $3 (§1.D / §2 v0.55), permanent on purchase. The paywall modal survives as the rung-purchase surface, reached from the lock-tap on the rendered sheet (Path B — §6.7): it stages the stored profile as pending and opens the ladder. The about-modal cap disclosure is superseded by open-surface copy ("readings are free and unlimited, on the free sheet"); disclosure of what a rung buys, that it is permanent, and the Gumroad boundary remains load-bearing. `eight_ball_tries_used_v1` is retired with the cap (§5 v0.55).

**v0.56 amendment (14-day single-offer sprint presentation, 2026-07-26 through 2026-08-08 inclusive):** for the sales sprint the buyer-facing paywall presents **one purchase choice** — the existing t3 product (`https://theeightball.gumroad.com/l/xjpvp`, `?paid=t3`) as `complete 8ball · $3 once`: every coordinate and the written card, permanently, for every reading on this device. The v0.36/v0.55 three-CTA ladder presentation is superseded **for the presentation only and for the sprint window only**: the t1/t2 products stay live on Gumroad, their `?paid=t1|t2` returns are honored unchanged, the stored tier stays monotonic, the §1.D R2 legacy grandfather survives, and every existing t1/t2 buyer retains exactly the access they own — `core/payments.js`, `ui/payments.js`, and `ui/tiers.js` are byte-untouched by this amendment. Three presentation surfaces are added, none a new flow: **(a)** the paywall MAY carry one compact **specimen preview** — the fixed catalog cell no. v (aries × dragon): its complete-sheet image from the already-public `/cards` hosted set plus its written entry filled at boot from the public deck bundle (`content/cards.v1.full.js` — the §5.C source-visible posture; the §4 carve-out file stays the only tracked home of card-content strings). The preview is labeled a fixed example, never the visitor's result; the visitor's own written entry remains t3-gated per §1.D / §5.C, and the §1.D v0.37 sealed-DOM purity of the visitor's sheet is unchanged. **(b)** A labeled result-rail **offer control** presents the same single Path B trigger (stage pending profile → open paywall) as the lock icon, visible below t3 and absent at t3 — the lock icon's own visibility rule; the §1.F density census stays price-free. **(c)** The cold first screen's mechanism strip and the head metadata read plain-English (`name + birth data in. one fixed identity sheet out`; descriptive page name `specimen registry`) — still §2 clinical, no predictive/therapeutic/guidance claim, pinned by `tests/reach_surface.test.js`. Sibling host-config change: `netlify.toml` adds five exact-match, non-forced 200 rewrites (`/r` `/x` `/ig` `/tt` `/pin` → the app) so channel attribution reads from the host's own first-party server logs per §5 v0.35 — no client event, cookie, pixel, query parameter, or third-party script; deterministically validated by `tests/channel_routes.test.js`. **Expiry:** the single-offer presentation lapses after 2026-08-08 unless the operator explicitly renews it; reverting is the mechanical restoration of the three-CTA ladder plus test re-pins, with no entitlement migration — nothing in the ownership model moved.

**v0.61 amendment (the dyad rung does not touch the sprint presentation, 2026-07-30).** §1.D v0.61 appends a fourth rung, `t5` · $6. The v0.56 single-offer presentation above is **unchanged by it and is not superseded**: the paywall still presents exactly one purchase choice (the t3 product at $3), its headline, specimen preview and result-rail offer control are byte-identical, `index.html` still contains exactly one Gumroad URL, and `core/payments.js` / `ui/payments.js` / `ui/tiers.js` carry no new CTA. The dyad's own control is a labeled entry to a screen, not an offer — it carries no price and no urgency, and while `T5_PRODUCT_URL` is empty the rung's CTA has no `href` and stays hidden. The sprint expiry (2026-08-08) and the mechanical restoration it describes are unaffected; when the ladder presentation is restored, whether `t5` joins it is a separate controller decision, not a consequence of this amendment.

**v0.62 commercial-truth amendment (verified 2026-07-31; final-worktree audit closure 2026-08-01).** This supersedes the current-state storefront claims in §1.D v0.55/v0.61 and §4.B v0.56/v0.61; their entitlement and historical-price text remains lineage per L17. The product's entitlement ladder is still `t1 / t2 / t3 / t5`, stored tiers and valid paid-return handling remain monotonic, and every existing t1/t2 owner keeps exactly what the device owns. The live storefront is a different and narrower fact: authenticated inspection found `xjpvp` (**8 ball — private**, $3) published and wired as the only buyer-facing offer; `neysyv` (**8 ball — comparative**, $6) exists but is unpublished and not for sale; `rzqezp` (**8 ball — public**, $9) exists but is unpublished and not for sale. Therefore t1/t2 are not currently separate $1/$2 storefront offers, the t5 product does **exist** as the unpublished comparative listing, and the retired public listing remains residue rather than a live t4 rung. `T5_PRODUCT_URL` stays empty and no comparative checkout or fulfillment path is live; the runtime still fails closed. The sprint's 2026-08-08 expiry cannot mechanically restore the old three-CTA ladder: any later storefront expansion requires a fresh controller decision, operator-hand listing publication where applicable, coherent product wiring and tests, and the ordinary release gates. No Gumroad or account mutation was required or performed for this truth repair.

**v0.69 amendment (the offer says both halves of what it sells, 2026-08-06).** §1.D v0.68 folded the comparative into t3 but changed no buyer-facing string, so for one cycle the $3 offer sold a second person's complete sheet and never mentioned it. This amendment aligns the presentation with the contents. **Copy only — no new flow, no new control, no new CTA, no price change, no entitlement change**; `core/payments.js` and `ui/tiers.js` are byte-untouched. Four strings move: the paywall title (`complete sheet + compare two people · $3 once`), the paywall body (restructured to name the two halves rather than run six items together with the comparative last), the CTA (`buy complete — every coordinate, the written card, and compare two people — $3`), and the result-rail offer control (`complete sheet + compare two people · $3 once`). One label moves with them: the §1.J entry control on the result rail reads `compare with a second person` instead of `read beside another sheet` — it names the purchase in the purchase's own words, and it remains an entry to an owned surface, carrying no price and no urgency (pinned as such). The specimen preview, the disclosure line, the facts line and the about-modal are unchanged.

**The lower rungs stay secondary, and nothing here was needed to keep them that way.** They are secondary twice over already: the §4.B v0.56 single-offer presentation means the paywall carries exactly one purchase choice — `tests/payments_markup.test.js` pins the modal against `paywall-cta-t1|paywall-cta-t2` and against the `rzqezp|neysyv` URLs, and `tests/public_surface.test.js` pins `index.html` to exactly one Gumroad URL — and the v0.62 commercial truth above is that neither is a live storefront offer at all: both listings are unpublished and not for sale. **Adding a $1/$2 CTA would therefore have been a regression on two counts** (it would supersede v0.56 mid-sprint and it would point at listings nobody can buy), so it was not done. Every existing t1/t2 owner keeps exactly the access they hold; `?paid=t1|t2` returns are honored unchanged; the stored tier stays monotonic.

**Storefront gap, restated because this amendment narrows it without closing it.** §1.D v0.68 recorded that the listing does not name the comparative. The app now does, which makes the two disagree in the other direction: the product page still describes the single sheet. The copy for closing that is drafted, operator-ready and unapplied at `audits/gumroad_listing_copy_2026-08-06.md`. **No agent may mutate the store (§10);** the draft exists so applying it is a paste, not a drafting session. The sprint expiry (2026-08-08) and the restoration terms in v0.62 are unaffected by this amendment.

If a line lands but you can't tell whether it crosses any of the above, it crosses. Cut it.

**Safety-patch carve-out.** Locked-decision #9 (immutable v1 pools) protects against silent flavor drift. It does NOT protect a doctrine-rule violation caught post-ship. If a shipped trait, template, or card line violates §4, it is cut in a patch release; the journal records the cut and the diff carries the doctrine note. Immutability is for taste discipline, not error preservation.

## §5. Privacy primitive

The product persists only user-entered profile fields, in `localStorage` only, on the user's own device:

- `eight_ball_profile_v1` — JSON-stringified profile object containing the user-entered fields below. Persists the profile payload across loads; written by the onboarding submit handler and by `applyPaidReturn()` when consuming `eight_ball_pending_profile_v1` on a paid round-trip.
- `name` (string)
- `dob` (ISO date YYYY-MM-DD)
- optional `time` (HH:MM string, 24-hour)
- optional `country` (country/zone entry code from `core/countries.js`)
- optional `lat` (decimal latitude)
- optional `lng` (decimal longitude)
- optional `city` (city display name from `assets/cities.json` — user's birthplace selection. Added v0.2.7.2.)
- optional `cc` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 country code, paired with `city` for display rehydration. Added v0.2.7.2.)
- optional `tz` (IANA timezone string — drives DST-aware rising-sign computation per §1.A v0.21. Added v0.2.7.2.)
- optional `gender` (`'male' | 'female'`, strict — any other value is dropped at the write seam, never stored. Feeds only the §1.D v0.63 kua block; optional forever, never required by any surface. Added v0.63.)
- `eight_ball_labels_revealed_v1` — separate string flag (`'true'` or `'false'`) tracking whether the user has chosen to reveal the symbol-name labels on the result card. UI preference only; independent of the profile payload. Added v0.2.7.
- `eight_ball_tries_used_v1` — integer (serialized as string). Monotonic counter incrementing on every new-pair shake regardless of paid state. Read against the constant `3` for the §4.B cap check. Added v0.3.0.
- `eight_ball_credits_v1` — integer (serialized as string). `+= 3` on each `?paid=t1` handler invocation; decrements by 1 on each new-pair shake when `> 0`. Hits zero → next new-pair triggers paywall or locked render depending on `tries_used`. Non-negative invariant. Added v0.3.0.
- `eight_ball_pending_profile_v1` — JSON-stringified profile object, transient. Written when the paywall modal opens (both Path A new-pair shake and Path B lock-tap on already-rendered card per §5.C). Consumed on the `?paid=t1` return: one credit decremented, profile saved to `eight_ball_profile_v1`, key deleted. Lifetime is one round-trip; either absent or present-and-valid. Added v0.3.0.
- `eight_ball_tier_v1` — string tier flag (`'t1' | 't2' | 't3'`), the highest rung purchased per §1.D; absent = free. Monotonic — written only by the `?paid=t1|t2|t3` return handler as max(current, purchased) by ladder rank, plus the one-time §1.D legacy-grandfather write (credits with no tier key persist as `'t3'` on first render); never downgrades. UI preference-free, profile-free: it stores which density the device has bought, nothing about the person. Added v0.6.0 (§1.D / §4.B v0.36).
- `eight_ball_facet_index_v2` — **RETIRED by §1.H v0.62 (calc v4); superseded by the `_v3` bullet below and preserved verbatim as lineage per L17. Never read, never written; named in `ui/payments.js` and here solely for the one-shot clear.** integer string (`'0' | '1' | '2'`) naming the currently visible v1 note position for the t3 written entry, never the next position. Profile-free and PII-free. New profile and consumed pending-profile return reset it to the life-path anchor; reload and same-profile submission preserve it; `forget` clears it. It changes only alongside a funded explicit t3 `flip again`; a zero-credit paywall opening leaves it unchanged. Added v0.49 as `eight_ball_facet_index_v1` (§1.H); versioned to `_v2` by the v0.54 calc-v3 amendment — the retired `_v1` key is never read, is cleared once on the first facet read after calc-v3 load, and stays named in code (and on this list) only for that clear.
- `eight_ball_facet_index_v3` — **the active facet key as of §1.H v0.62 (calc v4).** Same shape, same semantics and same reset/preserve/clear rules as the `_v2` bullet above; only the name moves. The two retired generations `_v1` and `_v2` are never read and never written — both are cleared once on the first facet read after calc-v4 load — and they stay named in `ui/payments.js` and on this list solely for that clear (the v0.48 retired-key precedent, exercised twice now). Versioning rather than migrating is the point: the single stored index cannot represent how a device arrived at a position, so a value stored against a life path that calc v4 has moved (`1970-01-04`: `4 → 22`, `mid → high`) has nothing to migrate INTO. **No other key is added, read or written by this amendment.**
- `eight_ball_saved_readings_v1` — JSON array for the user-initiated Saved Readings archive. Each entry contains local metadata (`id`, user-editable `title`, ISO `savedAt`) plus a `profile` object restricted to the same user-entered reconstruction fields allowed for `eight_ball_profile_v1` (`name`, `dob`, optional `time`/`city`/`cc`/`tz`/`country`/`lat`/`lng`). No derived coordinate, catalog result, tier, credit, card prose, or payment data is stored. Added v0.50 (§5.E).

**v0.36 amendment (v0.6.0, allow-list semantics).** The `?paid=t1` references in the `eight_ball_credits_v1` and `eight_ball_pending_profile_v1` bullets above are preserved verbatim from their v0.3.0 wording per L17. As of v0.6.0 the same semantics apply to every `?paid=t1|t2|t3` return: +3 credits on any rung, identical pending-profile round-trip consumption (§1.D / §4.B / §5.B Call 2 v0.36). No other change to either key's behavior.

**v0.49 amendment (§1.H).** The §1.D "Credits unchanged" / β-idempotence language and the v0.3.0/v0.36 bullets above remain lineage for the form-submit read state machine: same-pair form submission is still credit-preserving. The t3 result-screen `flip again` is a separate state machine and now decrements `eight_ball_credits_v1` by 1 on each funded advance. A zero-credit t3 top-up clears `eight_ball_pending_profile_v1` before opening the paywall so an abandoned Path A/B intent cannot auto-consume on return. No other change to either key's behavior.

**v0.55 amendment (ownership — counters retired, 2026-07-26).** The counter semantics in the `eight_ball_tries_used_v1`, `eight_ball_credits_v1`, `eight_ball_pending_profile_v1`, `eight_ball_tier_v1`, and `eight_ball_facet_index_v2` bullets above (and their v0.36/v0.49 amendments) are superseded as follows. `eight_ball_tries_used_v1` is **retired** — never read, never written; the code names no such key and the scanner allow-list drops it (the v0.48 retired-key precedent; stale values on old devices are inert integers). `eight_ball_credits_v1` is **frozen as a read-only legacy signal**: never granted, never decremented; its sole consumer is the §1.D R2 grandfather read at render time, and once that persists the tier the value is ignored forever. `eight_ball_pending_profile_v1` keeps its one-round-trip lifetime but is written only by the Path B lock-tap (Path A is retired, §4.B v0.55) and its consumption no longer decrements anything. `eight_ball_tier_v1` is unchanged in shape and remains monotonic — it is now the entire purchase record. `eight_ball_facet_index_v2` advances on any explicit t3 flip (no funding requirement, §1.H v0.55); reset/preserve/clear rules unchanged.

Nothing else. No derived profile is stored — it's recomputed on each load. No analytics. No third-party endpoints. No third-party scripts. System fonts only.

**v0.50 allow-list amendment.** The "Nothing else" inventory above is superseded only by `eight_ball_saved_readings_v1` as specified in §5.E. The no-derived-profile rule remains literal: reopening an entry runs its stored input payload through the existing `profileFromPayload` → `buildProfile` and render path, so calculation outputs continue to have one authority.

**v0.63 gender-field amendment (§1.D v0.63, controller override, 2026-08-03).** The `eight_ball_profile_v1` field list above gains the one optional `gender` bullet, under the strict two-token vocabulary stated there. The field is user-entered and calc-driving — the §1.D v0.63 kua block reads it — so it forwards through `optsFromPayload` like the other calculation inputs; the no-derived-profile rule is untouched. The §5.E "same user-entered reconstruction fields" parenthetical (`name`, `dob`, optional `time`/`city`/`cc`/`tz`/`country`/`lat`/`lng`) is superseded to include optional `gender` under the same strict vocabulary — the archive must reconstruct the same reading the fresh submit produced, and a gender dropped there would silently re-key a saved kua on reopen. The privacy-copy update this section requires before merge shipped in the same PR: the about-modal storage sentence names the optional input, its single consumer, and that it can stay blank.

**v0.69 gender-field amendment (the consumer is gone; the disclosure moves to the point of entry, 2026-08-06).** Two claims above are superseded and preserved as lineage per L17. **(1)** The field-list bullet's "**Feeds only the §1.D v0.63 kua block**" and this v0.63 amendment's "**user-entered and calc-driving — the §1.D v0.63 kua block reads it**" are both false as of §1.D v0.67, which deleted that block. The field is user-entered and **persisted**, and it has **no calculation or output reader**. That is the precise form, and the precision is load-bearing: the field IS read — by `saveProfile`, `optsFromPayload`, `populateRisingFields` (rehydrating the control), the `ui/readings.js` archive round-trip, and `buildProfile`, which copies it onto the profile object. Every one of those is persistence or rehydration plumbing, moving the value the user typed back to the user. **None of them is a coordinate, a catalog key, a note slot, a render decision, or anything that reaches the share artifact.** "Nothing reads it" would be the easier sentence and it would be false. It still forwards through `optsFromPayload` — not because a calculation needs it, but because dropping it there would silently discard a value the user supplied and the archive round-trips. Everything else in both statements survives unchanged: strict `'male' | 'female'`, dropped off-vocabulary at every write seam, optional forever, never required by any surface, never on the free surface, and the §5.E archive parenthetical still includes it. **(2)** The v0.63 privacy-copy requirement was discharged by an about-modal sentence naming "its single consumer". There is no consumer to name, so the disclosure moves to where the decision is actually made: the control itself now carries `optional · stored on this device · does not affect your reading`, bound to the select by `aria-describedby` so it reaches assistive tech as part of the field. The about-modal sentence is retained and already states that no surface reads the input.

**The claim is mechanically true, not merely written.** "Does not affect your reading" is enforced by two differentials in `tests/profile.test.js`, both run female-vs-absent AND male-vs-female, because a single comparison would still pass if something keyed off mere presence. The first compares `buildProfile`'s own output. The second — added after a pre-merge lane observed that the first would stay green if a reader were introduced further down — compares what the profile actually PRODUCES: `getCard`'s catalog cell, and `cellRenderState` over every compartment at full entitlement, so a difference cannot hide behind a sealed empty string. If gender ever begins to move a coordinate, a catalog key or a rendered cell, the sentence on the form goes red. The copy deliberately claims **nothing further**: no promise about future use, and no privacy guarantee beyond what the code already enforces (pinned by a test that rejects `never`/`always`/`anonymised`/`encrypted`/`private`/`secure`/`guarantee` in the string).

**The open question from §1.D v0.67 is NOT closed by this amendment, and this is not a purpose.** A disclosure is not a justification: the product still stores a demographic no surface reads, and §5 admitted it on a ground that is now vacant. It needs either a named purpose or removal from this allow-list, and both remain the controller's call. What this amendment does is stop the document from asserting a consumer that does not exist, and stop the form from asking without saying.


**v0.71 amendment (precision corrections to v0.69 and v0.70, and the L17 breach that produced them, 2026-08-06).** This amendment exists because the corrections it carries were first made **the wrong way**: commit `60366cf` edited the v0.69 amendment above, the v0.70 four-events clause, and both footer entries **in place**. That is an L17 breach — amendments supersede, never edit — and it is a compounded one, because the same commit correctly superseded §1.D v0.67 through v0.69 rather than editing it, and its own text cites the PR #190 precedent for not doing exactly what it then did. The five blocks were restored byte-for-byte from `76e84cf` and their substance re-expressed here. **Nothing below is a new decision; it is the same three corrections, relocated to where L17 requires them.**

**(1) "No surface reads it" is retired as a phrasing.** v0.69 above says the field has "no calculation or output reader" and then ends by endorsing the about-modal's "no surface reads the input"; the second half is superseded. The phrase is false even on the narrow reading of *surface* as a rendered one — `populateRisingFields` writes the persisted value back into the visible `#gender-input` control on every rehydration, which is why a stale gender could once survive a form reset (the F1 residue class in §1.D v0.67). §1.D v0.67's own "stores a demographic input that no surface reads" and its "a stored field with no reader" follow-on are superseded here for the same reason — not stale, imprecise. **Two canonical forms from here on:** "no calculation or output reader" in doctrine and code comments; "does not affect your reading" in user-facing copy. The about modal now carries the second, so it and the control state one claim in one wording. The open question §1.D v0.67 raised is untouched and still open.

**(2) `paid_t3_cta_clicked` counts the checkout hand-off and NOT offer exposure.** v0.70's parenthetical says it "separates 'nobody sees the offer' from 'everybody sees it and declines'". It cannot, and that claim is superseded. Nothing fires when the paywall opens, and three uncounted steps sit between the two counted events: the lock icon and the offer control emit nothing and being in the DOM is not being in a viewport; `stagePurchase` refuses to open the modal at all if the pending profile cannot be written and read back; only then does the CTA exist to be tapped. A render with no tap is equally consistent with never noticed, storage blocked, opened and dismissed, or read in full and refused — and **no combination of the four events can separate them**. Closing that gap needs an offer-exposure event on modal open, which is a fifth event and therefore an amendment of its own; it is **not** proposed here and the list stays CLOSED at four. The operator plan's metric is renamed accordingly and both sides of its ratio are now filtered to the unentitled rungs (an unfiltered numerator over a filtered denominator counted t3 taps the denominator had excluded).

**(3) Under a null sink the counts are UNOBSERVED, not zero.** "Structurally zero" is superseded wherever it appeared. `recordMeasurement` builds its record, returns it and drops it; nothing accumulates and the module exports no way to read a count back. Zero would be a finding about the world — "no device has ever tapped the CTA" — and nothing has established that. Every rate is undefined rather than 0, because its denominator is unobserved too.

**(4) The static guard on the inline render path was widened.** v0.70 records that a reader added inside `index.html`'s `renderCard` is out of reach of a runtime differential and is covered by a static scan instead. That scan matched only `.gender` and `['gender']`, so a destructured read — `const { gender } = profile;` — changed visible output while every test stayed green (verified by counterexample). The guard now rejects the identifier in any form in the stripped `renderCard` body, and carries the destructuring counter-case as a fixture.

Suite 56 files / 1985 tests green; product audit PASS 14/0/0/0 on a clean tree (`product.git_status` warns only while changes are uncommitted); local PII scan clean, 861 files; `index.html` 1474/1500. **STAGED — cross-model audit before merge per §10/L48; merge is the operator's word.**


**v0.72 amendment (the static gender guard was defeated three ways; what it guarantees is now stated exactly, 2026-08-06).** §5 v0.71 item (4) said the guard "now rejects the identifier in any form in the stripped `renderCard` body". **That claim was false in two directions and is superseded here.** A re-audit and then an adversarial red-team defeated it:

**(a) The matcher stripped literals before matching**, so `profile["gender"]` became `profile[""]` and an entire template literal vanished with its `${...}` inside it. **(b) The extractor brace-matched before stripping comments**, so a `// }}}}` comment truncated `renderCard`'s body from 3348 characters to 4 and anything below it left the scanned region; quoting the function's signature in a comment hijacked the extraction the same way. **(c) The scope was wrong twice over** — the guard read only `renderCard`, so a reader in any sibling helper of the same inline module was invisible; and the separate `core/`+`ui/` scan still used a property-only regex, so a destructured read in `ui/result.js` passed with the whole suite green. **(d) The word boundary was wrong** — `\bgender\b` did not see `getGenderInput()`, so `renderCard` could call the form's own accessor, read the live control and change the card while naming no property at all. That one is ordinary code, not obfuscation, and is the reason the matcher is now boundary-free.

**The repair is lexical, in `tests/helpers/js-lex.js`.** One pass classifies every character CODE / COMMENT / STRING, with template interpolations classified as code; braces are counted only where they are code, the signature is located only in code, and the identifier scan drops comment text while keeping code and string text — because a computed property key lives in a string, which is exactly what stripping literals destroyed. The same function now backs the host guard and the `core/`+`ui/` scan, so the two cannot drift apart again as they had.

**What the guard guarantees, stated exactly, because this document has overclaimed coverage three times running.** No line of the inline module, and no line of any `core/` or `ui/` module outside the three-file input path, mentions `gender` in any casing — except two lines in the host: the accessor's import and its single call at the submit seam. Every SPELLED read fails the suite, wherever it sits. **It does not stop an adversary.** The red-team got past it with a runtime-built key (`profile['gen' + 'der']`), a Unicode escape inside the property name, and a `JSON.stringify(profile).includes('"female"')` sniff — each executes and changes the card, and none writes the identifier. No lexical check closes that class; only executing `renderCard` would, and it cannot be executed from vitest (§6 keeps it in the inline module, §12 forbids jsdom). **The runtime differential over the pure surfaces is the real guarantee; this is the fence around what that differential cannot reach.**

**It fails CLOSED and that is deliberate.** Innocent user-visible copy, an aria-label, a CSS class or a data attribute containing the word would trip it; none exists today. The remedy when one is genuinely wanted is to add it to the pinned lines, a reviewed act — never to loosen the matcher.

Suite 56 files / 1999 tests green; product audit PASS 14/0/0/0 on a clean tree; local PII scan clean, 862 files; `index.html` 1474/1500. **STAGED — cross-model audit before merge per §10/L48; merge is the operator's word.**


**v0.73 amendment (the lexical guard was still false-greening; and v0.72 miscounted its own scope, 2026-08-06).** An independent audit of the v0.72 commit found four bounded defects. **Three are in the guard v0.72 announced as the repair, so v0.72's guarantee — "every SPELLED read fails the suite, wherever it sits" — was false as written and is superseded here.**

**(1) The lexer still swallowed live code, three ways, all of them on ordinary JavaScript rather than an attack.** A `}` inside a regex literal was classified as structure, so `replace(/\}/g, '')` closed the extracted block early and everything below it went unscanned. A regex immediately after the `)` of `if (...)` was read as division, after which the `//` inside a character class opened a line comment and blanked every live statement to the end of the line — the `/*` form blanked five lines. And the regex-start test matched its keyword alternation **unanchored**, so the `in` inside the identifier `plain` made `plain / 2 /* } */` lex as a regex and truncated a 94-character body to 43. **FIXED:** regex characters are their own lexical kind and cannot move a brace counter; control-head parens are tracked so the `)` of `if (...)` is distinguished from the `)` of `(a+b)/2`; and the keyword test is anchored on an identifier boundary with the token window widened so `instanceof` survives. Seven counter-cases pin the class, plus a division case pinning the other direction.

**(2) The corpus guarantee was written broader than the scan reached.** The `core/`+`ui/` sweep read only top-level `.js` files, and the host guard matched only the FIRST `<script type="module">` block. A module in a future subdirectory, or a second inline module, would have carried a reader neither one saw. Both are flat and single today; nothing forbade either. **FIXED:** the sweep recurses, the host guard concatenates every module block, and the scan asserts it actually reached the corpus it claims (including every file on its own allow-list). The single-inline-module count is pinned separately as a §6 invariant, but the guard no longer depends on it.

**(3) v0.72's own scope statement was wrong.** It says "defeated three ways" in its amendment heading, its footer entry and its changelog bullet, while enumerating four categories — (a) literal-stripping order, (b) brace/comment order, (c) scope, (d) word boundary — across five concrete bypasses. The correct count is **four categories, five bypasses**; v0.72's text stands as written per L17 and is corrected here rather than edited.

**(4) Two assurance claims overclaimed and are narrowed.** v0.72 said the host guard and the module sweep "share one function, so they cannot drift apart again". They share the **classifier and the matcher constant**, not one whole function — they answer different questions — and "cannot drift" is too strong. What is true, and now literally so, is that the two things that were separately wrong before (how source is stripped, and what pattern is matched) have exactly one definition each. Separately, the limits prose named a Unicode-escape bypass (`profile.gend\u0065r`) that no test asserted; that counter-case now ships beside the other two.

**The guarantee, restated and unchanged in kind:** every SPELLED read of the field fails the suite — anywhere in the host's inline module or in any `core/`/`ui/` module outside the three-file input path, at any nesting depth. It still does **not** stop an adversary: a runtime-built key, a Unicode escape in the property name, and a value-scanning sniff all pass, and no lexical check closes that class. The runtime differential over the pure surfaces remains the real guarantee; this is the fence around what that differential cannot reach, and it fails closed.

Suite 56 files / 2001 tests green; product audit PASS 14/0/0/0 on a clean tree; local PII scan clean, 862 files; `index.html` 1474/1500. **STAGED — cross-model audit before merge per §10/L48; merge is the operator's word.**


**v0.74 amendment (the absolute claim moves off the lexer and onto a raw allowlist, 2026-08-06).** A final-byte audit produced the repro that settles three rounds of argument. At the real `renderCard` anchor:

```js
const spread = [.../[//]/.exec(cell.name)];
cardName.textContent = profile.gender ? "f" : cell.name;
```

Valid JavaScript, executed by Node, changing what the card displays — and **both lexical guards reported clean**. A regex may legally begin after a spread `...`, which the previous-token heuristic does not list, so the `/` read as division and the `//` inside the character class opened a comment over a live read. Independent lanes reproduced `for await` and division-RHS variants.

**The conclusion, stated plainly because it should have been drawn two amendments ago:** enumerating every ES position where a regex may begin is not something a hand lexer does correctly, and v0.72 and v0.73 each closed the newest hole and then re-asserted the same absolute claim. **§5 v0.73's "every spelled read fails at any nesting depth" is superseded.**

**The PRIMARY invariant is now raw and fail-closed:** every line of the bounded corpus that contains the identifier, in any casing, is pinned verbatim — `index.html` plus every `core/`/`ui/` module outside the three input-path files. It parses nothing, so no syntax confusion can hide a read from it. Comments and user-visible copy are on the list too, which is deliberate: stale gender COPY is a defect this cycle already corrected twice, and pinning it means a copy edit gets re-read rather than assumed. The remedy when the list must grow is to add the line verbatim, as a reviewed act; never to relax the matcher.

**The lexical guards are retained as SECONDARY diagnostics** with narrowed claims — they locate a read and name where it is, which a line list cannot — and a counter-case pins one of their known blind spots so the primary/secondary split is enforced by behaviour rather than by prose. Two further defects in them are fixed in the same change: the inline-module matcher missed valid `type=module`, `type = "module"` and `TYPE="module"` spellings, and its own test asserted on a token the baseline already contained, so it could not tell whether a second block was read at all (a unique sentinel replaces it).

**What is unchanged:** the runtime differential over the pure surfaces remains the real guarantee, and no static check of any kind closes the indirection class — a runtime-built key, a Unicode escape in the property name, or a value-scanning sniff writes no identifier and is invisible to raw and lexical scanning alike.
