**v0.75 amendment (the raw allowlist had two holes of its own, 2026-08-06).** v0.74 moved the absolute claim onto a raw allowlist. An audit of that commit found the allowlist itself incomplete in two ways, so **v0.74's "every line of the bounded corpus that contains the identifier is pinned verbatim" was true only of part of the corpus** and is superseded here.

**(1) Three files were exempt WHOLESALE.** `core/profile.js`, `ui/profile.js` and `ui/readings.js` were skipped on the stated ground that the runtime differential covered them. It does not: the differential drives `buildProfile` and the pure output surfaces, and never drives `optsFromPayload`, `profileFromPayload`, `saveProfile` or `populateRisingFields`. A male-only transformation in any of those would have passed everything. **FIXED — the exemption is gone and all 41 raw occurrences across all six files are pinned, owner files included.**

**(2) The allowlist pinned line TEXT but not PLACE.** Deleting the legitimate submit-seam line and inserting the identical text into a render-path helper leaves the raw inventory byte-identical while the field starts driving the card — reproduced, six lines before and six after, same strings. **FIXED:** the collection occurrence is pinned to the raw submit-handler region (`profileForm` submit through the `tryAnother` handler), asserted to appear exactly once inside it and nowhere else, with the relocation as a discriminating counter-case that first proves the raw inventory does not move.

**Four evidence and truth corrections ride with it.** The spread repro this document has cited three times used `exec(cell.name)`, but **no card name in the 144-cell deck contains a slash**, so the spread of a `null` result throws before the gender branch — the bypass is real, the published evidence for it was not, and the fixture now uses a guaranteed match and was executed to confirm the output changes. The directory recursion was unexercised because `core/` and `ui/` are flat, and is now pinned against `tests/`. A comment still claimed a word boundary excludes `getGenderInput` when the boundary was removed precisely so that it does not. And the module-wide lexical guard was still labelled PRIMARY after the claim moved.

**The Unicode form, recorded correctly at the third attempt.** §5 v0.72–v0.74 and two audit addenda referred to this bypass as `profile.gender` — without the escape, which describes an ordinary read that every guard catches and inverts the point. The form is **`profile.gend\u0065r`**: a property access whose `e` is a Unicode escape, reading the same property while containing no literal `gender` substring. It is on the recorded-limits list because no scan, raw or lexical, can see it.

**v0.76 amendment (the location pin was the wrong region, and v0.75 libelled v0.73, 2026-08-06).** An audit of the v0.75 commit found the location guard it introduced still false-greening, plus one claim in v0.75 that is simply untrue of the record.

**(1) The region was sliced, not brace-matched.** v0.75 pinned the collection line to "the raw submit-handler region", implemented as the text between the submit listener's opening and the NEXT listener's opening. That span **includes everything after the submit callback closes**. Moving the exact line into a helper defined in that gap, and calling it from `renderCard`, passed the raw inventory, the whole-file uniqueness check and the region count — while the card changed by gender. **FIXED:** the region is now the submit callback's own **brace-matched body**, obtained through the same `functionBody` used elsewhere, and the counter-case moves the line into a helper that is genuinely **invoked** (v0.75's fixture inserted an uncalled helper, so it never demonstrated what it claimed).

**(2) The pin was a raw substring, so it could not tell code from text.** Wrapping the exact bytes in a template literal inside the callback satisfied inventory, uniqueness and region count while the executable `getGenderInput()` call went from one to none. **FIXED:** the occurrence must now classify as **CODE**, with that neutering as its counter-case.

**(3) A generic alias carried the value past every guard.** The handler read `const g = getGenderInput()`, and `g` is nameless as far as any gender scan is concerned — a later use of it could drive the card with no gender token anywhere. **FIXED in the host:** the alias is now `genderInput`, so any onward use spells the identifier and trips the raw scan. This is the one product-code change in the amendment: a local rename inside the submit handler, no behaviour change, verified in-browser (gender still persists, render clean, no console errors).

**(4) v0.75 states something false about this document.** It says §5 "v0.72–v0.74 and two audit addenda referred to this bypass as `profile.gender` — without the escape". **v0.73 already carries the escaped form**, in its own body. The correct statement is that v0.72, v0.74 and the two addenda dropped the escape while v0.73 did not. Recorded here rather than edited into v0.75, per L17 — and noted as the fourth consecutive amendment in which a claim ABOUT the evidence, rather than the fix itself, was the defect. The form remains **`profile.gend\u0065r`**.

**(5) Two stale descriptions in the test corpus** are corrected: one still said the three owner files were excluded and runtime-covered (v0.75 removed that exemption), and one still described the identifier scan as property-only and blind to destructuring (fixed several rounds earlier).

**v0.77 amendment (the place invariant stops depending on the lexer; and the escape claim is finally stated accurately, 2026-08-06).** An audit of the v0.76 commit found the place guard still resting on the one component this section has repeatedly recorded as incomplete, plus a reachability gap the guard never addressed, plus two tautological fixtures and a citation still wrong at the third telling.

**(1) The place invariant was built on the hand lexer — again.** v0.76 replaced a sliced region with the submit callback's **brace-matched** body, which is the lexer. A lane defeated it by hiding the callback's close behind a spread-regex and reopening a block, then placing a called helper outside the real callback: module parses, raw inventory unchanged, uniqueness 1, "submit-body" count 1, occurrence CODE, `renderCard` tokens empty, helper invoked. **v0.76's place guarantee is superseded.** Patching the lexer once more would repeat the mistake §5 v0.74 already named. **FIXED lexer-free:** the collection line is pinned inside an **exact contiguous block of raw source** — the `opts` object it populates, its own comment, the line itself, and the branch that follows — required to appear byte-for-byte exactly once. It parses nothing, so no lexical confusion can move it. The lexer survives only as a labelled diagnostic asserting the occurrence is CODE, carrying no absolute claim.

**(2) Place was never reachability, and nothing tested it.** Wrapping the line in `if (false)` **inside the real callback** leaves the raw inventory identical, uniqueness at 1, the occurrence CODE, and v0.76's submit-body count at 1 — while gender never forwards and never persists. Every guard green, the field silently dead. **FIXED:** the block pin breaks on the interposed line, and the case ships as its own counter-case.

**(3) Two fixtures asserted less than they claimed.** The invocation check used `toContain('displayName(cell, opts)')`, which the inserted **declaration** satisfies by itself, so it would have passed even if the call site never applied — now the exact call line, counted, with its absence at baseline asserted first. And the template-literal fixture used a one-line wrapper, which changes the trimmed line and is therefore caught by the raw allowlist already — a false premise; it now uses a multiline template that leaves the line's own text intact, so the block pin is the sole catch, with the premise asserted.

**(4) The escape claim, stated accurately at the third attempt.** v0.75 said v0.72–v0.74 dropped the escape; v0.76 narrowed that to v0.72, v0.74 and the addenda. **Both are wrong.** The record is: **v0.72 and v0.74 render no Unicode example at all** — they name the class generically, and v0.74's `profile.gender` is its separate spread/regex repro, a different bypass; **v0.73 prints `profile.gend\u0065r` correctly**; and **only audit addenda 6 and 7 printed the malformed form.** Corrected here per L17 rather than edited into v0.75 or v0.76.

**(5) One characterisation was unfair to the record.** v0.76's footer says the superseded uncalled fixture "demonstrated nothing". It demonstrated a real if weaker fact — that the line had left the region — but not the output change its name implied. Stated correctly here.

**v0.78 amendment (the escape claim was false ON ARRIVAL, and the defect is now pinned mechanically, 2026-08-06).** v0.77 stated that "only audit addenda 6 and 7 printed the malformed form". **That was untrue at the moment it was written**: Addendum 10 — the same change that carried v0.77 — printed the malformed form too, in the very table asserting who had rendered it correctly. Its bytes at that line held no `0x5c`. So the count is **three** addenda, not two: 6, 8's own erratum, and 10.

**This is the fourth consecutive attempt at one sentence.** Addendum 6 printed it bare; Addendum 8's erratum said "this is the correction" and printed it bare again in its prose; Addendum 10 declared the matter settled and printed it bare a third time; v0.77 then described that history wrongly. Every attempt was a careful re-reading, and prose review cannot see a missing backslash — which is why this amendment does not simply correct the sentence again.

**The defect is pinned mechanically.** `tests/prose_coordinate_count.test.js` now requires that, in DOCTRINE, the journal and the audit artifact, **every line naming `profile.gend` either carries the real escape sequence or appears verbatim on an allowlist of lines that deliberately render the ORDINARY read** (quoting the historical error, or showing the unrelated spread repro). Verified by counterfactual: removing one backslash reds the suite. The two malformed sites in the artifact were repaired to hold a real `0x5c`, confirmed with `od -c` and an exact fixed-string match rather than by reading.

**The historical record, corrected and now enforceable.** §5 v0.72 and v0.74 render no Unicode example at all; **v0.73 renders `profile.gend\u0065r` correctly**; addenda 6, 8 (in prose) and 10 rendered it malformed and are now repaired or superseded. v0.75, v0.76 and v0.77 each mis-stated some part of this and all three stand unedited per L17.

**In-place byte repair, disclosed.** Repairing an artifact's bytes is not the append-only posture used everywhere else in this cycle, and it is done here deliberately and narrowly: the sentences already declared their intent in words ("correct", "what the fixture actually contains") while their glyphs contradicted it, so this restores the rendering to the stated intent rather than revising any finding. No claim, verdict or count was altered. Addendum 11 records the repair.

**v0.79 amendment (the seam is EXECUTED, not pinned; and the in-place history edit is reverted, 2026-08-06).** Two findings, and the first ends a seven-round argument the second-to-last six amendments were all losing.

**(1) Every byte pin was defeated by moving the BLOCK rather than the LINE.** v0.77's raw adjacency block, v0.78's allowlist, and every earlier guard survive intact when the whole opts/collection/city segment is wrapped in `if (false)` — or dropped into an uncalled helper — beside a live gender-free fallback. Independently reproduced: raw inventory byte-identical, block count 1, line count 1, occurrence CODE, module parses, and the field **never forwards and never persists**. The suite is green on a file where gender is silently dead. **Worse, the counter-case named for that very bypass passes on it** — `an UNREACHABLE collection line inside the handler is caught` applies its own `if (false)` on top, which breaks the block and satisfies the assertion, so the test named after the defect was green while the defect shipped. **v0.77's and v0.78's place/reachability guarantees are superseded.**

**The fix is not an eighth pin.** The option object is now built by **one pure exported function**, `buildSubmitOpts` in `ui/profile.js`, and `tests/submit_seam.test.js` **EXECUTES the host's real submit-handler body** — extracted from `index.html` and compiled with `new Function` against stubs — asserting that the collected value reaches **both** `buildProfile` and `saveProfile`. Behaviour is the former block's quirk for quirk (verified by a 125-case differential against the old inline logic: zero differences, including `lat: 0`, an empty `cc`, and off-vocabulary input). `index.html` drops 1477 → 1469 lines. The three bypasses — dead block, relocated block, commented-out block — ship as counter-cases that fail **behaviourally**: applied to the real file they report *expected undefined to be 'female'*, not a string count. **`new Function` is a new pattern in this suite; §12 bans jsdom, not compilation.** Verified live in a browser: gender persists with a birth time, and the key is absent rather than empty without one.

**The residual, stated exactly:** vitest compiles the handler body but never loads the page, so *that the listener is registered and fires* is still unproven here — only a browser closes that, and this change was browser-verified by hand. Runtime indirection naming no identifier remains open and is now confined to the ~10 lines between the call and its two consumers, covered by three raw facts (one `buildSubmitOpts({` line; `opts` bound exactly once; both consumer lines present verbatim).

**(2) The in-place repair of audit history is REVERTED.** v0.78 recorded repairing two malformed renderings in the audit artifact and claimed it "altered no claim, verdict or count". **Both parts were wrong.** The sites were **Addendum 7 and Addendum 10, not 6, 8 and 10** — and Addendum 8's still-current erratum explicitly says *"Addendum 7's erratum tried to correct it and reproduced the same error… Both addenda stand"*, so editing Addendum 7 made a live claim in Addendum 8 false. That is exactly the alteration v0.78 denied making. **Both lines are restored byte-for-byte from `d1c93e4`**, their bare renderings are allowlisted as deliberate historical entries, and the true pre-repair set is recorded here: **addenda 6, 7 and 10 rendered it malformed; addendum 8 rendered it correctly.** The append-only posture holds everywhere; v0.78's paragraph stands unedited as the record of the mistake.

**(3) One fixture claim corrected.** v0.77 and Addendum 10 called the block pin the SOLE catch for the multiline-template mutation. It is caught twice — block count 0 **and** kind STRING. The genuine CODE-only discriminator is the whole-block **comment** wrapper, which now ships as an executed counter-case instead.

**v0.80 amendment (executing the seam in ONE environment was not enough; and three count/attribution errors in v0.79, 2026-08-06).**

**(1) Node's globals are not the browser's, and that gap was a live bypass.** v0.79 replaced byte pinning with an executed seam and named exactly one residual — that listener registration is unproven. **That statement was incomplete and is superseded.** A handler branching on `typeof window === "undefined"` forwards the field under the vitest harness and drops it in a browser: mutation applied, raw gender inventory byte-identical, the exact gender-bearing host line still one, all four driver tests green, and the shipped listener would omit the value. The manual browser pass proves the current bytes, not the class. **FIXED:** every drive now runs under **both** environments — bare node and a browser-like global — every assertion is made in both, an explicit equality test requires the two to agree, and the `typeof window` mutation ships as a fourth counter-case shaped to what actually catches it (the environments DISAGREEING, since it forwards correctly under node by construction). Re-run against the real file with the mutation applied: **6 of 7 tests red**, including the `[browser]` variant that was green before.

**The residual, restated exactly:** vitest compiles and runs the handler body under both global shapes, but it never loads the page, so *that the listener is registered and fires* remains unproven by any test — only a browser closes that, and it is browser-checked by hand each cycle. Runtime indirection naming no identifier also stays open.

**(2) v0.79 mischaracterised v0.78.** It called "v0.78's allowlist" a defeated submit byte pin and superseded "v0.78's place/reachability guarantees". **v0.78 added the Unicode-prose rendering guard — it is not a submit-seam guard and it is not defeated;** it remains valid and in force. The defeated seam-static rounds number **six**, not seven, exactly as the source and test comments have said throughout. v0.79's paragraph stands unedited; this is the correction.

**(3) Two count errors.** Addendum 12 opens "two P2s plus a P3" while its own seam heading is P1, and v0.79 says "Two findings" then enumerates three. The correct tally for that round is **one P1, one P2, one P3**. Separately, the rendering guard's own comment named addenda 6, 8 and 10 as the malformed set when the verified set is **6, 7 and 10** — Addendum 8 rendered it correctly — which is the same misattribution v0.79 §(2) corrected in the doctrine and which had survived in the test's documentation; both now agree.

**(4) Two stale figures in the test corpus** are corrected: a comment still quoted the pre-extraction host shape `opts.gender = genderInput`, and the raw allowlist's summary said 43 occurrences where the bounded corpus now holds 44.

Suite 57 files / 2014 tests green; product audit PASS 14/0/0/0 on a clean tree; local PII scan clean, 863 files; `index.html` 1469/1500. **STAGED — cross-model audit before merge per §10/L48; merge is the operator's word.**


**v0.81 amendment (closing the environment CLASS, not one probe, 2026-08-06).**

**(1) v0.80 closed the example and left the class open.** Its browser shape was `globalThis.window = {document:{}, navigator:{}}` — a name, not a page. A handler probing **`typeof document`** rather than `typeof window` therefore forwarded the field under *both* declared shapes and dropped it in a real browser, with the host-call count still one and the raw gender inventory byte-identical. **v0.80's environment-equivalence guarantee is superseded.** Same-class survivors were `typeof location`, `typeof self`, `globalThis.document` presence, `window !== globalThis`, and `window.navigator !== navigator`.

**FIXED at the class level, two ways.** The browser shape now installs every environment-sensitive global a page exposes **with a page's identities** — `window === globalThis === self`, bare `document`/`location`/`navigator` as well as their `window.*` forms, and `window.navigator === navigator` — via property *descriptors*, because node defines `navigator` as a getter-only accessor that plain assignment cannot replace. Eight probes ship as counter-cases, each verified to forward under node and drop under the browser shape; a ninth guards the guard by asserting the identities hold inside the helper and are gone outside it, so the probes cannot pass vacuously. Re-run with the audit's own `typeof document` mutation, and with `typeof location` and `window !== globalThis`: **14 of 16 red** in each case, all green on restore.

**And a bounded RAW ban:** the submit handler — 38 lines — may name **no** environment-sensitive global at all (`window`, `document`, `location`, `navigator`, `self`, `globalThis`, `process`, `top`, `parent`, `frames`, `screen`). It names none today. The option object must not depend on where it runs, and the cheapest way to keep that true is to forbid the question being asked in that scope.

**`typeof navigator` is deliberately NOT among the probes:** node defines `navigator`, so it is not a node-vs-browser discriminator and cannot express this bypass. Its *identity* still can, and that case is covered.

**The residual, stated without narrowing it again:** the page is never loaded by any test, so **listener registration and firing** is unproven there and is browser-checked by hand each cycle. **Runtime-built indirection** — `globalThis["docu" + "ment"]`, or any probe that spells no identifier — is closed by nothing here and is honestly open; no static or stubbed-runtime check reaches it. The claim this amendment makes is narrow and exact: **a spelled environment probe in the submit handler cannot change what the option object carries without failing the suite.**

**(2) One quantifier correction, appended not edited.** Addendum 12 names three bypasses and then says "verified by applying **both**". Three were applied, not two. Addendum 12 stands unedited — the artifact is append-only — and Addendum 13 claimed all three P3 items absorbed while recording only two; this is the third.

**v0.85 amendment (index.html is pinned whole; H1–H6 become permanent tests; and the residual moves one level out, 2026-08-07).** The re-audit of `7a23cf1` returned DO NOT MERGE — P0 0 / P1 1 / P2 2 / P3 1. Absorbed. v0.84, Addendum 17 and both 2026-08-07 journal entries are preserved byte-for-byte; corrections are here.

**(1) P1 — H1–H6 existed only as prose.** They were verified false-greens recorded in Addendum 17 and in comments, with no permanent discriminating test. **`index.html` is now pinned WHOLE over raw bytes — 69,373 B, SHA-256 `71e97b80e68b6bc8c7d39649963ba795a438a384dc0f0e32e56951eeeedb1bd0`** — `cleanHtml()` compares that whole file rather than the handler region, the two region pins are retained as **diagnostics** (they name which bytes the drives execute; they are redundant against the file hash for anything inside it), and **all six ship as permanent fixtures**. Each asserts the same pair: that the diagnostic regions stay **byte-identical** — which is why regions were never enough — and that the whole-file pin **moves**. That pairing is the intended reason; "something went red" would not distinguish the new guard from the old.

**(2) H6's provenance, established before editing, and a correction to v0.84.** H6 is **`index.html` line 1058**, one occurrence, `'./core/profile.js'` → `'./vendor-core.js'`, a **−1 byte** edit, plus a companion `vendor-core.js` at the repo root whose `buildProfile` deletes the field from the live, **unfrozen** options object one statement before `saveProfile`. **H6 is therefore INSIDE the host, not outside it**, so neither "pin its exact runtime file" nor a runtime manifest is required to close it. Measured across all six: **every one of H1–H6 requires an index.html edit and every one moves the whole-file pin** — 69,563 / 69,466 / 69,458 / 69,458 / 69,507 / 69,372 bytes against a clean 69,373. **v0.84 and Addendum 17 said a whole-index pin "kills five of the six" without naming a survivor; that was unsupported and is withdrawn — there is no survivor among the six.** H3 and H6 additionally need a root file; both are inert without their index.html half.

**(3) Secondary defenses, and they are only that.** Added as positive exact sets, not deny-lists: the `<script>` tags in `index.html` must be exactly the two reviewed ones (so an importmap fails because the set changed); its **eighteen** module specifiers must be exactly the reviewed list and each must exist on disk; the repo root must carry exactly `vitest.config.js`. Each was verified to fire alone. **v0.84 and Addendum 17 said "seventeen import lines"; the correct count is EIGHTEEN** — one (`./ui/payments.js`) sits on the closing line of a multi-line import at line 1069, so a line-anchored scan misses it, and the assertion scans `from '…'` occurrences for exactly that reason.

**(4) THE RESIDUAL MOVES ONE LEVEL OUT, AND IS NOT CLOSED. Reachability does not see code inside an allowed module.** Verified: inserting into `core/profile.js`'s `buildProfile`

```
if (typeof location !== 'undefined' && location.protocol !== 'file:' && opts)
  { try { delete opts['g' + 'ender']; } catch (_) {} }
```

leaves `index.html` byte-identical (69,373 / `71e97b80…`), `ui/profile.js` byte-identical (17,566 / `d6fba912…`), adds no root file, changes no specifier and no script tag — and runs **57 files / 2047 tests GREEN**. Every assertion added above is satisfied by it. Closing this class needs a **deterministic raw-byte manifest of every runtime module the page reaches**; the re-audit made that conditional on H6 being outside the host, and H6 is not, so it was neither ordered nor built. It is named here as the exact open item rather than papered over, and it reds on every product edit, which is a scope call for the controller and the audit.

**(5) P2 — the generic pin message is narrowed.** It claimed the pinned bytes are "what the behavioural tests in this file execute". False for the import specifier line, which is never executed here because the drives inject their dependencies; false for `resolveGenderSelect`, which production reaches via `{ form, anchor }` while the drives pass `{ genderSelect }`; and false for most of `index.html` now that it is pinned whole. Each pin now carries its own `executed` statement, primary and diagnostic pins say which they are, and the message states plainly that a pin says what a **file contains** — not that the page loads that file, and not that no other runtime module changed.

**(6) P3 — the 32 → 30 arithmetic, corrected.** Addendum 17 wrote "32 − 4 declaration pins + 1 whole-file pin + 1 new fixture, and the two anchor tests merged". Wrong: there was one anchor test and it remained one. Correct: **six source-pin tests became three (−3), plus one alternate-export fixture (+1) = 30.** This amendment takes it to **40**: three pins become four (+1), six H-fixtures (+6), three secondary assertions (+3).

**Nothing new was added beyond that authority.** No runtime edit, no parser, no package, no browser harness, no lexer expansion. **Product runtime byte-unchanged** vs `446a187`.

Suite 57 files / 2047 tests green; product audit PASS 14/0/0/0 on a clean tree; assurance 102 tests OK; local PII scan clean, 863 files; `index.html` 1469/1500. **STAGED — DO NOT MERGE; the H7 class above is open and named.**


**v0.84 amendment (a declaration slice pins a substring, not the live export; and the seam guard is not sufficient, 2026-08-07).** The final cross-model audit of `7bb5630` returned DO NOT MERGE — P0 0 / P1 3 / P2 2 / P3 1. All six are absorbed, each reproduced in this repo before anything was edited. v0.83, Addendum 16 and the 2026-08-07 journal entry are preserved byte-for-byte and corrected here additively. **Read the "not sufficient" section before quoting anything above it.**

**(1) P1 — the four `ui/profile.js` pins guarded SUBSTRINGS, not the live export.** `export function f(){}` creates a **mutable** binding and ES module exports are **live**, so appending to the module

```
function readControl() {
  if (import.meta.url.startsWith('http')) return undefined;
  const v = _genderSelect && _genderSelect.value;
  return v === 'male' || v === 'female' ? v : undefined;
}
getGenderInput = readControl;
```

leaves the pinned `getGenderInput` declaration **byte-identical — 143 bytes, `825a4df3…`** — while every importer receives `readControl`. The Unicode escapes are load-bearing: written plainly the raw allowlist in `tests/profile.test.js` turns red (1 failed / 2038 passed); escaped, the literal substring never appears and the whole suite is **57 files / 2039 tests GREEN**. Driven for real, the same bytes return `'female'` under a `file:` module URL and **`undefined`** under `https://the-eight-ball.netlify.app/ui/profile.js`. **v0.83's "the six pinned units' bytes are closed" is superseded**: a slice closes a slice.

**FIXED by pinning the module, not its parts.** The four declaration-slice pins become **ONE raw-Buffer pin over the whole of `ui/profile.js`: 17,566 bytes, SHA-256 `d6fba912fcb9edc146c0af3fd93203652b3e1acc533f39cbba11fe007996099e`.** Nothing is beside the pinned region in that module any more. No parser and no package: `node_modules` holds 36 packages, of which only `es-module-lexer` (import/export statements) and `@oxc-project/types` (declarations, no runtime) parse anything.

**(2) P1 — L17: the footer's rolling labels were demoted, which rewrote prior lines.** Both restored byte-for-byte to their `580a1a3` form; current status now sits in an additive block above them. Recorded for the next audit: the demotion had precedent in `d424253`, `446a187` and `580a1a3`, and was disclosed in Addendum 16 — so the convention and the restored form now conflict, and choosing between codifying a §13 carve-out and abandoning rotation is left open rather than settled here.

**(3) P1 — the counter-cases took their baseline from the LIVE file.** Each read the file, mutated a copy, and asserted the two differ. With a survivor **already on disk** both sides carry it: installing the nested-shadow survivor left its own named fixture **passing**, with only the generic handler-pin test red (1 of 32). The event-in-place fixture escaped by luck — that survivor happens to be behaviourally visible — while the nested-shadow one is invisible by construction. **Fixed at every site**: `cleanHtml()` / `cleanProfile()` assert the live source matches the **reviewed pin constant** first, and `pinFor` throws at import unless exactly one pin matches its label. Recorded plainly in the test file: a counter-case is a **demonstration against a clean tree**, not a standing detector; what detects a shipped survivor is the byte pin.

**(4) P2 — the read recording is FIRST-HOP ONLY; "member-path policy" is withdrawn.** Measured, not inferred. `strictAccess` has a single `get` trap and returns values un-proxied, so it records a **string-keyed `get` on the root object and nothing else**. Silent: nested reads (`e.target.ownerDocument` records `target` alone), symbol-keyed reads, `in`, `Object.keys` / `for…in` / `getOwnPropertyNames` / `Reflect.ownKeys`, `getOwnPropertyDescriptor`, and — **not in the finding as filed, and the larger hole** — **writes and `delete`, which have no trap and are neither recorded nor blocked**, including on the control, so `control.value = 'male'` would rewrite the value under test unseen. Spread IS recorded. Consequently v0.83's "(1) fails three independent ways" is narrowed: rewriting the flagship read as `Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(e,'target').value.ownerDocument` returns the same value and records nothing — verified, the mutation still goes red but that assertion stays **green**. Only the freeze and the byte pin generalise.

**(5) P2 — the byte counts were UTF-16 code units.** `String.length` counts units; §6's comments carry `§` and `β`. The handler body is **2,017 UTF-8 bytes**, recorded as 2,006; `resolveGenderSelect` is **1,137**, recorded as 1,135 (no longer pinned separately). The SHA-256 values were already over UTF-8 and did not move. Fingerprints are now computed over raw `Buffer`s, with a test asserting the decoded text re-encodes to the file's exact bytes.

**(6) P3 — correction.** v0.83 and the 2026-08-07 journal entry say "between the two" / "between the two consumers". Correct: **between production and both consumers** — the delete runs immediately after `buildSubmitOpts` returns, before either consumer is called.

**THE REPAIR IS NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT. Six bypasses were demonstrated against the REPAIRED guard**, each with every pin byte-identical, `SUBMIT_SIG` occurring exactly once, and the full suite green — three of them reproduced directly by this lane:

- **one of `index.html`'s seventeen import lines** swapped to a root shim re-exporting `buildProfile`, which deletes the field from the **live, unfrozen** options object one statement before `saveProfile` (a **−1 byte** edit to `index.html`);
- an inline `<script type="importmap">` remapping the pinned specifier, so `ui/profile.js` stays byte-identical on disk and **the page never loads it**;
- a second `initProfileUI({…, genderSelect: {value: ''}})` from the unpinned boot region, repointing the module's control at a dead stub while the visible control keeps working;
- `profileForm` rebound to a detached element, so the pinned bytes run and reach no user;
- a second submit listener under a different spelling calling `stopImmediatePropagation()`;
- a `defineProperty` shim on the live control's `value`, leaving the UI showing the user's choice.

**So, stated exactly:** the freeze closes in-place mutation **by the pinned handler body**, not the class. Identity coupling constrains what the handler passes on, not what the real consumers do with it. The whole-file pin closes any rebinding expressible in `ui/profile.js`'s own bytes; it does not establish that `ui/profile.js` is the module the page loads. The two `index.html` pins cover **2,270 of 69,373 bytes — 3.3% of the file, and 1 of its 17 import lines.**

**The residual, each entry naming the mechanism that closes it.** **Closed by byte identity:** the bytes of `ui/profile.js` entire, of the submit handler body, and of the seam's import specifier line. **Closed by the freeze:** mutation of the produced object by the pinned handler body. **Closed by identity coupling:** a delivered object that is not the produced object, as far as the handler. **Closed by dual-environment execution:** node/browser disagreement across the eight probes. **Closed by the read policy, first hop and string keys only:** a handler read on its event beyond `preventDefault`, or a producer read on its control beyond `value`, written as a direct property access. **Open, and closed by nothing here:** module *resolution* (importmap, a swapped specifier, any root file no test names); the other 96.7% of `index.html`, including its sixteen unpinned imports, its `const` bindings, its boot region and every other listener registration; listener registration and firing at all, including ordering and `stopImmediatePropagation`; a repeat `initProfileUI`; the live DOM control, which no drive touches; `resolveGenderSelect`, pinned but never executed because production boots `{ form, anchor }` while the drives pass `{ genderSelect }` and §12 forbids a DOM harness; second-hop, descriptor-routed, symbol-keyed, `has` and ownKeys-style reads; writes and deletes on either proxied ref; and a pin updated without review. **No generalized class is claimed closed on analysis of any kind.**

**The named next step, deliberately NOT taken here.** Pinning `index.html` whole-file the way `ui/profile.js` now is would kill five of the six, and adding reachability assertions — no importmap, every specifier resolving inside `./ui/` or `./core/`, the module inventory extended to the repo root — would close the remaining resolution class. Both are real work with real cost: `index.html` is the product's single host under active amendment, and a whole-file pin reds on every unrelated edit. That trade is a controller and audit decision, and shipping new unaudited guards inside a correction cycle is the failure mode this section exists to prevent. Also recorded and not adopted: `module.registerHooks` gives a module a genuine `https:` `import.meta.url` in-process with no network and no package — it is how the P1 divergence was demonstrated — but it is a new harness capability needing its own audit, and `engines.node >= 20.19` predates it.

**Product runtime is byte-unchanged** — `index.html`, `ui/`, `core/`, `content/` and `assets/` are identical to `446a187`. Feature freeze holds; this is tests and records.


**v0.83 amendment (the lexical surface is retired too; the absolute claim moves onto exact bytes, 2026-08-07).** Two P1s and a P2, all three reproduced in this repo before anything was edited. v0.82's positive free-identifier policy is **superseded**; nothing below it is edited, and its record stands as written.

**(1) An allowed root is a free doorway — member paths were never on the surface.** Inserted immediately after the real host call:

```
if (e.target && e.target.ownerDocument) delete opts[opts.constructor.keys(opts)[1]];
```

`e` was already on the pinned surface and property accesses are excluded from a free-identifier scan **by construction**, so the reported surface did not move; `Object` was reached as `opts.constructor`. Every other guard held: host call present exactly once, raw gender inventory byte-identical, one produced object, both consumers holding **that exact object** — and gender deleted out of it in place, between the two. The seam's event stub had no `target`, so the mutation was inert under the harness and live in a browser. **Full suite 57 files / 2029 tests GREEN**, and a real-event-shaped drive rendered with the field omitted.

**(2) `import.meta` distinguishes the harness from the shipped module and spells no free identifier.** At the top of the real producer:

```
if (import.meta.url.startsWith('http')) return undefined;
```

`import` is a keyword and every member name is discarded, so the producer's surface stayed exactly `['_genderSelect']`. Vitest loads modules from `file:`; the deployed page fetches them over `http(s):`. **Full suite GREEN.**

**(3) P2 — the policy was not merely under-listed, it was structurally wrong, and the claim built on it was an overclaim.** Its extractor collects every declaration into ONE body-wide locals set with no notion of scope, so a probe can be laundered by a declaration that does not shadow it: `if (typeof Image !== 'undefined') delete …;` followed by a dead `if (false) { const Image = null; }` subtracts `Image` globally. JavaScript does not shadow backwards. **Full suite GREEN.** `tests/helpers/js-lex.js` had stated in its own header, from the day it was written, that it is **SECONDARY, NOT PRIMARY** and cannot carry an absolute claim; v0.82 made it primary anyway. Accordingly v0.82's "closed: any spelled reference outside the pinned dependency surface", its "a scope that may reference only its own module-local binding cannot probe anything", and its residual of "listener registration + runtime-built indirection" were all **broader than the evidence**: member paths on an allowed root, `import.meta`, and flat-scope laundering are **spelled** survivors, not runtime-built indirection.

**FIXED by moving the absolute claim off analysis and onto exact bytes.** No lexer rule was added, no deny-list, no environment name, and no package. `codeOnly` and `freeIdentifiers` are **deleted** from `tests/helpers/js-lex.js`; `classify`/`functionBody`/`genderTokens` stay, still secondary, still used to say WHERE a read is.

- **SOURCE PINS — the primary guard, enumerating nothing.** Every function the selected value passes through is small and bounded, so each one's exact source is pinned by **length and SHA-256**: the submit handler body in `index.html` (2006 bytes — *the same extraction the driver executes*), the seam's import specifier line (253 bytes), and `buildSubmitOpts` (309), `getGenderInput` (143), `initProfileUI` (131) and `resolveGenderSelect` (1135) in `ui/profile.js`. A member path, an `import.meta` branch, a shadowed declaration, a runtime-built key and a whitespace change all land here identically — each is a byte that is not the reviewed byte. It also fails closed on the lexer itself: a mis-located or truncated extraction changes the pinned length and hash, where before it silently shrank what was scanned. A companion test pins that each signature is present exactly once and slices to a line-start close, so a rename cannot reduce a pin to a stale constant nobody checks.
- **A RUNTIME member-path policy.** The event and the gender control are `Proxy` refs that record every property read; the pinned read sets are exactly `['preventDefault']` and `['value']`. A new path fails because it was **read**, not because it was foreseen — and no declaration anywhere can launder a runtime read. The event is production-shaped (`type`, `isTrusted`, `target` with `ownerDocument`, `currentTarget`), deliberately: against the old bare `{preventDefault}` stub the P1 mutation silently no-op'd, so the harness would have "passed" a live bypass by being too poor to run it.
- **The produced option object is FROZEN before the handler receives it**, and its key list is compared after both consumers have had it. An in-place edit is now a strict-mode `TypeError`, not a silent field deletion. This is a harness fence and claims nothing about production, which is byte-unchanged.

**Counter-cases ship for all three survivors, and each states which assertion goes red.** (1) fails **three independent ways** — `eventReads` gains `target`, the frozen object throws `Cannot delete property 'gender'` so the run never reaches `showResult`, and the handler pin moves; the first two are what a blind hash update cannot launder. (2) and (3) fail on the pins alone, and each counter-case **asserts its own blindness premise** rather than implying coverage: for (2), that this harness's module URL is `file:` so the branch cannot fire here; for (3), that neither environment defines `Image` so the delete never executes. Re-verified by applying each survivor to the real tree: **(1) 21 of 32 red · (2) 2 of 32 red · (3) 1 of 32 red**, all green on restore. Three further probes of the new guard were run and each failed closed: a redirected import specifier, a whitespace-only handler edit, and an extractor forced to stop early.

**The residual, stated to the byte and no wider.** **Closed:** any change to the bytes of the six pinned units; any read the handler makes on its event beyond `preventDefault`, or the producer on its control beyond `value`; any mutation of the produced object; any delivered object that is not the produced object; and any node-vs-browser disagreement across the eight probes. **Open, and closed by nothing here:** the page is never loaded, so **listener registration and firing** rests on the manual browser pass — and a second listener registered by a different spelling (`profileForm.onsubmit = …`) is part of that same gap; **`resolveGenderSelect` is pinned but not executed**, because production boots with `{ form, anchor }` and builds the control while the drives pass `{ genderSelect }` and take its early return — §12 forbids a DOM harness, so its bytes and the browser pass are its whole cover; **module-level or other-function code in `ui/profile.js` that reassigns `_genderSelect`** at import time under an environment the harness cannot express; **symbol-keyed reads** on the proxied refs, which the access policy does not record and only the byte pins see; and **a pin updated without review**, which the behavioural half bounds but does not eliminate. A real-browser runner would close the first two. **No generalized class is claimed closed on analysis of any kind** — the closure above is byte identity, not inference.

**Product runtime is byte-unchanged** by this amendment — `index.html`, `ui/`, `core/`, `content/` and `assets/` are identical to `446a187`. Feature freeze holds; this is tests and docs.

Suite 57 files / 2039 tests green; product audit PASS 14/0/0/0 on a clean tree; assurance suite 102 tests OK; local PII scan clean, 863 files; `index.html` 1469/1500. **STAGED — cross-model audit before merge per §10/L48; merge is the controller's word.**


**v0.82 amendment (finite lists retired; the surface is pinned positively, 2026-08-06).** Two P1s, both verified, and together they retire the approach v0.81 was still using.

**(1) A finite enumeration of browser globals cannot close the class.** v0.81 shipped a shim installing seven names and a raw ban listing eleven. **`history` and `HTMLElement` are in neither.** A handler probing them selected a gender-free object and routed **both** consumers through it, with the pinned call surviving byte-for-byte once and the raw gender inventory byte-identical — **all 16 seam tests green**, and a real-browser-shaped run omitting the field. The same mutation showed a second thing: **the presence of the pinned call is not coupled to what the consumers receive.** v0.81's claim that the spelled environment class is closed is **superseded**.

**(2) The driver stubbed the very producer it was verifying.** Sabotaging the real `getGenderInput` — `if (typeof history !== "undefined") return undefined;` — left **246 of 246** relevant tests green, because the seam injected `getGenderInput: () => gender` in its place. A driver that replaces the thing under test proves only that its own stub works.

**FIXED structurally, with no new list.** Three changes, all in tests:

- **A POSITIVE free-identifier policy** over the bounded handler **and** the real producer. Every identifier a scope references without declaring is pinned; anything new fails — `history` fails **because it is new to the surface, not because it is forbidden**. The handler's surface is 28 names, the producer's is one (`_genderSelect`). This replaces the deny-list, which is deleted rather than extended. The extractor is deliberately conservative (object keys register as free), because over-reporting fails closed while under-reporting is a hole.
- **Direct coupling by IDENTITY.** The object returned by the pinned call is captured, and both consumers are asserted to receive *that reference* — not an equal shape — in both environments, with the option object built exactly once.
- **The REAL producer is driven**, wired to a stub control, instead of being replaced by one.

Counter-cases ship for every verified bypass: `history`, `HTMLElement` and `queueMicrotask` probes; the consumer-decoupling route; and the producer sabotage. Re-run against the repaired suite, the handler bypass fails the surface policy **and** both coupling assertions, and the producer sabotage fails the producer surface with `['_genderSelect', 'history']` — **caught without either environment defining `history`**, which is the point: the closure no longer depends on having anticipated the global.

**The residual, no broader than the evidence.** **Closed:** a spelled reference to anything outside the pinned dependency surface, in the handler or the producer, fails the suite; and the delivered object is the produced object. **Open and closed by nothing here:** the page is never loaded by any test, so **listener registration and firing** rests on the manual browser pass; **runtime-built indirection** that spells no identifier (`globalThis["hist" + "ory"]`) is reachable by neither the policy nor the stubbed runtime; and the surface policy is **lexical**, so it sees names, not values. A real-browser runner would close the first; nothing in this repo closes the second, and no persistent dependency was added to chase it.

**Product runtime is byte-unchanged** by this amendment — `index.html`, `ui/`, `core/`, `content/` and `assets/` are identical to `446a187`. Feature freeze holds.

Suite 57 files / 2029 tests green; product audit PASS 14/0/0/0 on a clean tree; local PII scan clean, 863 files; `index.html` 1469/1500. **STAGED — cross-model audit before merge per §10/L48; merge is the operator's word.**

**v0.70 amendment (the measurement contract — four events, no collector, 2026-08-06).** The product names the only four things it may ever count, fixes the shape a count may take, and counts none of them anywhere. `core/measurement.js` holds the list and one injectable local sink whose default is `null`. **This introduces no analytics, no tracker and no collector**, and §7 gate 7 ("no third-party / client telemetry, permanently") is unchanged and unqualified by it. The network primitives this section bans stay banned in tracked source — `tests/privacy_scan.test.js` scans `core/` and therefore scans this module too.

**The four events, and the list is CLOSED:** `reading_completed` (the denominator — without it every other figure is a rate with no base), `paid_t3_cta_clicked` (separates "nobody sees the offer" from "everybody sees it and declines" — opposite problems with opposite fixes), `comparative_opened` (the comparative is now half of what $3 buys per §1.D v0.68; if it is never opened the rung is mispriced against its own contents), and `share_completed` (the only reach signal the product can observe without tracking anyone). A fifth event is an amendment here, not an edit there.

**The payload is two fields and cannot be widened from a call site.** A record is `{ event, tier }` — `tier` from the §1.D vocabulary `free | t1 | t2 | t3`, which is device density and already the whole of what `eight_ball_tier_v1` stores. It is BUILT from an object literal rather than filtered down from a caller's object, so a name, DOB, gender, city, coordinate value or card string cannot reach it even if a call site passes one in; and `recordMeasurement` takes two positional values, so there is no object to over-populate. Explicitly absent, each for a reason: **no id, session token or counter** (nothing that can join two records into a sequence, which is the line between counting events and tracking a person); **no timestamp** (a timestamp is a join key, and ordering answers none of the four questions); **no URL, referrer, user agent, viewport or locale** (a fingerprint assembled from harmless fields is still a fingerprint). Two records of the same event on the same device are byte-identical, and a test asserts exactly that.

**Storage and network are untouched.** No localStorage key is added, read or written — the §5 allow-list is unchanged and needs no extension. Nothing is transmitted. The retired-token discipline is honored at the boundary too: a raw `t4` or `t5` handed straight in builds no record rather than emitting a rung that no longer exists.

**Where it fires, and how that is kept honest.** Four call sites: `renderCard` in `index.html` after a completed render; the CTA in `ui/payments.js`, resolved from inside the modal so the host gains no line and the Buy Link stays the plain bare href §5.B Call 2 requires; `ui/dyad.js` after the entitlement gate, so refused taps are not counted as opened screens; and `ui/share.js` on both delivery paths, inside the native-share `try` after the await so a dismissed sheet is not counted as a share. `ui/share.js` takes it through a **hook, not an import** — that module's §5.D posture is to import nothing and know no tier constant, and that posture wins; the host owns both halves and hands down a nullary callback, with a test pinning that the host actually wires it. **Three of the four sites are driven** against a recording sink through the real module functions — `comparative_opened` in `tests/dyad_surface.test.js` (entitled open, three refused rungs, a throwing host hook, a tier that moves mid-handler), `share_completed` in `tests/share_behavior.test.js` (native resolve, native dismiss, download fallback, clipboard failure, unwired host), and `paid_t3_cta_clicked` in `tests/measurement.test.js`. **`reading_completed` is pinned by source shape instead**, because `renderCard` lives inside `index.html`'s single inline module and no test harness executes it; extracting it to make it drivable would refactor the §6 single-file posture for a call site nobody disputes, and its live cover is the browser pass where the event was observed firing with the render tier. That split is recorded rather than smoothed over: an earlier draft of this clause claimed all four were exercised against the real functions when three were string matches, and one of those string matches was pinning a call order that was itself wrong. Claiming coverage one does not have is the defect this cycle spent its time removing.

**No collector is proposed, and the recommendation is to hold.** Adopting one would need this section amended to say what is collected and where it goes, a first-party server-side path (§5 v0.35 already permits reading the host's own request logs, which the `netlify.toml` channel routes already exploit with no client event at all), an about-modal disclosure before merge rather than after, and an argument that the existing signals are insufficient. They may not be. The standing finding on record is that reach, not conversion, is the binding constraint by roughly two orders of magnitude — at current volumes these counters would produce small integers with wide error bars, and a decision taken on them would be a decision taken on noise. The contract is worth having now because it is what stops an ad-hoc snippet being pasted in later under deadline; the collector is not worth having until the denominator is large enough for a rate to mean something. The reasoning is written out at `audits/measurement_plan_2026-08-06.md`.

**Network behavior is scope-restricted, not absolute** (v0.21 amendment). The product makes no telemetry calls, no third-party calls, no fingerprinting calls, no out-of-band data flow.

**First-party server-side observability is not telemetry** (v0.35 clarification). Passive aggregate request logs generated by the hosting platform (e.g. Netlify's own server-side analytics) are server-side, carry no client-injected tracker, no fingerprint, no third-party script, and no identifier the product emits. Reading the operator's own origin logs is permitted. The ban is on out-of-band / third-party data flow the product *creates* — not on the operator observing first-party traffic to their own site. This clarification adds no code surface and changes no scanner: `tests/privacy_scan.test.js` still forbids `fetch()`/`XMLHttpRequest`/`navigator.sendBeacon`/third-party scripts in tracked source, because first-party server logs are produced by the host platform, never by tracked source.

The following same-origin asset loads are permitted:

- **Initial page load.** HTML and ES modules served from the deployed origin, plus same-origin static assets (favicons, og:image).
- **Same-origin lazy loads.** Dynamic ES `import()` of same-origin JSON or modules. As of v0.2.7.2, `assets/cities.json` is lazy-loaded the first time the user opens the rising-sign `<details>` element. Lazy loads carry no user state — they fetch a static asset whose bytes are identical for every visitor.
- **§5.B user-initiated network calls.** Two outbound network calls, both user-initiated (see §5.B): (1) feedback form POST to Netlify Forms, (2) Gumroad Buy Link redirect for paid-tier unlock. Added v0.3.0 (LS path); swapped to Gumroad at v0.3.0.3.

`fetch()`, `XMLHttpRequest`, and `navigator.sendBeacon` remain forbidden in tracked source under `core/`, `content/`, and `index.html` — enforced by `tests/privacy_scan.test.js`. Dynamic `import()` with import attributes (`with: { type: 'json' }`) is the permitted same-origin lazy-load mechanism for static JSON; it does not trip the `fetch(` ban because it is an ES module-loader call, not an XHR.

The v0.14 wording "zero network requests after page load" is retired by this amendment. The intent — no third-party traffic, no telemetry, no out-of-band data flow — is preserved verbatim.

If a future feature requires storing or transmitting more, that feature requires a doctrine amendment to §5 and a privacy-policy update before merge.

## §5.B — User-initiated network calls (feedback v0.2.5, LS redirect v0.3.0 retired, Gumroad redirect v0.3.0.3)

The site permits exactly two user-initiated network calls:

1. **Feedback POST** — submission of a feedback form to the Netlify Forms endpoint at the same origin (added v0.2.5).
2. **Gumroad Buy Link redirect** — navigation away to the operator's hosted Gumroad checkout when the user taps the paywall CTA (added v0.3.0 as Lemon Squeezy path; swapped to Gumroad at v0.3.0.3).

Both calls are out-of-band — neither echoes user profile state. These are the only network calls permitted in the product runtime; everything else in §5 stands.

### Call 1 — Feedback POST

Constraints:

- **User-initiated only.** The submission fires only on user click of the explicit submit button. No timer, no auto-fire, no submission on page load, on shake, on result-render, or on any other implicit trigger.
- **User-authored content only.** The submitted payload contains exactly two fields: a free-text `message` and an optional free-text `contact`, both typed by the user inside the form. No localStorage profile data (`name`, `dob`, `time`, `country`, `lat`, `lng`, `city`, `cc`, `tz`) is included. No derived coordinates. No name, no DOB, no IP enrichment from the page, no analytics tags, no UTM params, no fingerprint. The form has no hidden field carrying user-data state.
- **Single named endpoint.** The `<form>` action is the same-origin Netlify Forms handler — either no `action` attribute (Netlify intercepts the POST and serves its branded thanks page) or `action="/?sent=1"` for a same-origin success redirect that the v0.2.5.2 banner JS detects and swaps to the in-page "thanks. read." state, then `replaceState` strips the query. The v0.2.5.2 success redirect is the shipped form (`tests/feedback_surface.test.js` locks `action="/?sent=1"`). No third-party form provider, no webhook fan-out, no secondary destination.
- **Native form post, no JavaScript fetch.** The form uses HTML `<form method="POST" data-netlify="true">` semantics. No `fetch()`, no `XMLHttpRequest`, no `navigator.sendBeacon`. The privacy scan (`tests/privacy_scan.test.js`) is unchanged and remains the enforcement.
- **Fail-silent.** On submission failure (offline, provider outage, network error), the browser handles the error natively. No retry queue, no remote error reporting, no telemetry of failure. If the user's browser is offline, the submit fails and the user can decide what to do.
- **Submission visibility.** Submissions land in the Netlify dashboard under the Forms tab. The operator may configure email notifications per Netlify defaults. Submissions are not echoed back to the page or persisted in localStorage.
- **No retention by 8ball.** 8ball-the-codebase does not retain submissions. Netlify-the-platform does (for as long as operator keeps them in the dashboard); that is operator's responsibility, not 8ball's runtime concern.

The about-modal copy is updated to disclose this surface honestly. Disclosure is the load-bearing piece: anyone reading the modal must learn that a feedback form exists and what happens when they submit it.

### Call 2 — Gumroad Buy Link redirect (v0.3.0 LS path → v0.3.0.3 Gumroad path)

User taps `unlock · $3` on the paywall modal → browser navigates to `https://theeightball.gumroad.com/l/rzqezp` (hosted by Gumroad, which acts as Merchant of Record). Unlike the v0.3.0 Lemon Squeezy path, Gumroad does not accept a URL-encoded `success_url` query parameter on the Buy Link href; post-purchase redirect to `/?paid=t1` is handled entirely on Gumroad's side via the product's Content-tab "→ unlock your reads" Button (configured 2026-05-16 chat-27 directive F) which links to `https://the-eight-ball.netlify.app/?paid=t1`. The redirect mechanism is therefore single-source (Gumroad-side Content Button), not the belt-and-suspenders pattern of the prior LS path. Two-click post-purchase flow: paywall tap → Gumroad hosted checkout → Content-tab Button tap → `/?paid=t1`.

Gumroad processes payment via Stripe (Gumroad's internal Stripe Connect integration; operator's verified Connect account cleared 2026-05-16 per `~/Desktop/8ball/audits/gumroad_post_confirm_recheck_2026-05-16.md`). PayPal optional at buyer choice per Gumroad's default checkout. On successful payment, Gumroad displays the product Content page with the "→ unlock your reads" Button; on Button-tap, browser navigates to `https://the-eight-ball.netlify.app/?paid=t1`. The page-load JS handler at the same `/?paid=t1` query parser calls `applyPaidReturn()` from `core/payments.js` to credit the device (`+= 3`) and — if `eight_ball_pending_profile_v1` is present — consume it (decrement one credit, save the profile, render unlocked), then `replaceState({}, '', window.location.pathname)` strips the query for a clean URL.

Constraints:

- **User-initiated only.** Tap of the explicit paywall CTA. No timer, no auto-fire, no redirect on page load, on shake, on result-render, or on any other implicit trigger.
- **Hosted redirect, not in-page overlay.** Plain `<a href>` navigation. The Gumroad overlay/embed script is permanently rejected per §5's third-party-script ban — same constraint as the prior LS path, operator decision logged in the v0.3.0.3 cycle commit.
- **No user data leaves on the redirect.** The Buy Link URL is static — no profile fields encoded, no UTM tags, no tracking identifiers appended. The bare URL contains no query parameters at all (distinct from the v0.3.0 LS path which carried URL-encoded `checkout[success_url]`); `tests/payments_markup.test.js` v0.3.0.3 assertions lock this shape.
- **No SDK, no `fetch`, no webhook.** 8ball-the-codebase never talks to Gumroad programmatically; the redirect IS the entire payment surface.
- **Two-click post-purchase return (Gumroad-specific).** The post-purchase flow is two clicks (paywall → Gumroad checkout → Content-tab Button → `/?paid=t1`), structurally different from the LS path's single-click `checkout[success_url]` redirect. The Content-tab Button text is operator-controlled and ships as "→ unlock your reads" per chat-27 directive F.
- **Trust-based return.** `/?paid=t1` is unsigned. Manual URL entry grants credits without payment (see §5.C). Acceptable for the arcade-toy model and disclosed in the about-modal.
- **No customer object retained.** Gumroad captures buyer email for its own receipt and tax compliance; 8ball never sees it. No localStorage entry for purchase metadata beyond `eight_ball_credits_v1` count.
- **Disclosure.** The about-modal names Gumroad as the payment processor and the data-flow boundary (payment + email to Gumroad; reading stays on-device). The paywall modal carries a compact `.modal-disclosure` line mirroring the same disclosure.

**v0.36 amendment (v0.6.0, tier ladder).** Call 2 generalizes to **three Gumroad products — one per rung**, same mechanism per product: t1 · $3 `https://theeightball.gumroad.com/l/rzqezp` → Content-tab Button → `/?paid=t1`; t2 · $6 `https://theeightball.gumroad.com/l/neysyv` → `/?paid=t2`; t3 · $9 `https://theeightball.gumroad.com/l/xjpvp` → `/?paid=t3`. Each paywall-ladder CTA is a plain bare-href Buy Link (no query parameters, no UTM, no tracking identifiers — `tests/payments_markup.test.js` locks all three shapes); the post-purchase redirect stays single-source on Gumroad's side via each product's Content-tab Button. The page-load handler is generalized to `?paid=t1|t2|t3`: `applyPaidReturn` credits the device +3 and raises the stored tier to max(current, purchased) per §1.D; unknown `?paid=` values are ignored (replay-safe branch preserved). All Call 2 constraints — user-initiated only, hosted redirect not overlay, no user data on redirect, no SDK/fetch/webhook, trust-based return, no customer object retained, disclosure — apply to each product unchanged. This remains one mechanism with three product instances; the §5.B "exactly two user-initiated network calls" enumeration is unchanged in kind (feedback POST + Gumroad Buy Link redirect).

**v0.55 amendment (ownership repricing, 2026-07-26).** The three products repriced on Gumroad 2026-07-26: **t1 · $1 / t2 · $2 / t3 · $3** — product IDs, Buy Link URLs, Content-tab Buttons, and `?paid=` params all unchanged; the v0.36 prices above are lineage. The base Call 2 prose's `unlock · $3` CTA copy and `+= 3` credit language are likewise lineage: the handler grants no credits — `applyPaidReturn` writes the monotonic tier (its entire effect) and hands back a pending profile if one is present. The listings now promise the purchase is permanent ("yours for good"); the code honours that per §1.D v0.55. The **trust-based return** constraint reads accordingly: manual `/?paid=tN` entry grants the tier flag without payment — still acceptable, still disclosed (§5.C). The **no customer object retained** constraint's "beyond `eight_ball_credits_v1` count" reads "beyond the `eight_ball_tier_v1` flag" — the legacy credit key persists only as the R2 grandfather signal. Everything else — mechanism, constraints, two-call enumeration — unchanged.

If a future feature would require a third endpoint, additional fields, or any non-user-initiated network behavior, that requires a further §5 amendment.

## §5.C — Content-delivery transparency (added v0.3.0)

The card-content layer (`content/cards.v1.full.js` — `name`, `type`, `habit`, `note` × 144 cards) ships in the public bundle. JS visibility is gated by the `eight_ball_credits_v1` flag; the data itself is inspectable via View Source, and `/?paid=t1` is not signed — manual URL entry will grant `+= 3` credits without payment. **The lock is a UI convention; the deck is in the source — we trust adults to tip when they enjoyed the toy.**

**v0.55 amendment (ownership, 2026-07-26).** The gate in the paragraph above is now the stored tier: JS visibility of the written entry is gated by `eight_ball_tier_v1` at t3 (the §1.D density rule; the credits flag survives only as the R2 grandfather signal). `/?paid=tN` remains unsigned — manual URL entry sets the tier flag, permanently, without payment. Same trust posture, now with a smaller blast radius in the other direction too: there is nothing to top up, so the only thing a replay can ever grant is a rung the toy sells for at most $3. Disclosure unchanged.

Backend-gated delivery (Netlify Functions + Stripe webhook verification, or any processor's server-validated license-key issuance) is doctrinally permitted as a future amendment but not built in v0.3.0. The about-modal discloses the bundle shape so payment is informed-tip, not deception. The disclosure line "the deck is visible in source; the lock is a convention, not a vault" is the load-bearing piece — users tip the operator for the toy, not for content scarcity.

## §5.D — Share surface (added v0.4.0)

The result surface MAY offer a user-initiated share affordance that renders the free card to a raster image (PNG) entirely on-device (SVG → canvas → `toBlob`; no network). Sharing uses the Web Share API where available (`navigator.share` with the image file); otherwise it falls back to a local file download plus a clipboard copy of the bare production URL. Invariants:

- **(a)** the shared artifact contains only the free coordinate symbols, their section labels, the catalog number, the 8ball wordmark, and the bare production URL — never the paid card-content layer (name/type/habit/note), never name or DOB;
- **(b)** no per-result link or query parameter encodes any profile field;
- **(c)** no telemetry, no share counting, and no network call of any kind is introduced — §5 same-origin/no-beacon and §7 no-telemetry are preserved;
- **(d)** the affordance is offered on the free surface to maximize organic reach.

The image builder reads only the rendered free coordinate symbol nodes (and their section titles + catalog number) from the live DOM; it never reads a profile object or the unlocked card-content slots, which is how invariants (a)/(b) are mechanically guaranteed. DOM-touching code lives in `ui/share.js` (`initShareUI({refs}, {hooks})` per §6), not in `index.html`. This clause is the privacy review contemplated by the §12 "until a privacy review explicitly clears it" wording.

**v0.36 amendment (v0.6.0, tier-aware share).** The share affordance renders the card **at the user's current render tier** — paid users emit denser PNGs. Invariant (a)'s "free coordinate symbols" generalizes to "the rendered coordinate symbols at the current tier"; every other protection in (a)–(d) is preserved verbatim and applies at every tier: labeled coordinate symbols + section labels + catalog number + 8ball wordmark + bare production URL only; never the written card-content layer (name/type/habit/note); never name or DOB; no per-result link or query parameter encoding any profile field; no telemetry, no share counting, no network call of any kind; affordance offered on the free surface (and every paid render). Mechanically the builder still reads only rendered coordinate symbol nodes from the live DOM and now skips tier-hidden rows (the `ui/tiers.js` gating sets them hidden); it still never reads a profile object or the card-content slots.

**v0.39 amendment (reach pass v1 — full-sheet share artifact).** The share artifact renders the FULL specimen sheet at every tier: the rendered coordinate values at the user's tier PLUS the SEALED STRUCTURE of the higher-tier compartments — section label + seal hatch, the coordinate VALUE absent. Rendering is **per compartment, not per row**: a mixed row (e.g. `SUN · RISING` on the free card) surfaces its open value beside its sealed compartment(s); the free PNG shows all 14 compartments (open values + seal hatches), mirroring the on-card v0.37 compartment sheet. This resolves the v0.37 logged deferral ("sealed-structure-in-PNG is a logged journal deferral; `ui/share.js` UNTOUCHED"). Every invariant (a)–(d) is preserved verbatim: a sealed coordinate's VALUE never appears in the artifact; never the written card-content layer (name/type/habit/note); never name or DOB; no per-result link or query parameter encodes any profile field; no telemetry, no share counting, no network call of any kind; the affordance is offered on the free surface. Invariant (a)'s "rendered coordinate symbols at the current tier" generalizes to "the rendered coordinate values at the current tier PLUS the seal-hatch structure (section label only, value absent) of the tier-hidden compartments." The mechanical guarantee is unchanged and is what makes the added structure safe: the builder reads ONLY the live DOM, where a sealed cell renders `textContent === ''` (the v0.37 compartment contract — absent, not hidden), so the sealed VALUE does not exist in the snapshot the builder serializes; `ui/tiers.js shareRowRefs` exposes a per-cell `{state, value}` snapshot derived from that same live cell state, never from a profile object or a tier constant (the prior tier-hidden row filter in `ui/share.js` is removed). NEW — share caption: the artifact MAY be accompanied by a clinical caption (the catalog numeral + the open coordinate VALUES + a `sealed remainder` marker + the bare production URL) carried in `navigator.share`'s text field where supported, and copied to the clipboard on the desktop fallback in place of the prior bare-URL-only copy. The caption is built from the SAME DOM snapshot and is bound by every (a)/(b) invariant — no name/DOB, no profile read, no per-result parameter, no sealed coordinate value — and stays in the §2 clinical register (no sales language). Sibling tracked-file changes co-versioned: `ui/share.js` (full-sheet builder — sealed cells draw an SVG `<pattern>` hatch + label, value absent; the per-cell `sealed` state replaces the tier-hidden row filter; `buildCaptionFromSnapshot` + caption coupled into the share flow), `ui/tiers.js` (`shareRowRefs` per-cell `{state, value}` snapshot, DOM-derived; the sealed value is never passed), `index.html` (one-line cold-landing `#mechanism-strip` near the registry header — clinical, no pricing/CTA; share-wiring comment updated; 1499/1500 held by offsetting compaction), `tests/share_surface.test.js` (full-sheet eight-row render, sealed = hatch + label + no value, aggregate paid-value-absence sentinel, F4 unresolved-vs-sealed in the PNG, caption PII/voice, share-flow caption coupling, mechanism-strip placement/no-CTA). Ships within the 0.7.x line — `package.json` deliberately not bumped (flagged for Procedure 4 version-truth review, as in v0.38). No calc change, no `content/` deck touch, no `core/` touch, no new dependency, no new localStorage key, no network call introduced — `privacy_scan` FORBIDDEN list + `LOCALSTORAGE_KEY_ALLOW` unchanged; `pii_scan` DOCTRINE_ALLOW unchanged. Trigger per §13: reach pass — the verified reach bottleneck (the 06-15 ship-gate read; orchestrator dissent on record since v0.38). Codex pre-build audit cleared this scope 2026-06-29 (P1 hook H2 → this amendment; disposition `~/Desktop/8ball/audits/codex_prebuild_reach_pass_v1_2026-06-29_response.md`); brief `~/Desktop/8ball/sessions/brief_reach_pass_v1_2026-06-29.md`.

## §5.E — Saved Readings (v0.50)

The result surface MAY offer `save reading` after a completed render. Saving is explicit and user-initiated; generating, rehydrating, sharing, opening the archive, or loading the page never saves an archive entry implicitly. The Previous Readings screen lists entries newest-first by `savedAt` and permits reopen, inline rename, confirmed individual deletion, and confirmed clear-all. `forget this device` clears both the current `eight_ball_profile_v1` payload and the complete Saved Readings archive.

**Storage minimum.** Each entry stores only `{ id, title, savedAt, profile }`. `profile` is a copy of the user-entered reconstruction payload allowed under §5; `id`, `title`, and `savedAt` exist only to identify, label, and order the local archive. Exact input profiles deduplicate to one saved entry. Derived coordinates, catalog/card output, card prose, entitlement tier, credits, tries, purchase metadata, and share artifacts are never copied into the archive.

**Revisit contract.** Opening a saved entry makes its profile the current local profile and recomputes the reading through the existing `profileFromPayload` / `buildProfile` / `showResult` path at the device's current entitled density. It does not call or alter the new-reading state machine, does not increment `eight_ball_tries_used_v1`, and does not decrement `eight_ball_credits_v1`. This is the same deterministic-rehydration class as cold-boot profile restoration and same-pair idempotence, not a new calculation or entitlement path. `core/`, `content/`, and `tests/fixtures.json` remain untouched.

**Failure and retention behavior.** Missing storage reads as an empty archive. Malformed outer JSON fails closed and is never silently overwritten by Save; the UI offers clear-all as recovery. Partially malformed arrays display only valid entries and disclose that some entries could not be read; the archive remains read-only until clear-all so an ordinary mutation cannot silently discard unreadable entries. Read, write, quota, or removal errors never crash generation or the calculation pipeline; the UI reports that saving/clearing is unavailable. Entries persist until the user deletes them, clears all, uses `forget this device`, clears site data, or the browser evicts local storage.

**Privacy boundary.** No account, backend, sync, analytics event, remote database, third-party storage, new endpoint, `fetch`, `XMLHttpRequest`, or beacon is introduced. The about modal and Previous Readings screen disclose the device-only scope and deletion controls. `tests/privacy_scan.test.js` allow-lists exactly `eight_ball_saved_readings_v1`; any second archive key or broader payload requires another §5 amendment.

## §5.F — Registry + Concordance (v0.51)

Concordance uses the Saved Readings archive as its only selector. Selecting two entry ids is transient UI state held in memory only. Comparison does not add a localStorage key, add a field to `eight_ball_saved_readings_v1`, create pair history, cache derived coordinates, store relation output, create a share artifact, or alter either saved entry. Closing or reloading the page discards the selection and result. The existing archive retention and deletion controls in §5.E remain the complete data-retention surface.

Both entries are validated by the existing archive loader and recomputed through the same calculation pipeline before comparison. Corrupt or partially valid archives preserve §5.E's fail-closed/read-only behavior; only valid displayed entries may be selected. If reconstruction or relation lookup fails, the UI reports that comparison is unavailable and leaves storage unchanged. Blocked/unavailable storage yields no selectable entries and does not affect ordinary reading generation. No account, backend, sync, analytics event, remote database, third-party storage, endpoint, network call, runtime dependency, or calculation-engine change is introduced. `tests/privacy_scan.test.js`'s key allow-list is unchanged from v0.50.

## §6. Architecture

- Single repo, private on GitHub as of v0.2.0 (was public through v0.1.4; flipped private to protect the future paid card-content layer at `~/dev/8ball-private/`).
- **Visibility correction (2026-08-01; L17).** The sentence above is preserved as historical lineage but superseded as current state: the GitHub repository is **PUBLIC**, verified against authenticated GitHub state on 2026-08-01. The separate authoring source at `~/dev/8ball-private/` remains private. Repository visibility is not content security: §1 v0.22 already records that the public bundle contains the full deck bytes and that its lock is a UI convention.
- Static site. Deployed on Netlify Pro ($20/mo) from `main` branch. (Was misdocumented as free tier; corrected 2026-07-20 to match 8BALL.md / README ops truth — mechanical, no product change.)
- ES modules in the browser. No build step.
- Four-folder source layout: `core/` (pure-logic ES modules), `ui/` (DOM-touching ES modules with `init*UI({refs}, {hooks})` DI shape per the v0.23 amendment below), `content/` (versioned static data — card deck, coordinate meanings, and the §1.I concordance registry), `tests/` (gates + `fixtures.json`). This clause pins the layout *shape*, not the file list — **v0.44 correction: no per-folder counts are hardcoded here anymore.** They drifted twice already (v0.41's "6 ui / 24 tests" was stale within two content cycles, per the 2026-07-04 drift-sweep) precisely because this clause restates a number it says elsewhere shouldn't live here. The canonical module/test inventory lives in `CLAUDE.md` (with `README.md`) — that is the single place to check current counts; this clause will not restate them again. Plus `index.html` and config. The private location at `~/dev/8ball-private/cards.v1.full.js` remains the authoring source; the public `content/cards.v1.full.js` is a verbatim copy maintained via `cp` at release-prep time.
- The single-file rule: `index.html` may not exceed 1500 lines. If it would, split into `ui/*.js` modules. Not before. **v0.23 amendment: the split-target shape is locked. A `ui/*.js` module is an ES module with pure exports (no DOM, vitest-testable without jsdom) alongside DOM-touching helpers gated by an `init*UI({refs}, {hooks})` injection point. Host-side `let` bindings stay in `index.html`; the module mutates them via host-injected setter callbacks (e.g. `setCurrentProfile`, `setSelectedCity`). Module-internal localStorage keys are bare-string `const KEY = '...'` definitions inside the module so `tests/privacy_scan.test.js`'s same-file identifier resolution catches them. Concrete precedents: `ui/payments.js` (v0.3.0 step 6, 165 LOC) and `ui/profile.js` (v0.3.0 step 7, 153 LOC). Future splits inherit the shape rather than re-deriving it.**

## §7. CI gate

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on every push and PR to main. The gate has these stages:
