1. **Calculation contract + engine pipeline** — `tests/profile.test.js` must pass (calc cases against `tests/fixtures.json`, `getCard` pipeline across the 144 positional catalog cells, `resolveBracket` cases). **v0.22 extension:** additionally verifies `content/cards.v1.full.js` parses as an ES module exporting `CARDS` with exactly 144 entries (12 sun signs × 12 animals), and runs the existing `BANNED_VOICE_REGISTER` scan against the deck's content strings (`name`, `type`, `habit`, `note.{low, mid, high}` fields). **v0.44 extension:** `tests/meanings_content.test.js` runs the same `BANNED_VOICE_REGISTER` + `BANNED_PATTERNS` tables (imported from `tests/profile.test.js`, not duplicated) against every `content/meanings.v1.js` entry, plus a completeness check against `SUN_SIGNS`/`ANIMALS`/`MAJOR_ARCANA` and a zero-tolerance second-person-address scan (§1.G). **Coverage catch-up (2026-07-20; documents shipped test reality, no rule change — see journal):** stage 1's enumeration above under-describes the live §2 voice-register surface — the same banned-term scans also run against the concordance registry and its assembled output (`tests/concordance.test.js`, #101/#107), the provenance placards (`tests/provenance.test.js`), and the atlas legend (`tests/atlas.test.js`), and as of the #104→#109 chain every `BANNED_VOICE_REGISTER`-based scan routes through the shared case-insensitive substring matcher `voiceRegisterHits()` with its explicit `SUBSTRING_SAFELIST`, while the second-person / diagnostic-framing regexes and the word-bounded `BANNED_PATTERNS` are separate voice-policy checks the helper also owns, applied only by the scans that name them (deck / meanings / concordance — not provenance/atlas) — `tests/helpers/voice-register.js` is now the canonical table home, superseding the `tests/profile.test.js` attribution in the v0.44 note above (preserved verbatim per L17).
   **v0.53 extension:** `tests/meanings_content.test.js` also checks all five `content/meanings.v2.js` element entries, all fourteen context roles, and every number-theme alias for shape, completeness, the canonical banned register/slur tables, and zero second-person address; `tests/meanings_behavior.test.js` pins the deterministic three-theme synthesis and the absence of derivation copy in resolved panels.
   **v0.54 extension:** calculation fixtures and direct tests pin strict `1..9` reduction across all six resolved numerology outputs and `null` for an empty contributing letter subset; tier tests require `—` for null/zero/out-of-range inputs so no tenth number can render; bracket/facet tests reject retired master values; meaning tests require exactly nine active entries and numerology-only partners; Concordance tests require all 36 distinct `1..9` pairs to remain unfiled and reject values outside the active range.
   **v0.62 extension (calc v4).** The v0.54 extension is superseded; its text stands as lineage per L17. Every gate above is re-pointed at the twelve-value terminal domain, and each is stated as the PAIR it has to be, because a domain widening is the one change an all-negative assertion greens on: calculation fixtures and direct tests pin the master-stop reduction (`29 → 11`, `38 → 11`, `44 → 8`) and a 5,000-value sweep that nothing escapes the domain, alongside the retained `null` guard; tier tests require `—` for null/zero/out-of-range inputs **and** require `11 / 22 / 33` to render as themselves on all six numerology cells; bracket/facet tests pin masters to `high`/third position, pin bracket and anchor agreement across the whole domain, and still reject `10 / 12 / 44`; meaning tests require exactly twelve active entries, byte-identity of the three master entries with immutable v1, and still refuse a value the calculator cannot produce; Concordance tests require exactly three registered master-reduction links out of 66 distinct pairs with the other 63 unfiled and none `adjacent`; dyad tests pin master inputs, master combined outputs, and the `sum !== combined` frame rule; public-tier tests pin that the birthday keeps its master value, that the mode table stays nine entries, that the bridge is reported in the returned reading, and — the pin the first implementation lacked — that the note is RENDERED for both reachable master birthdays on the host block and on both dyad sheets, cleared on the sealed branch, on `clear()`, and on a following unbridged render. The facet-key migration pin covers both retired generations (`_v1` and `_v2`), and `LOCALSTORAGE_KEY_ALLOW` adds `eight_ball_facet_index_v3` while retaining both retired names for the one-shot clear.
2. **Privacy scan** — `tests/privacy_scan.test.js` must pass (no unpermitted network calls — only §5-permitted same-origin lazy loads and §5.B user-initiated calls are allowed; no third-party fonts/scripts; system fonts only). **v0.22 extension:** `LOCALSTORAGE_KEY_ALLOW` extended with `eight_ball_tries_used_v1`, `eight_ball_credits_v1`, `eight_ball_pending_profile_v1` per §5. **v0.49 extension:** the allow-list also includes the PII-free current-position key `eight_ball_facet_index_v1` per §1.H / §5. **v0.50 extension:** the allow-list adds exactly `eight_ball_saved_readings_v1`; `tests/readings.test.js` locks its minimal schema, save/load/deduplication, rename, deletion, clear-all, corruption, and unavailable-storage behavior. **v0.54 extension:** the allow-list adds the versioned `eight_ball_facet_index_v2` and retains `eight_ball_facet_index_v1` solely because `ui/payments.js` still names the retired key for its one-shot calc-v3 clear; `tests/facet_rotation.test.js` pins the migration (a stale pre-v3 position never renders; `forget` scrubs both generations). **v0.55 amendment:** the allow-list retires `eight_ball_tries_used_v1` — the ownership model (§5 v0.55) reads and writes no such key; retired-key precedent v0.48.

**v0.51 test extension.** `tests/concordance.test.js` pins the complete finite registered inventory, matching-axis status/output shape, paid element entitlement, transient retention marker, host-side `profileFromPayload` reconstruction, no profile mutation, and absence of storage/network capability. The privacy allow-list remains unchanged; `core/` and `tests/fixtures.json` must have an empty feature diff.
3. **PII scan** — `tests/pii_scan.test.js` must pass (operator-name leakage, SIRR cross-reference leakage, labeled-DOB leakage). **v0.22 extension:** scans `content/cards.v1.full.js` for the same banned patterns; no DOB strings should appear in card content but the scan codifies the invariant.
4. **Dependency discipline** — `tests/dependency_discipline.test.js` must pass (npm runtime dependencies absent/empty, no build step, devDependencies ≤ 5).
5. **Single-file rule** — `index.html` ≤ 1500 lines.
6. **Payments state machine** (added v0.3.0) — `tests/payments_state.test.js` must pass (`isNewPair`, `nextShakeState`, `applyPaidReturn` transitions covering credits, cap, paywall trigger, replay-attack `no-pending` branch, same-profile idempotence, pending-profile round-trip). **v0.49 extension:** `tests/facet_rotation.test.js` covers `nextFacetState`, life-path anchors, strict round-robin rotation, current-position persistence, debit coupling, t3-only host wiring, and the zero-credit no-hidden-burn path (§1.H). **v0.55 amendment:** both suites rewritten to the ownership model — `nextShakeState` renders unconditionally (render / render-idempotent only), `applyPaidReturn` covers the monotonic tier write + pending render with no credit grant, the R2 grandfather and replay-safe `no-pending` branches stay pinned, and `tests/facet_rotation.test.js` covers unfunded always-advance rotation; the credit/cap/paywall-trigger transitions above are lineage. Density assertions in `tests/tiers.test.js` must keep proving the four distinct §1.D surfaces (free = 5 of 15 · t1 = 10 · t2 = 14 · t3 = 15 + `cardEntry`). Markup-static assertions for the paywall surface in `index.html` are covered by `tests/payments_markup.test.js` (counted under stage 5's file budget; runs in the same vitest pass).

**v0.61 extension (the dyad rung, §1.J).** Three suites join the gate, and they map onto the stages above rather than forming a fourth thing. **Stage 1** gains `tests/dyad.test.js` (the relation engine: determinism, table totality with every `kind` re-derived from the sheng/ke cycles rather than trusted, the byte-identity of each single side, and the branch-pair expansion — the one remaining deliberate fork, pinned against its original `ui/concordance.js` table walk by a differential test, because `core/` must not import `ui/`. The day master is NOT a fork — the F4 remediation (line 153 above) reads it off `profile.dayPillar` rather than recomputing it, and the suite pins CONSUMPTION, a supplied pillar that contradicts the raw date still winning, not a second calculation agreeing with the first; this paragraph called it a fork for a day after that fix landed, and PR #187 P2-R4 is the correction.) and the dyad half of `tests/content_shape.test.js` (the §1.J grammar pins, held to the same classes of rule the 144-card deck is). **Stage 1's voice surface** gains `tests/dyad_content.test.js`, which runs the canonical `voiceRegisterHits` / second-person / diagnostic-framing / `BANNED_PATTERNS` tables over both the frozen tables and the ASSEMBLED runtime output (the #101/#107 convention), plus two scans this surface needs and no single-reading surface does: a compatibility-claim scan and a person-as-subject scan, each carrying a positive-fire sentinel so neither can false-green. **Stages 2 and 6** gain `tests/dyad_surface.test.js`: no new `localStorage` key and no network capability in `ui/dyad.js`, the fail-closed offer (`T5_PRODUCT_URL` empty ⇒ no `href`, hidden CTA), the §1.D v0.37 sealed-DOM contract driven through a real render, and the ladder append itself — `RETIREMENT_COLLISIONS` empty, a stored `t4` still migrating to `t3`, the R2 grandfather still capped at `t3`, and the §1.F census identical at t3 and t5. Density assertions in `tests/tiers.test.js` keep proving the four distinct §1.D surfaces; t5 adds no coordinate, so the four-surface count is unchanged and that is itself pinned.

(History note: the `cards.v*.js` content-scan retired in v0.2.0 when the deck moved private; it returns as of v0.3.0 against the new public-tracked `content/cards.v1.full.js` — see stage 1's v0.22 extension and stage 3's. The private content-authoring pipeline at `~/dev/8ball-private/` continues to run its own scans before each release-prep `cp` to the public path.)

Netlify is configured to auto-deploy on push to `main`. CI runs in parallel on the same push. Failed CI does not currently block the auto-deploy itself — this gap is acknowledged and acceptable while traffic is operator-only. Wiring a Netlify required-check on the GitHub Actions status is queued for the first traction milestone (see `journal.md`); at that point, this paragraph gets re-tightened to "a failed gate blocks the deploy."

**v0.43 amendment (A3 — Netlify-required-check trigger bound per §13).** "The first traction milestone" above is an unobservable park, against §13 v0.35's named-trigger rule. Bind it to the concrete proxy already used as the journal's ship-gate: **≥5 paid purchases plus ≥1 strong divination-niche signal**. Until that threshold is met, the failed-CI-does-not-block-deploy gap is an **indefinitely-accepted gap** while traffic is operator-scale — not parked work awaiting an unnamed event. (At the mid-June ship-gate read the count was 0 sales — reach is the verified bottleneck — so the trigger is nowhere near firing; this is wording precision, not a deploy-risk change.) When the threshold is met, the paragraph above re-tightens to "a failed gate blocks the deploy." Per L17 the original wording is preserved verbatim above.

## §8. Release ritual

Every release, however small, has automated gates and ritual gates.

**Automated gates (CI, blocking):**

1. CI green (6 stages — calc+pipeline / privacy / PII / dependency / single-file / payments-state). See §7 for the per-stage breakdown.
2. **Doctrine/content change requires journal entry.** PRs touching `DOCTRINE.md` or `content/*.js` must also touch `journal.md`. Enforced in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

**Ritual gates (operator/reviewer responsibility):**

3. PR opened with a one-line summary. (PR title discipline; CI doesn't validate title quality.)
4. Operator runs local PII audit (`audits/run_local_audit.sh`). The public CI cannot see the operator's personal data; the local audit closes that gap. Skipping it is the failure mode, so it's a checklist item, not a vibe.
5. Reviewer reads the diff. Asks: any new line cross §4? Any new path cross §5?
6. Cross-model audit on doctrine or content changes. See §10. Solo authority IS the failure mode. Doctrine and content changes go through Codex (or ChatGPT for content batches) before merge. Mechanical edits do not.
7. Merge.
8. Append to `journal.md` with the `===== YYYY-MM-DD · Title =====` shape: what shipped, what was rejected, what's deferred.
9. **Live-fire on local deploy preview.** Static audit cannot simulate DOM/CSS or engine runtime. Releases touching `index.html`, `core/engine.js`, or content batches require an operator-run local-server pass against the changed surface before merge. Phase-2F-1 made this load-bearing — the flip-state inversion bug was invisible to Codex's static audit and only surfaced when the operator shook the deploy preview.
10. Confirm Netlify auto-deployed. Open the live URL. Shake it.

**v0.43 amendment (A1 self-firing rule-kill step + B4 journal-header shape).** Two corrections to the ritual above:
- **Self-firing rule-kill step (A1, see §13).** Folded into gate 8 (the journal append, where the last-review date is both read and written): **at every doctrine-touching ship, if the newest rule-kill review entry in `journal.md` is more than 30 days old, run the §13 rule-kill review as part of this close-out and log it.** This is the event-based accelerator for the §13 cadence — the monthly date is the floor, the ship-event is the accelerator. The check is mechanical (read the newest review date from `journal.md`), not a remembered cadence.
- **Journal-header shape (B4).** Gate 8's `===== YYYY-MM-DD · Title =====` example names a retired header shape; the live journal uses `## YYYY-MM-DD — Title — STATUS`. Read the example as that shape. Per L17 the `=====` form is preserved verbatim above and superseded here.

## §9. The SIRR boundary

Tracked content of this repo MAY NOT contain the string "SIRR" (case-sensitive) or any SIRR-domain vocabulary (`sirr.studio`, `abjad`, `hebrew_gematria`, etc.) outside the boundary-naming files allow-listed in `tests/pii_scan.test.js` (`DOCTRINE_ALLOW`). SIRR is a sibling project, not a parent or child of `8ball`; the two repos share no code and no imports. Enforced by `tests/pii_scan.test.js`.

## §10. Multi-model lane system

**The author-judge problem is real.** A single instance writing AND reviewing its own work has a structural blind spot. SIRR's session L4 named this directly: "Solo authority on doctrine IS the failure mode."

For 8ball at its current scale, the system runs **five agent roles plus one always-on controller**. Full role docs live at `~/dev/8ball/agents/*.md`; platform-specific constraints at `~/dev/8ball/agents/PLATFORMS.md`; this § is the constitutional summary.

**Core agent roles** (v0.29):

| Role | Tool | Role doc | Read access | Write access |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Orchestrator** — context, briefs, dispositions, sequencing | Claude (chat) | [`agents/orchestrator.md`](agents/orchestrator.md) | Everywhere in `~/dev/8ball/` and `~/Desktop/8ball/` | Pragmatic for fast initial work; routes through implementer for production-grade edits |
| **Implementer** — multi-file production edits, git ops, repo filesystem | Claude Code (CC) — `/usr/local/bin/claude` | [`agents/implementer.md`](agents/implementer.md) | Repo + `~/Desktop/8ball/` | Repo + `~/Desktop/8ball/` |
| **Auditor** — adversarial pre-merge review on doctrine, content, release-gates | Codex — Mac desktop app, paste-and-relay | [`agents/auditor.md`](agents/auditor.md) | Pasted briefs only | Returns categorized verdicts (PASS / P3 / P2 / P1 / P0) |
| **Verifier** — live UX on deployed product, structured findings + screenshots | Claude in Chrome (CiC) — browser extension | [`agents/verifier.md`](agents/verifier.md) | Per-session domain allowlist (default = read-only/static; non-default requires controller approval) | Reports text + screenshots only; no irreversible action clicks |
| **Inspector** — read-only state on operator infrastructure dashboards (Gumroad, Netlify, GitHub, social), structured observations | Claude in Chrome (CiC) + osascript + operator-screenshot adjacents | [`agents/inspector.md`](agents/inspector.md) | Operator's own dashboards (project-operational scope, never personal accounts); per-session CiC allowlist for non-default domains | Reports observations only; no irreversible action clicks, no credentials, no payment entries, no terms acceptance |

**Controller** (always-on, never an agent): the **Operator**. Sole authority for merges, deploys, payments, account changes, sharing-permission changes, TOS acceptance, identity verification flows, and any irreversible third-party action. Full role doc at [`agents/controller.md`](agents/controller.md).

**Adjunct lanes** (not core agents; invoked ad-hoc, no project-specific role doc): **ChatGPT** for content/copy review (paste-relay); **Perplexity** for web search; **Gemini** for second opinion. Per `~/MUHAB.md` §3.

**Lane discipline (v0.24):**

- The orchestrator does NOT autonomously override §4 or §5. Borderline content gets flagged for ChatGPT review before merge.
- Doctrine amendments go through the auditor before merge. Mechanical edits do not.
- Periodic corpus drift-sweep audits run when state-doc age + cumulative shipped surface change warrant it. Drift-sweep brief shape, paste workflow, verdict format, and pre-budget-absorb expectation codified at `agents/auditor.md` Procedure 6. (v0.27)
- All filesystem and git operations route to the implementer for any change touching ≥3 files or modifying `core/`. Single-file documentation tweaks may stay in chat.
- Live-UX verification routes to the verifier post-deploy-preview (pre-merge for surface changes) and post-merge for the first 24–48h on production for any significant ship.
- Operational-dashboard state reads route to the inspector when canonical files (`8BALL.md`, journal) need to assert state of an operator-controlled third-party surface — ship-gate verification, processor activation banner state, deploy-status confirmation, social-profile state. Inspector directives originate from a named canonical-file-consumption purpose; never open-ended exploration. (v0.29)
- The controller is always the final approver. No agent auto-merges. Per L48: explicit audit-cleared signal before merge; five-minute-CI-green-to-merge windows are the L48 failure shape.

For per-cycle artifact locations (briefs, audits, directives, reports), see `agents/PLATFORMS.md` "Artifact-location matrix". For SIRR-specific lane discipline, see `~/dev/SIRR/SIRR.md` §7.

## §11. PII rule

Personal data of the operator, family, friends, or any other identifiable person is NEVER tracked content. The repo is private as of v0.2.0, but this rule is independent of repo visibility — tracked content is the durable boundary, not the current ACL state. Repos can flip; the rule survives the flip.

**Visibility correction (2026-08-01; L17).** The historical current-state sentence above is superseded: the repository is **PUBLIC**, verified against authenticated GitHub state on 2026-08-01. The PII rule is unchanged and remains deliberately visibility-independent; public visibility makes the tracked-content boundary immediately observable, not newly applicable.

Specifically forbidden in any tracked file:

- The operator's full name in association with personal data (DOB, address, ID numbers).
- Test fixtures using real-person DOBs labeled to a real person.
- Any string matching combination patterns: `(operator-name) + (YYYY-MM-DD)` within 40 characters.
- SIRR cross-references outside of explicit boundary-naming doctrine.

The `tests/pii_scan.test.js` enforces a public-banned subset. The local audit at `audits/LOCAL_PII_AUDIT.md` closes the gap for personal data the public CI cannot know about.

**Fixture DOB rule (sub-rule of §11):** Fixture DOBs are chosen for the calc path they exercise, not anchored to any real person. If a calc path accidentally hits a real-person DOB, the fixture date is shifted by 12 years (preserves Chinese zodiac animal and life path mod-9 distribution while breaking real-DOB anchor) and the calc path is re-verified.

## §12. Out of scope

Two tiers. **[PERMANENT]** = load-bearing invariant (privacy, no-backend, no-oracle, SIRR); relaxing it changes what the object is — do not revisit. **[GUARDED]** = parked behind a named evidence trigger, not forbidden forever; revisit only on that trigger, never as idle feature-creep. (v0.35.)

- **[PERMANENT]** Account systems
- **[PERMANENT]** Email capture
- **[PERMANENT]** Push notifications
- **[PERMANENT]** AI-generated trait phrases at runtime (latency, cost, repeatability all break)
- **[GUARDED]** Multi-language until v2 of the trait pool exists in any language
- **[GUARDED]** Astrology charts, planetary aspects, anything beyond sun sign + optional rising sign — revisit only if it serves the object, never as feature-creep, and never in a way that crosses into horoscope/guidance identity
- **[PERMANENT]** Daily/weekly horoscopes ("today's reading")
- **[PERMANENT]** Sharing/social-card export of unconstrained content. The original wording — "Sharing/social-card export until a privacy review explicitly clears it" — is preserved here as lineage: that privacy review landed at v0.31 (§5.D), clearing a user-initiated, client-side, no-network export of the **free** card (labeled coordinate symbols + catalog number, no paid content, no PII). Permanently out of scope remains any export that carries the paid card-content layer (name/type/habit/note), any PII (name/DOB), or any per-result link / query parameter encoding a profile field.
- **[PERMANENT]** Anything touching the SIRR engine, codebase, or vocabulary

**v0.43 amendment (A2 — [GUARDED] gated-work pass, P1; corrects a 3-review-inherited error).** §13 v0.35 requires every [GUARDED] item to carry a NAMED, OBSERVABLE evidence trigger. There are exactly two; both fail it and are corrected here. Per L17 the two [GUARDED] lines above are preserved verbatim and superseded:
- **Astrology charts, planetary aspects → reclassified [GUARDED] → [PERMANENT].** Not genuinely guarded: §12 already permanently bans the identity they belong to — the [PERMANENT] header names *no-oracle* as a load-bearing invariant whose relaxation "changes what the object is," and "[PERMANENT] Daily/weekly horoscopes" bans the reading/guidance register. Charts and planetary aspects ARE that horoscope/guidance identity. The only genuinely-guarded astrology surface — optional rising sign — already shipped and cleared (§1.A). Relaxing the line would change what the object is, so it is [PERMANENT], not parked.
- **Multi-language trigger → re-keyed to a live observable.** Its trigger "until v2 of the trait pool exists in any language" references the trait-pool / template-pool system (`traits.v1.js` / `templates.v1.js`) **retired and DELETED in Phase-2F-2** (§4). A condition keyed to re-versioning a deleted, never-recurring system can never fire — the exact "parked with no defined release condition = drift" case §13 v0.35 exists to catch. (Flagged at the 2026-05-15 inaugural review; wrongly reversed 2026-05-30 — "string still present" conflated with "trigger valid"; inherited until the 2026-06-30 cross-verification caught it.) Re-keyed: **revisit multi-language only if a v2 of the cards deck (`cards.v2.*`) exists in any language.** Remains [GUARDED].

## §13. Refresh discipline

This file is **maintained by hand**, not generated. Update it when:

- A locked rule is added under any §-number
- A canonical path changes
- A doctrine version locks (note the version)
- A blocker is resolved or added
- A new tool/relay is automated (kill the old manual entry)

**Rule-kill review:** every locked rule that hasn't fired in 30 days → archive. Doctrine that doesn't breathe accumulates cost without producing safety. ~15 min/month.

**Gated-work discipline (v0.35):** parked/gated work carries a NAMED evidence trigger. "Parked" with no defined release condition is drift — at rule-kill review, either name the trigger or kill the item.

**Amendment discipline (L17, codified in-document, v0.42):** amendments **supersede, never edit** — historical clause text and prior footer version entries are preserved verbatim; a change is a new dated amendment block (or footer entry) that supersedes the old, and §-numbers are preserved under substance rewrite. This is the rule the in-document "per L17" citations refer to; its origin is the journal L17 lessons entry. Sibling standing lessons this constitution cites: **L48** — no merge before an explicit cross-model audit-cleared signal (§10); **L53** — describing labeled-DOB events in prose can re-fire the PII scan, so keep operator/role tokens away from ISO dates in files not on the scan's allow-list.

Cadence is **monthly** (amended v0.32, 2026-05-30 — was weekly). Rationale: weekly review at a solo cadence produced zero pruning (every review 2026-05-08 through 2026-05-30 was all-KEEP calibration — rules younger than the 30-day archive threshold have no firing surface to evaluate), and the weekly cadence silently lapsed for two consecutive Fridays (2026-05-22 + 2026-05-29) — the prune-discipline became the rule that stopped firing. Monthly aligns the review interval to the 30-day threshold it enforces, so each review actually has rules old enough to bite. Co-locate the monthly firing with other monthly discipline beats where possible (e.g. the v0.3.1 gate-close read 2026-06-15) so it has a recurring trigger and does not lapse.

First rule-kill review fired the first Friday after the doctrine aged 7 days (2026-05-15, inaugural). The catch-up review covering the two lapsed weekly slots fired 2026-05-30 (`~/Desktop/8ball/sessions/friday_rule_kill_review_2026-05-30.md`). Next review: 2026-06-15 (first monthly).

**v0.43 amendment (A1 — self-firing review trigger; the date cadence failed twice).** The monthly date cadence (v0.32, preserved verbatim above as lineage) is superseded as the *primary* trigger. It lapsed twice: first the weekly slots that prompted weekly→monthly, then the monthly review itself — due mid-June, it did not self-fire and ran 15 days late, firing only because a handoff carried it. A calendar a solo operator never watches cannot self-fire; swapping one date for another does not change the failure class. Replace it with an **event-based trigger backed by a date floor**, both first-class:
- **Accelerator (event).** Every doctrine-touching ship runs the rule-kill review in its §8 close-out (new ritual step, §8 v0.43) **if the last logged review is more than 30 days old** — binding the review to the journal-append ritual that already fires on every ship.
- **Floor (date).** Absent any doctrine-touching ship, the review still fires monthly as a backstop (the v0.32 cadence, retained as the floor).
- **Mechanical read, not memory.** "Last review date" is the date of the newest `## YYYY-MM-DD — … rule-kill review …` entry in `journal.md`; the >30-day check reads that date. This review logged 2026-06-30, so the next fires on the first doctrine-touching ship after 2026-07-30, or 2026-07-30 itself as the floor.

If you find yourself adding more locked rules than you're killing at review time, that's the recursion firing through the orchestrator. Stop drafting and ship something.

## §14. Operator calibration

Carried from an 8ball close-out session (2026-07-05), refined across a short review pass into fail-closed mechanisms rather than judgment calls. This section governs how the orchestrator (and any Claude lane) reads the operator and calibrates itself; it sits alongside §10's agent-role/lane architecture rather than replacing it.

**Objective gate (before the first file of any new cycle).**
1. Declare the objective in writing: money, mastery, or practice. One answer — it kills the others.
2. The slowest feedback loop that matters must resolve in <24h. If it doesn't, change the channel before writing code.
3. Name what gets deleted when this works. No subtraction plan = collection, not product.

**Reading the operator.**
- Reconstruct, don't clarify — but only for compression-trust: terseness that reads as deliberate shorthand on a reversible, low-blast-radius ask with an established acceptance criterion. Terseness that instead reads as unresolved ambiguity is not compression, whatever its length, and still gets a stated assumption or a clarifying question. Either way, an override-risk flag below still fires first.
- Override-risk flags, checked against the message, the current transcript, and canonical doctrine — not inferred tone or assumed fatigue, but not claimed as mechanically observable in isolation either. (a) and (b) require reading against standing law and thread history, not a keyword match; a close call fires the flag rather than waiting for certainty: (a) two asks in one message that contradict each other or a standing law; (b) scope doubling mid-thread without a new objective stated; (c) a directive that reverses a decision made <2h earlier in the same session; (d) a client-surfaced timestamp past midnight local on an irreversible-adjacent ask. Any flag fires → restate the interpretation and offer to defer to the morning read before executing. Supersedes an earlier draft of this rule keyed to "typos rising," which a chat transcript never actually exposes.
- Operational definitions, since these decide when (d) fires: "local" is the operator's own local clock, not server/UTC time; "irreversible-adjacent" means the ask sits within one step of something on the circuit-break list below (posts, merges, payments, deploys), not any midnight decision generally; "the morning read" is the operator's own re-read of the same thread after normal waking hours, not a fixed clock time.
- Irreversible-adjacent decisions flagged under (d) auto-defer to the morning read. Non-negotiable.
- Morning reads outrank midnight directives when the two conflict. Say so plainly — directness lands here, deference doesn't.
- Friction is designed, not narrated: the right action stays one tap; the wrong action requires a written sentence of justification. Naming a failure pattern is not the intervention.
- Taste calls (register, restraint, subtraction) get fast deference, even against conventional advice.

**Self-calibration (Claude, every lane).**
- Named failure mode: executing to avoid questioning — momentum reads as progress while direction drifts. The on-record instance is #69/#70 (2026-07-04): the same merge-before-audit-cleared-signal gap (L48) fired twice in one session, which is what made it a pattern rather than a slip.
- Weekly strategic check — "are we pointed at the right thing" — is exempt from terse-mode and is never requested; it fires on its own trigger. Mechanism, not memory: the newest `journal.md` entry carries a `next_strategic_read: YYYY-MM-DD` line; every session bootstrap reads it, and if it's overdue or missing the check runs before any task work, then the date rolls forward 7 days. Mirrors the §13 v0.43 self-firing rule-kill trigger exactly — a cadence a solo operator never watches cannot self-fire, so the trigger lives in a file that already gets read, not in memory.
- On unanimous-counsel overrides (all lanes agree, operator overrides anyway): the real objective must be stated out loud before executing. The override may be right, or may be revealing an unstated goal — lanes lack the operator's private context either way, so don't assume the override was correct going in. State the real objective, then judge the override against it.
- Own outcomes, not tasks.

**Standing laws.**
- Circuit-break on irreversibles: posts, merges, payments, deploys stay operator hands. Restates §10's controller-authority list; "posts" is the one item that list doesn't already name, since §10 predates the social-reach work.
- Dissent-then-BLOCK below a named evidence threshold (not dissent-then-execute). The threshold is a number or named condition pinned in writing for the specific decision at hand, never inferred per-session. If none is pinned, the absence is the answer: no threshold defined = below threshold, fail-closed rather than fail-open — same instinct as §12's gated-work discipline and the §13 amendment discipline, where an undefined gate is drift, not permission. Worked example already on file: the reach/build-B gate at `journal.md` 2026-06 ("build-B parked behind a NAMED evidence gate `[reach ≥ ~2–3k qualified] OR [≥1 strong divination-niche inflection signal]`") — not "N≥400," which is the *sub-threshold*, unmeasurable case logged the same week, not a gate value. Each gated decision pins its own number; none generalizes to a universal bar. This threshold rule governs empirical claims, gated-work decisions, and taste calls — it does not extend to safety, privacy, or violations of this doctrine's other standing rules. Those aren't waived by declaring an objective or invoking taste, no matter how firmly stated.
- Prototype before batch. Dry-run before install.
- ONE canonical file per domain, repo-resident, committed — no desktop vault, no split-truth layer. `DOCTRINE.md` is canon for product/process law, `journal.md` is canon for current state; this section is that file for operator calibration and is not re-pasted into chats as a parallel copy. Ephemeral review artifacts — audit briefs, relay run output, one-off critiques — can live outside the repo until their findings are reduced into canon; that's scratch space, not a second truth layer.
- Park = delete with a log line. WIP cap: 3.
- Audit intensity scales with blast radius. User-visible falsehoods outrank all polish.
- Refinement requires a named signal + hypothesis. No signal, no pass.
- One subtractive pass per cycle, scored by what it deletes.
- English only on public surfaces (western audience).
- No metric enters canon unless pinned from a dashboard. Estimates never get formatted as data.
- Weekly analytics read: file-backed like the strategic-read trigger above — a `next_analytics_read: YYYY-MM-DD` line in `journal.md`, checked at session bootstrap; overdue or missing = read runs now, then the date rolls forward 7 days. A calendar cadence nobody's watching doesn't self-fire — this section already made that argument once above; it applies here too.

**Build physics.**
- Generator-first: everything derivable derives from one data source; edit one line, regenerate all.
- Golden-master the full output space when it's deterministic — CI backbone, not a someday.
- Separate change-classes architecturally (copy / presentation / entitlement / engine) so cheap changes are audit-exempt by construction.
- When a constraint creates friction, reach for the primitive (worktree, script, file-drop) — never a new rule. Rules cost compliance forever; primitives don't.
- Agents communicate by file-drop from day one. Operator relays only where auth requires it.
- Post-merge close-out is a script, not a ritual.
- The distribution loop is one tap before any build automation exists.
- Automate nothing before three manual cycles of the same friction.

**Baseline.** This partnership's fluency starts at July-speed, not May-speed. Hold it there.

---

**CURRENT DOCTRINE VERSION — ADDITIVE BLOCK (L17). The current version is v0.85.** Entry labels in this block are **permanent version stamps** and never move again: status is carried by this header sentence, so a new release adds an entry and edits one word here rather than demoting three lines. That is a one-time normalisation of the two labels this block itself introduced at `7bb5630` and `7a23cf1` (`current:` / `current, prior:` → `§5 v0.8N (date):`); every entry BODY is byte-identical, and it is disclosed here rather than left to be discovered. The three rolling labels below — `doctrine version:` / `doctrine version, prior:` / `superseded:` — are **restored byte-for-byte to their `580a1a3` lines** and are **NOT** the current version. v0.83 demoted them; the final audit ruled that a breach of L17, which protects lines and not only bodies. Demotion is retired as the mechanism: current status is carried here, additively, and the rolling block is frozen. The demotion had precedent (`d424253`, `446a187`, `580a1a3` all rotated the same three labels) and was disclosed in Addendum 16 — that precedent now conflicts with this block, and reconciling it (codify a carve-out under §13, or stop rotating) is an open question left to the next audit rather than settled here.

**§5 v0.85 (2026-08-07):** 2026-08-07 · v0.85 (§5 — **`index.html` pinned WHOLE; H1–H6 made permanent tests; the residual moves one level out and is NOT closed.** Absorbs the re-audit of `7a23cf1` (P0 0 / P1 1 / P2 2 / P3 1). **P1** — H1–H6 were verified false-greens living only in prose. `index.html` is now pinned whole over raw bytes (**69,373 B / `71e97b80e68b6bc8c7d39649963ba795a438a384dc0f0e32e56951eeeedb1bd0`**), `cleanHtml()` compares that whole file, the two region pins are retained as DIAGNOSTICS, and all six ship as permanent fixtures asserting BOTH that the diagnostic regions stay byte-identical and that the whole-file pin moves. **H6 provenance, established before editing:** `index.html` **line 1058**, `'./core/profile.js'` → `'./vendor-core.js'`, **−1 byte**, plus a root shim that deletes the field from the live UNFROZEN object before `saveProfile`. Now 40 (+1 pin, +6 fixtures, +3 secondary). No runtime edit, parser, package, browser harness or lexer expansion. **Product runtime byte-unchanged** vs `446a187`. **§5 v0.84 (2026-08-07):** 2026-08-07 · v0.84 (§5 — **a declaration slice pins a SUBSTRING, not the live export; and the seam guard is NOT sufficient.** Absorbs the final audit of `7bb5630`: P0 0 / P1 3 / P2 2 / P3 1, every finding reproduced before editing. **P1** — `export function f(){}` is a MUTABLE binding and module exports are LIVE, so appending `getGenderInput = readControl` left the pinned declaration byte-identical at 143 B / `825a4df3…` while every importer received a different accessor; with Unicode escapes the raw allowlist stayed green too, and the full suite ran **57 files / 2039 tests GREEN** while the same bytes returned `undefined` under an `https:` module URL and `female` under `file:`. **§5 v0.83 (2026-08-07):** 2026-08-07 · v0.83 (§5 — **the lexical surface is retired too; the absolute claim moves onto EXACT BYTES.** Three survivors of v0.82's free-identifier policy, all reproduced before editing, all with the full 57/2029 suite GREEN. **(1)** An allowed root is a free doorway: `if (e.target && e.target.ownerDocument) delete opts[opts.constructor.keys(opts)[1]];` — `e` was already on the surface, member paths are excluded from the scan by construction, and `Object` came in as `opts.constructor`. **superseded:** 2026-08-06 · v0.74 (§5 — **the absolute claim moves off the hand lexer and onto a raw, fail-closed allowlist.** A final-byte audit produced the settling repro: `const spread = [.../[//]/.exec(cell.name)]; cardName.textContent = profile.gender ? "f" : cell.name;` — valid JavaScript, executed, changing the card, and **both lexical guards reported clean**, because a regex may legally begin after a spread `...` and the heuristic does not list it, so the `//` inside the character class opened a comment over a live read. `for await` and division-RHS variants reproduce independently. **§5 v0.73's "every spelled read fails at any nesting depth" is superseded**: enumerating every ES position a regex may begin in is not something a hand lexer does correctly, and v0.72 and v0.73 each closed the newest hole and re-asserted the same absolute claim. The PRIMARY invariant is now a raw allowlist — every line of `index.html` and of every `core/`/`ui/` module outside the three input-path files that contains the identifier, in any casing, pinned verbatim. It parses nothing, so no syntax confusion can hide a read; comments and user-visible copy are pinned too, deliberately, because stale gender copy is a defect this cycle already corrected twice. The lexical guards are retained as SECONDARY diagnostics with narrowed claims, and a counter-case pins one of their blind spots so the split is enforced by behaviour, not prose. Also fixed: the inline-module matcher missed `type=module` / `type = "module"` / `TYPE="module"`, and its test asserted on a token the baseline already carried (a unique sentinel replaces it). **Unchanged:** the runtime differential over the pure surfaces is the real guarantee, and no static check closes the indirection class — a runtime-built key, a Unicode escape or a value-scanning sniff writes no identifier and is invisible to raw and lexical scanning alike. Suite 56 files / 2003 tests green; product audit PASS 14/0/0/0 on a clean tree; local PII scan clean, 862 files; `index.html` 1474/1500. **STAGED — cross-model audit before merge per §10/L48; merge is the operator's word.**)

**superseded:** 2026-08-06 · v0.73 (§5 — **the lexical guard was still false-greening, and v0.72 miscounted its own scope.** An independent audit of the v0.72 commit found four bounded defects, three of them in the guard v0.72 announced as the repair — so **v0.72's "every SPELLED read fails the suite, wherever it sits" was false as written** and is superseded here. **(1)** The lexer swallowed live code three ways, all on ordinary JavaScript: a `}` inside a regex counted as structure (`replace(/\}/g, '')` truncated the extracted block); a regex after the `)` of `if (...)` read as division, so a `//` inside its character class opened a line comment and blanked the rest of the line (the `/*` form blanked five); and the regex-start keyword test was UNANCHORED, so the `in` inside `plain` made `plain / 2 /* } */` lex as a regex and cut a 94-character body to 43. Fixed with a distinct regex kind that cannot move a brace counter, control-head paren tracking, and an anchored keyword test on a widened token window; seven counter-cases plus a division case pin both directions. **(2)** The corpus guarantee was broader than the scan: `core/`+`ui/` read only top-level files and the host guard matched only the FIRST inline module block. Both are flat and single today and nothing forbade either. The sweep now recurses, the host guard concatenates every block, the scan asserts it reached its own allow-list, and the single-module count is pinned as a §6 invariant the guard no longer depends on. **(3)** v0.72 says "defeated three ways" in three places while enumerating four categories across five bypasses; the correct count is four and five. **(4)** "They share one function, so they cannot drift" is narrowed — they share the classifier and the matcher constant, not one function — and the Unicode-escape bypass the limits prose named but never asserted now has its counter-case. **The guarantee is unchanged in kind and still bounded:** every spelled read fails at any nesting depth; a runtime-built key, a Unicode escape and a value-scanning sniff still pass, and no lexical check closes that class. The runtime differential over the pure surfaces is the real guarantee. Suite 56 files / 2001 tests green; product audit PASS 14/0/0/0 on a clean tree; local PII scan clean, 862 files; `index.html` 1474/1500. **STAGED — cross-model audit before merge per §10/L48; merge is the operator's word.**)

**superseded:** 2026-08-06 · v0.72 (§5 — **the static gender guard was defeated three ways; its guarantee is now stated exactly.** v0.71 item (4) claimed the guard rejected the identifier "in any form". It did not. A re-audit and an adversarial red-team got past it by stripping literals before matching (so `profile["gender"]` became `profile[""]`), by brace-matching before stripping comments (a `// }}}}` comment cut renderCard's body from 3348 characters to 4; quoting the signature in a comment hijacked the extraction), by scope (a sibling helper in the same inline module was unscanned, and the `core/`+`ui/` scan still used a property-only regex, so a destructured read in `ui/result.js` passed with the suite green), and by word boundary (`\bgender\b` did not see `getGenderInput()`, so renderCard could call the accessor, read the live control and change the card while naming no property — ordinary code, not obfuscation). **Repaired lexically** in `tests/helpers/js-lex.js`: one pass classifies every character CODE/COMMENT/STRING with template interpolations as code; braces and the signature are found only in code; the scan drops comments and KEEPS string text, because a computed key lives in a literal. **superseded:** 2026-08-06 · v0.70 (§5 — **the measurement contract: four events, a payload that cannot carry a person, and no collector.** `core/measurement.js` names the only four things the product may ever count — `reading_completed`, `paid_t3_cta_clicked`, `comparative_opened`, `share_completed` — fixes the payload at `{event, tier}` over the §1.D vocabulary, and ships with a **null sink**. No analytics, no tracker, no collector, no storage key, no network call; §7 gate 7 is unchanged and unqualified, and `tests/privacy_scan.test.js` scans `core/` so it scans this module. **The shape is built, not filtered:** the record is an object literal of exactly two checked values and `recordMeasurement` takes two positional arguments, so there is no object for a call site to over-populate and no name, DOB, gender, city, coordinate or card string can reach it. Absent on purpose: no id, session token or counter (nothing that joins two records into a sequence), no timestamp (a timestamp IS a join key), no URL/UA/viewport/locale. Two records of the same event on one device are byte-identical, and a test asserts it. Retired `t4`/`t5` tokens build no record rather than emit a rung that no longer exists. **Four call sites, each at the moment the thing happened:** `renderCard` after a completed render; the CTA resolved from inside the modal so the host gains no line and the Buy Link stays the bare href §5.B requires; `ui/dyad.js` after the entitlement gate AND after `open()` actually reveals the screen (a pre-merge lane found the record firing before both, so a throwing host hook or the second gate could count a screen that never opened); `ui/share.js` on both delivery paths, inside the native `try` after the await so a dismissed sheet is not a share. `ui/share.js` takes it through a **hook, not an import** — that module's §5.D posture is to import nothing and know no tier constant, and the posture wins; a test pins that the host wires it. **Coverage is stated precisely rather than flatteringly:** three sites are driven against a recording sink through the real module functions (`tests/dyad_surface.test.js`, `tests/share_behavior.test.js`, `tests/measurement.test.js`), and `reading_completed` is a source-shape pin because `renderCard` lives in index.html's inline module that no harness executes — its live cover is the browser pass. An earlier draft of this clause claimed all four were driven when three were string matches; that is corrected here. The operator plan at `audits/measurement_plan_2026-08-06.md` carries the three rate definitions and states what the payload makes uncomputable (no funnel, no per-person rate, no cohort, no time series). **The recommendation is to hold at contract-only** until reach moves; the contract earns its place now because it is what stops an ad-hoc snippet being pasted in later under deadline. This SUPERSEDES the v0.54 nine-number amendment and is a controller product/commercial decision — *"master numbers sell so keep them"* — forced by a truth defect rather than a preference: the locked X-offer specimen shows `11` and `22` while the shipped calculator reduced them, and the 2026-07-31 Codex preflight returned STOP on exactly that mismatch (`audits/codex_x_offer_preflight_2026-07-31_response.md`). The repair the controller refused is deleting the masters from the creative; the product is made truthful instead. What v0.54 got right survives untouched: an absent contributing letter class is still `null` / `—`, never a displayed `0`, and the versioning discipline that kept every content file immutable through the narrowing is exactly why widening costs four new active files and no edit to a shipped one. Masters resolve to the `high` bracket and the THIRD facet position — the pre-calc-v3 contract, restored; there is no fourth bracket, because a catalog cell has three note positions. The facet key versions `_v2` → **`_v3`** and both retired generations are cleared once on the first v4 read, because a position stored under calc v3 was computed against a life path that has since moved (`1970-01-04`: `4 → 22`, `mid → high`) — no other key is added. Active content becomes `meanings.v3` (twelve entries; the three master bodies reused BYTE-FOR-BYTE from immutable v1 and pinned by identity, plus one added `theme` field each), `concordance.v3` (twelve-value domain; exactly the three v1 master-reduction links registered, the other 63 pairs unfiled, none `adjacent`), `dyad.v2` (v1 tables unedited; two combined-path frames and one provenance line reworded, and the frame rule moves from `sum > 9` to `sum !== combined` so a sum that STOPS at 11 no longer announces that 11 reduces to 11), and `public.v3` (v1/v2 tables unedited; a master birthday keeps its value and reaches the nine-entry mode table through the declared `MASTER_MODE_BRIDGE`, DISCLOSED as `mode.modeKey` / `mode.bridged` / `mode.bridgeNote` AND rendered as a fourth line on the host block and both dyad sheets — no master work mode is authored, and CI pins the table at nine entries so none can be). **Corrected pre-merge on an independent audit (2026-07-31):** the first implementation disclosed the bridge in the returned object and discarded it in the sole production formatter, so the rendered surface performed the exact silent substitution this amendment names as the wrong fix; every existing surface test used a non-master birthday and could not catch it. Two P2s closed with it — active state statements still naming the retired `_v2` facet key, and a `meanings.v3` that restated v2's nine themes instead of carrying them structurally. `index.html` is byte-identical at 1497 lines; no new dependency, no network call, no new persisted key beyond the versioned facet name, no tier/entitlement change, no deck edit, and the tarot birth-card reduction (§1.C, `22 → 0 · the fool`) is a separate contract this does not touch. Suite 51 files / **1826** tests green (1814 at the first-audited HEAD `d03a768`; the remediation above added ten — the master-birthday disclosure at both surfaces, and the cross-version registry pin; the final-worktree P2 absorb added two production-seam regression tests); auditor assurance 93 green; product audit PASS 13/0/1/0. **STAGED — a fresh independent Codex audit is mandatory before merge consideration per §10/L48; the earlier X-offer preflight is NOT implementation clearance. No push, PR, merge or deploy authority is claimed by this entry.**)

**superseded:** 2026-07-30 · v0.61 (§1.D + new §1.J — the DYAD rung. A fourth ladder rung `t5`, param `?paid=t5`, price $6, appended so every existing rung keeps its rank and no t1/t2/t3 owner is affected; the R2 legacy grandfather deliberately stays at t3. **The ladder skips `t4` and the skip is load-bearing**: `t4` is retired and lives in `RETIRED_TIERS`, so a dyad shipped under that token would sit in both tables and `normalizeTier` would rewrite every paying dyad device down to t3 before `isTier` saw it. `RETIREMENT_COLLISIONS` derives that invariant from both tables at module load and is pinned empty, and rank is ladder POSITION rather than the digit in the token. t5 buys a SECOND complete specimen sheet — every coordinate, its own written 144-card entry, its own public read — plus a relation layer of three axes computed by `core/dyad.js` from the two already-calculated profiles: the directed five-element relation between the two day masters (one authored passage per direction), the combined life path (a neutral reduction clause beside the existing nine-number registry's own body, carried byte-for-byte), and the card pair (year branches through §1.I's five named families × the ordered life-path bracket pair). Like `cardEntry` and `publicRead` it is a BLOCK, so the §1.F census is byte-identical at t3 and t5. Entitlement is all-or-nothing: below t5 the entry control is absent, the screen refuses to open, a submission is refused and the render produces nothing. Commercially NOT live and fail-closed — the Gumroad product does not exist, `T5_PRODUCT_URL` ships empty, and the §4.B v0.56 single-$3 sprint surface is byte-untouched with exactly one Gumroad URL still in `index.html`. §1.J binds the relation layer to §1.I's register law verbatim and adds a no-person-as-subject rule, both mechanically scanned over the tables AND the assembled runtime output with positive-fire sentinels. **First implementation was REFUSED** by a Codex pre-merge audit with six P1 findings (ungated entry and second sheet; person B's data left in hidden DOM on close; a second-person form with none of the primary form's validation; a recomputed day master; a narrowed two-column output instead of two standalone readings; re-authored number meanings) — all closed, and the corrections are recorded in the clauses themselves rather than only in the journal. Two limits on the record: the dyad sheets are a second DOM writer over one shared value mapping rather than a second instance of `ui/tiers.js`, and are therefore not tappable; and **no cross-model audit has cleared the corrected branch** — per L48 a fresh independent read is required before merge. Suite 51 files / 1758 tests green. STAGED — merge is its own word. **SHIPPED — squash-merged to `main` as `2cdaa3c` (#187) on 2026-07-30; the STAGED language and this entry's “no cross-model audit has cleared the corrected branch” sentence are superseded by that merge and its audit chain, corrected on sighting per the v0.48–v0.56 footer precedent (mechanical edit, see `audits/mechanical_footer_correction_v061_2026-07-30.md`). L48 was met, not overridden: Codex DO NOT MERGE at `9c749ef` (six P1, two P2) → correction commits `98792b1` + `e34458a` → independent Codex re-audit SAFE TO MERGE (`audits/codex_pr187_premerge_reaudit_2026-07-30_response.md`), then merge.**)

**superseded:** 2026-07-29 · v0.60 (§1.D — the public rung is RETIRED and its read folds into t3. `TIER_ORDER` returns to three rungs; `?paid=t4` is no longer a tier; the public read becomes a t3 ceiling block beside the written entry, still a BLOCK so the §1.F census is unmoved at 15 of 15. Controller ruling on three grounds: the tier was named for an exposure radius it never delivered (the block renders privately and is absent from the §5.D share surface); the price could not be defended ($9 for five derived strings computing no new coordinate, above a $3 rung selling fifteen coordinates plus the written 144-card entry); and there are no sales to build on (the revenue-reset diagnosis on record is zero outside orders). **superseded:** 2026-07-29 · v0.57 (§3 calc v3.1 — era-correct civil offset for lunar-year and solar-term cusps. calc v2 evaluated the whole 1900–2100 table at a flat UTC+8; China kept Beijing local mean time, UTC+7:45:40, until China Standard Time was adopted in 1929, so a new moon or solar term falling in the first ~14 minutes after midnight was filed one day late. Four dates move, all pre-1929: lunar new year 1916 Feb 4 → Feb 3, and solar terms 1911 lixia, 1912 xiaohan, 1927 bailu each one day earlier. The visible defect was a 1916-02-03 birth filed under the previous year's animal — rabbit instead of dragon. `core/calendar.js` is the only source file changed; numerology, sun sign, rising sign, birth card, pillars ~~and the catalog index are untouched~~ **are untouched — the catalog index is NOT among them, erratum below**, no stored reading needs migration, and no dependency, localStorage key, network call or scanner list changes. `tests/fixtures.json` gains two pre-1929 boundary cases per §3; `tests/calendar.test.js` pins 1916 → Feb 3 and drops it from the ICU-divergence list, which now holds only the three years where ICU is the one in error. Verified against three independent implementations — sxtwl, lunardate, borax — unanimous with each other on 1900–2050 and with this change on 1900–2100. NOTE: the Hong Kong Observatory tables named under calc v2 could not be reached from the implementing environment; egress policy blocked `hko.gov.hk`, so the three-library consensus substitutes and wants HKO re-confirmation. Suite 43 files / 1500 tests green. STAGED on `claude/calendar-pre1929-lmt-fix`; per §10/L48 a cross-model audit is REQUIRED before merge — this is a calc change and a DOCTRINE change, the case the don't-do list names explicitly, and an override is not an appropriate substitute here. **SHIPPED — squash-merged to `main` as `8b51c38` (#140) on 2026-07-29; the STAGED language above is superseded by that merge, corrected on sighting per the v0.48/v0.50/v0.51/v0.55 footer precedent (mechanical edit, see `audits/mechanical_footer_correction_2026-07-29.md`). Corrected, NOT vindicated: the requirement stated above was not satisfied. No cross-model audit was run, and the gate was cleared by explicit controller override — `audits/L48_override_pr140_2026-07-29.md`, L48 sighting #12, which opens by calling itself the weakest use of that instrument in the chain and records that one author wrote the change, its evidence and its clearance. A retroactive cross-model read has since been commissioned (brief at `audits/codex_pr140_retroactive_audit_2026-07-29_brief.md`, #145, merged `a9957b5`); its verdict is outstanding, and it is deliberately named so that it cannot retroactively satisfy any gate. The HKO re-confirmation the NOTE above asks for also remains open. **Erratum, 2026-07-29 P1-D: the "catalog index... untouched" claim above was false. The catalog is `(sunSign, animal)`-driven (§1) using the public year-pillar animal — exactly the coordinate this entry moved for 1916-02-03 (rabbit → dragon). Its catalog index therefore moved too: Aquarius+rabbit `cxxiv` → Aquarius+dragon `cxxv`, confirmed against shipped `core/engine.js`. Numerology, sun sign, rising sign, birth card, and pillars genuinely are untouched. Blast-radius/truth defect in the record; the shipped catalog computation itself was already correct. Also: the HKO re-confirmation this entry left open has now happened (2026-07-29, P1-D) — see the NOTE above in this same paragraph and journal.md. ~~It found eight further boundary mismatches against the authority this entry could not reach, outside the four dates this entry corrected; `core/calendar.js` now carries a sourced correction table for exactly those eight, with zero mismatches remaining against the full HKO 1901–2100 index.~~ **— erratum, 2026-07-30 (Codex pre-merge audit, finding F), struck per L17 and superseded by the fuller erratum under §3 calc v3.1: "outside the four dates this entry corrected" is FALSE — 1911 lixia and 1912 xiaohan are two OF those four, and the correction table restores for them the very HKO values this entry moved away from, so calc v3.1's pre-1929 offset was a regression at those two boundaries. Six of the eight are genuinely new. "The full HKO 1901–2100 index" also overstates the scope: the comparison covers the 12 month-starting terms plus the lunar-new-year date per year (2,400 solar + 200 lunar, zero mismatches), not HKO's full 24-term index — the 12 major terms carry no direct coverage. The eight override values themselves are unaffected and remain verified against the 200 official HKO source files.**)
